# Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya

*"The Rising of the Moon of Chaitanya"*

*A drama in ten acts by Kavi Karnapura (Paramananda Sena)*

*Translated from the Sanskrit and Prakrit into English*

---

## Translator's Preface

The *Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya* (*Śrī-caitanya-candrodaya-nāṭaka*) is a Sanskrit drama by Kavi Karnapura, the youngest
son of Shivananda Sena, written, according to tradition, in 1572 (Shaka 1494) at the request of Prataparudra, the
king of Orissa (in the prologue the stage-director speaks of his command to stage the play before Jagannatha). It is
one of the earliest lives of Sri Chaitanya: the author saw Mahaprabhu with his own eyes as a child, and Krishnadasa
Kaviraja in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* relies on it directly and quotes it. The drama covers the whole lila of
Mahaprabhu — from His descent and His pastimes in Navadvipa to His sannyasa, His life in Puri, His journeys to the
south and to Vrindavana, and the yearly chariot festivals; the events are interwoven with scenes of allegorical
characters (Maitri, Prema-bhakti, Viraga, Kali, Adharma) and with a play within the play (Act 3, the "Dana-keli",
performed by the Lord's associates in Navadvipa).

**Source.** The electronic text of the Gaudiya Grantha Mandira, compiled from the editions of M. Guha (Navadvipa,
2nd ed., 1971) and R. Mishra (Varanasi, 1966). Doubtful passages have been checked against the *Kāvyamālā*
edition No. 87 (Bombay, Nirnaya-sagar, 1917); gaps in the electronic text have been filled from it (in square
brackets or with a note), and important variant readings are indicated in the notes. The anonymous notes of
Guha's edition and the editors' notes have not been translated.

**Principles.** The prose, the verses and the stage directions are translated; the Prakrit speeches (spoken by
women, servants and the vidushaka) are translated from the Prakrit with the help of the Sanskrit *chāyā*; where a
character who normally speaks Prakrit changes to Sanskrit, this is marked, as in the original. The verses are given
in prose and numbered within each act. The numbering of the electronic text is in places confused (missing or
repeated numbers, unnumbered quotations); in such cases the numbers have been aligned, where possible following the
*Kāvyamālā*, and this is noted. The translator's notes (realia, plays on words, sources of quotations, variant
readings) are given at the end of each act. Modern English translations of the drama have not been used.

**The English version** was made from the Sanskrit and Prakrit of the same source, in parallel with the Russian
translation of the project: the numbering of the verses and the notes coincide in both languages. Verses of the
drama quoted in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* have been compared in sense with our translation of the CC. Following
Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, the pastimes of Radha and Krishna and of Sri Chaitanya described here are
transcendental (*aprākṛta*); the love of the gopis is prema, not worldly passion, and the Deities are the Lord
Himself, not images.

---

## Contents

- Act 1. Absorption in His Own Bliss (Svānandāveśa)
- Act 2. The Vision of All the Avataras (Sarvāvatāra-darśana)
- Act 3. The Sport of the Toll (Dāna-vinoda)
- Act 4. The Acceptance of Sannyasa (Sannyāsa-parigrahaḥ)
- Act 5. Pastimes in the Town of Advaita (Śrīmad-advaita-pura-vilāsa)
- Act 6. Mercy to Sarvabhauma (Sārvabhaumānugraha)
- Act 7. Pilgrimage (Tīrthāṭanaḥ)
- Act 8. Mercy to Prataparudra (Pratāparudrānugraha)[^08-1]
- Act 9. The Journey to Mathura (Śrī-mathurā-gamana)
- Act 10. The Great Festival (Mahā-mahotsava)

---

# Act 1. Absorption in His Own Bliss (Svānandāveśa)

*Prologue: at the command of King Prataparudra the stage-director produces a drama about Chaitanya; Kali and Adharma anxiously discuss the appearance of Vishvambhara and His associates; the great bathing ceremony (abhisheka) in the house of Shrivasa; the Lord's mercy to Shrivasa, Murari, Mukunda, Shuklambara and Shachi.*

> **1.** To the treasures — Kumuda, Padma, Shankha and the rest — He brings distaste with the fresh moonstones of bhakti;
> He has driven a thorn of grief into Kali, as into the chakravaka bird —
> may Gaurachandra destroy the darkness of the objects of the senses![^01-1]

*(At the end of the nandi, the benediction.)*

**Stage-director.** Enough of lengthy speech! Ho there, ho! Listen! Today, at the festival of the journey to Gundicha of the Lord, Sri Purushottama-deva — the fresh shoot of a great sapphire, gleaming like ground collyrium, sprung up on the shore of the ocean, the mine of jewels; the grove of tamala trees with dense foliage, seen again and again in the caves of the Blue Mountain; the exultant elephant who has appeared beside the great banyan with its deepest of hollows[^01-2] — at the festival when the hollow of the vessel of the universe resounds with cries of "Jaya! Jaya!" ringing from the eloquent mouths of the jubilant throngs of charming people come from all the quarters and intermediate quarters of the world; when the host of maidens of the quarters rejoices at the tumult raised by the delight of the Lord's kirtana, sung by devotees whose hearts exult with joy and who are undivided in rasa; when the crowds are endlessly deafened, unable to hear any other sound for the din of the pataha drums, eager to keep company with the thundering mardalas, of the precious panava drums, of the countless booming dhakkas, the deepest roar of the bheris and the thunder of the dundubhis — at such a moment I received a command from Gajapati Prataparudra. He drew near with love, by right of his service, and yet he was dejected: his heart was wounded by grief at the disappearance which the Lord, Sri Krishna Chaitanya, is spreading,[^01-3] and, turned away from joy by his sorrowful plight — for that chief reason — he paid no honour even to such supreme bliss. This protector of the earth, with undisguised longing, spoke thus: "O master of the bards!

> **2.** Here is the Lord of the Blue Mountain, here the same splendour, the same journey to Gundicha;
> the same virtuous people come from all quarters, the same thirst of theirs to see;
> the same gardens that eclipse the beauty of the Nandana grove —
> yet all of it, alas, without Mahaprabhu, we count as empty.

Therefore you must now gladden me with some auspicious performance, full of rasa, whose essence is the outpouring fragrance of the qualities of that very Lord, the king of renunciants, rich in the good fortune of divine love. For:

> **3.** There is no means of bearing the pain born of not seeing the beloved face to face
> except the portrayal of his qualities — told by friends or else enacted on the stage."

So here I must make every effort. And this is a wonder:

> **4.** The fire of his valour, though smokeless, is inferred in every quarter;
> it gives happiness to the virtuous and is eager to burn the swarm of moths, his enemies.
> Fearing lest the world should burst from the onrush of its heat,
> the Lord of Shri plastered the egg of the universe on the outside beforehand with many coverings.[^01-4]

He is like valour embodied and gathered into one, and he is also like tranquillity embodied: immersed in shanta-rasa, which arose of itself from the nature of his steadily growing devotion to the Lord, he has shaken off rajas and tamas, cast off ignorance, and does not let the thirst for sense objects take root even in the hearts of others. And so now, casting off doubt and making this body of mine fruitful, I shall fulfil the cherished good of this king by staging the drama named *Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya* ("The Rising of the Moon of Chaitanya"), which dispels the darkness of the heart's impurity. It was composed by the poet Paramananda Dasa, favoured by Srinatha, the son of Shivananda Sena, a dear associate of that same Lord, Sri Krishna Chaitanya, who protects [the world] with His mercy.[^01-5] *(Looking ahead.)* Ho there, ho! This way, this way!

*(Enter the assistant.)*

**Assistant.** Sir! A wonder, a wonder!

**Stage-director.** My friend, what is it?

**Assistant.** Even amid this supreme bliss of the chariot festival of the Lord — the moving fountain of joy on Nilachala — some people, bereft of happiness and utterly downcast, regard the universe as a pot broken before its time, and they weep. Like this:

> **5.** "Ah, here is the goal of the journey of the Ornament of Nilachala, here the rows of new gardens,
> here the very road of the victorious procession —
> yet all this burns the eyes like a cruel bilious fever, cuts the mind like the speech of the wicked,
> and torments the body like an ulcer in the heart."

Tell me, then, what is the secret here?

**Stage-director.** My friend, you are fortunate: today there have come before these eyes of yours those very great devotees who make sight fruitful, the saviours of the world.[^01-6]

**Assistant.** Sir, who are they?

**Stage-director.** The dear associates of Sri Krishna Chaitanya.

**Assistant.** Sir, and who is this Chaitanya Gosvami?

**Stage-director.** My friend, you must still be sitting hidden in the pot of your mother's womb, if you have not heard even the name of this Sri Mahaprabhu! Listen, then:

> **6.** Behold a wonder! Its root is the crest-jewel of renunciants, the lord of sages named Madhava;
> its shoot is Srila Advaita; its trunk, renowned in the three worlds, is the Avadhuta;
> the branches of the trunk are Srimad Vakreshvara and others whose bodies are made of rasa;
> its spreading growth is bhakti-yoga, and its flower and fruit are prema free of all deceit.[^01-7]

And further:

> **7.** Its summit shines, piercing even the bliss of Brahman, and there has built its nest
> a pair of birds woven of the pastimes of Radha and Krishna, devoid of all separateness;
> its shade relieves the weariness of the road of samsara; it is the cause of the fulfilment of the devotees' desires —
> such a wish-fulfilling tree, Chaitanya, has appeared here in the world.[^01-8]

**Assistant.** Sir, what is the purpose of this descent, which has come so near to people?

**Stage-director.** My friend, attend! Learned men possessed by the demon of attachment to their own doctrine think that the supreme goal of man is merely dissolution — the extinction of the mind in the attributeless, all-pervading supreme Brahman — and that the only means to it, the only wealth, is meditation on non-duality; and they hold this to be primary and established by all the shastras. The truth has remained beyond their grasp, though it abides as the highest in those very shastras, only in hidden form: that Bhagavan Sri Krishna alone, whose form is condensed being, consciousness and bliss, whose pastimes are eternal, who is full of every good fortune, is Brahman with attributes; that worship of Him, sung by Sanandana and others, faultless, is the complete goal of man; and that the means, the wealth for attaining it, is bhakti-yoga of many kinds, chief among which is the congregational chanting of the Name. To reveal this, Bhagavan Sri Krishna, becoming Chaitanya, has come into the world.

**Assistant.** Sir, and has this Hari composed here a book setting forth His own doctrine?[^01-9]

**Stage-director.** Who does not know, indeed, that the Lord is the author of the Vedas? Yet the impulse which the Inner Ruler wishes to give cannot be limited by outward instruction, nor by place, nor by time.

**Assistant.** Sir, why then does not everyone delight in this generous teaching?

**Stage-director.** My friend, people in the world are subject to various *vāsanās* (latent tendencies) — how could all of them set foot on the path that transcends the world? For faith bound by *vāsanās* gives rise to differences of understanding.

**Assistant.** Sir, bhakti-yoga, which is beyond the reach of the authors of the shastras,[^01-10] also produces nothing but knowledge, however full of wonder; its effective fruit is *kaivalya* (isolation) in Brahman. Where, then, is the difference?

**Stage-director.** My friend,

> **8.** "One who has taken such a vow, chanting the name of his Beloved, becomes full of attachment, and his heart melts;
> he laughs loudly, weeps, cries out, sings, and dances like one mad, indifferent to the ways of the world."[^01-11]

By these and other words [it is declared that] the state which gives rise to *rati* (attraction) — a property of bhakti-yoga, unshakeable as a mountain, in the form of the congregational chanting of the Lord's name and the rest — leads to the position of an associate [of the Lord].[^01-12] And likewise in the words "with their beautiful limbs…" and so on:

> **9.** "The virtuous, O mother, behold My beautiful, divine forms, bestowers of blessings,
> with gracious faces and reddish eyes, and converse with them in words they long for."[^01-13]

Thus their liberation lies in that very form [of an associate], not in *kaivalya*. That is why Kapila and others have said: "Bhakti is greater than *siddhi* (liberation)."[^01-14] Therefore in the age of Kali it is the congregational chanting of the Name — which surpasses the power of [all other] means of attaining the goals of man — that brings to the fore the bhava called *rati*.

**Assistant.** Sir! This heartfelt utterance is very astonishing: for the shastra says that the Lord's name produces only liberation. And if you say otherwise — in fact it is said: "Uttering 'Narayana!' at the time of death, he attained liberation."

**Stage-director** *(smiling)*. The word "liberation" here means the form of an associate, for in the same place it is said: "At once he assumed the form of those who abide beside the Lord."[^01-15] This very teaching of Sri Krishna Chaitanya sweeps away other doctrines. And it is followed by the virtuous and the wise. Therefore even this age of Kali has been blessed by His descent.

**Assistant.** How can that be? For we hear the censure:

> **10.** "In the age of Kali, O king, people whose minds are split by heresy will for the most part not worship
> the Lord Achyuta, the supreme teacher of the worlds, at whose lotus feet the lords of the three worlds bow."[^01-16]

**Stage-director.** That refers to former ages of Kali, before the descent of Sri Krishna. Otherwise such sayings as these, which point to the future descent of Chaitanya, would contradict one another:

> **11.** "Out of mercy for the devotees who will be born in the age of Kali, He spread His most holy fame,
> which dispels suffering, grief and darkness";[^01-17]

> **12.** "People of the Krita and other ages, O king, desire to be born in the age of Kali:
> for in the age of Kali devotees of Narayana will appear."[^01-18]

**Assistant.** And how is it that Kali does not harm them?

**Stage-director.**[^01-19]

> **13.** He who in the dark fortnight wanes unfailingly day after day —
> how can this *doṣā-kara* harm those who have taken refuge in the *viṣṇu-pada*?[^01-20]

**Behind the scenes.** Hey, who are you, that, reviling me as a *doṣā-kara*, you put me on a level with the moon?

**Stage-director** *(looking intently)*. My friend, as was to be expected, this [Kali] — his heart blazing with excess of wrath, merciless in his ascendancy — is approaching, together with his dear friend Adharma. Let us leave this place.

*(Exeunt both.)*

*End of the prologue.*

*(Then enters Kali, attended by Adharma.)*

**Kali.** Friend Adharma! The master of the bards spoke the truth.

**Adharma.** What exactly?

*(Kali again recites "He who in the dark fortnight…" and so on.)*

**Adharma.**[^01-21] My friend, king of the age! Why, that scoundrel has called you yourself a *doṣā-kara*! Ah, you sinner, you wretched actor! Listen, then!

> **14.** He who, together with his vassals — purity, good conduct, austerity, forbearance, tranquillity, self-control,
> and discrimination and the rest — tore King Dharma up by the roots;
> by whom even Dharma's favourites, who purify by a mere glance, were blinded in an instant —
> I, the sole doer of all this, am in his service: and this is the Kali they revile![^01-22]

Stand, sinner! Stand, stand!

> **15.** Where there is dharma, there is Krishna; where Krishna is, there is victory.
> Where there is no dharma, how can Krishna be with him on whose side is Kali himself?[^01-23]

**Kali.** My friend, do not revile him. Understand: for

> **16.** My time is past, my friend: now my power has been crushed by a boy —
> as the might of Takshaka, king of serpents, fades the moment a shoot of the great healing herb breaks through.

**Adharma.** King of the age! Who is this boy (*kumāraka*)? A vile killer (*ku-māraka*)? Or a destroyer of *ku*, the earth?

**Kali.** Neither (*nobhayam*) — he cannot inspire fear (*no bhayam*).[^01-24] But:

> **17.** In Navadvipa, of Purandara Mishra named Jagannatha,
> this boy was born to Shachi — and He cleaves my very heart.

**Adharma** *(laughing)*. Ho there, king of the age!

> **18.** For fear of the wrath of your mighty, club-like arms, blazing like the great sun,
> the Bull [of Dharma], with but a single leg left, has grown dumb and, like an owl, has hidden in a mountain cave.
> And you, whose feet are served by servants such as I, are afraid of a brahmin brat?
> Ah, what unheard-of confusion of mind is this of yours![^01-25]

**Kali.** Friend, He is not merely the child of a "god of the earth" (*bhū-deva-bāla*); He is the God of the god Bala (*bāla-deva-deva*).[^01-26] For

> **19.** Hari, Hari![^01-27] Hari, whose heart is full of sweet feeling, in order to teach the bhakti-yoga of Hari,
> purifying the whole world, with the lovely lustre of a golden lotus,
> has descended here, in a brahmin's house, and plays His childhood pastimes.

And so:

> **20.** He, being born at the full moon, on the pretext of an eclipse
> made all the people of the world chant the name of Hari at once.[^01-28]

**Adharma.** This too is your delusion: you are interpreting otherwise what happened by the maxim of "the crow and the palm fruit".[^01-29] Listen, listen then:

> **21.** Where are you — great in might, with the mightiest of allies, long and firmly rooted —
> and where is this sprout born in a family of brahmins?
> And you fear Him? Ah, what a delusion!

**Kali.** Friend, consider it as it really is:

> **22.** The self-luminous do not depend on time, place, age, lineage and the like:
> the young sun, as soon as it rises, dispels the dense darkness.

Nor is He without allies: for even before His own descent He caused hosts of His dear associates to appear on earth. Like this:

> **23.** Advaita, best of acharyas — manifestly the abode of Shambhu (Shiva), not inferior to Bhagavan Himself;[^01-30]
> Nityananda the Avadhuta — the glorious splendour of Sankarshana;
> and one named Shrivasa — victorious together with Shrikanta and Shripati, and with Srila Rama too —
> ornament of the brahmin race, the very power of Narada.[^01-31]

And further:

> **24.** Acharya-ratna, Haridasa, Murari, Gangadasa and others, together with Gadadhara Pandita and the rest,
> Vidyanidhi and those with him, Vasudeva Acharya and others, and Mukunda at the head of the others.

And further:

> **25.** With them Sri Vakreshvara, with Nrisimha, with Shuklambara,
> with them Sri Damodara and Shankara, and Jagadananda, one of the chief —
> a host of His beloved ones, connoisseurs of the dance and play of manifold feelings,
> His kinsmen from childhood, has descended to earth for the deliverance of the world.[^01-32]

**Adharma.** King of the age, tell me: how was it established that He is Ishvara, the Lord?

**Kali.** My friend, the power to attract the hearts of all people is the exclusive sign of Bhagavan, because He is made of bliss. One made of bliss is able to gladden living beings, just as a very rich man makes another rich as well. And He has this very sign of the Supreme: even as a child He astonished the hearts of all people. Like this:

> **26.** Shiva, Shiva! Even in infancy He had depth, steadfastness,
> memory, intelligence, extraordinary learning, sweetness, tenderness and other
> qualities that especially attract all people — who, seeing them,
> would not come here to the conviction: "This is Vishnu!"?[^01-33]

**Adharma.** That is no proof (*anaikāntika*):[^01-34] let Him be merely some excellent soul.

**Kali.** Not so.

> **27.** "Know that whatever is glorious, beautiful or mighty
> is born of a spark of My splendour."[^01-35]

So the Lord spoke in general [of each single excellence]; and since He has hosts of such qualities by the tens of thousands, His divinity is all the more proven. And we ourselves are the evidence of it: for we are not afraid of any living being.

**Adharma.** King of the age, they say He has married?

**Kali.** Indeed!

> **28.** Ah, when the Lord descends into the world, there descends with Him
> His own particular potency — Shri herself in bodily form.
> Accepting her, who imitated the play of human beings, and spending a few days with her,
> God caused her to disappear.[^01-36]

And so her human nature [is explained by the words]: "When He is a god, she is a goddess; when He is a man, she is a woman."[^01-37] Moreover:

> **29.** Taking as His wife another beloved, known as Vishnupriya, the embodiment of a portion of Bhu (the Earth),
> He, ever new, wishing to reveal the lesson of renunciation, will leave her too, His new bride.

And further:

> **30.** His elder brother, taking no wife — Bhagavan Sankarshana, who appeared on earth as Vishvarupa —
> having passed his splendour on to a lord of the Puris (Paramananda Puri), took up the wandering life first and disappeared.[^01-38]

**Adharma** *(reflecting a moment; aside)*. Ah, it looks like trouble! It was from an excess of fancy that I pictured everything otherwise. But

> **31.** The great Name wearies my heart, loosens limb after limb,
> throws into disorder all the obedient servants, the senses;
> oh, it takes away memory, ah, it shakes off steadfastness —
> there is no other calamity for me than the mention of the Great One.

**Kali.** My friend! What [I] have experienced, now experience, experience [you too]!

**Adharma.** My friend! There is a means, sure and without flaw, to overcome Him and to prosper ourselves.

**Kali.** Friend, what is it?

**Adharma.** My friend,

> **32.** These six ministers — Lust and the rest — there is nothing beyond their power:
> by the pride of their invincible mighty arms the whole world is under your single parasol.

Just now all six have returned from their campaign of conquering the quarters of the world. Each, having conquered one quarter and rid the world of every thorn, will now, casting off doubt, present himself at the foot of the feet of his master — you. All of them together we shall send to defeat Him. Let me describe their valour:

> **33.** From the pride of his arms Brahma, born of the lotus, himself approached his own daughter,
> and Pashupati (Shiva), though content in himself, ran after Mohini —
> this is Kama (Lust), whose loud fame in conquering the three worlds is like sport with women.
> And what of the others — wretched creatures![^01-39]

**Kali.** My friend, you do not know what divinity is:

> **34.** Once he himself went to overcome Narayana — and was defeated by Him.[^01-40]
> Embodied beings cannot delude Hari, the enchanter of the enchanter of the world.

And yet they have already been appointed by me. They gave their promise: "When His childhood is past, we shall overcome Him." But that too is impossible. For

> **35.** At the very beginning of His youth He left His new wife, radiant like Lakshmi,
> and, fulfilling His own duty, went to Gaya and performed the funeral rite for His father.[^01-41]

And further:

> **36.** There, by the will of providence, He accepted Ishvara Puri, a lord of sannyasis whom He met;
> the Lord of the self-controlled received from this preceptor, as from a guru,
> the knowledge of ten syllables that had been in the keeping of Madhava Puri.[^01-42]

And further:

> **37.** Returning to His home, surrounded by dear associates headed by Shrivasa, Rama and Haridasa,
> He sings, dances, enacts [feelings] and weeps bitterly — and plunges the three worlds into oceans of bliss.[^01-43]

How could wretched Kama find his chance here?

**Adharma.** Friend, do not say so!

> **38.** Those who were devoted to the severest austerities, to tranquillity, self-control, vows, concentration, meditation and yoga,
> whose wisdom rejected, like vomited food, everything up to the seat of Brahma and the riches of the three worlds,
> who conquered even the enemies — Kandarpa and the rest, invincible by nature —
> fell the moment he touched them: tell me, who can conquer him, Anger, and how?[^01-44]

**Kali.** My friend, what can wretched Anger do to Him? Here is what happened. There lived two, named Jagannatha and Madhava:[^01-45] ministers in the sport of every kind of lawlessness; their hearts were baked in the five great sins with all their ramifications; their whole measure was nothing but the oppression of all the people; arrant plunderers, some sort of brahmins by dress alone, and shabby dress at that; zealous in evil deeds, miserly, brothers born of one womb, in appearance drums of deceit; from day to day the filth in their minds grew ever greater. Of His own accord, with the insistence of mercy, He called them and had them brought before Him, saying: "Whatever sin you have committed, greedy for the poison of sin — give it all to Me with attention." Hearing this, they were for a moment somehow stunned with amazement and astonishment, and then they said: "We give it!" — and, taking water from His hand,[^01-46] at once began to shine; a thick thrill of the hair, rising swiftly, covered them like armour, tears of joy flowed from their eyes like the water of consecration into bliss, their throats were choked, and stammering they repeated: "Krishna, Krishna!" Through the purity of a mind long restrained, through bhakti-yoga long attained, lust and the other unbridled vices left them, and they ascended to the rank of great devotees. By such a transformation, born of bliss, He without any hindrance dispelled the doubts of all the onlookers and made them motionless like figures in a painting. So what is surprising if the Lord, who lightens every sin, who with a single sidelong glance cuts away from the hearts of others the band of the six enemies, lust and the rest, brings anger under His control?

*(Behind the scenes, a joyful uproar.)*

**Adharma** *(acting as if listening)*. My friend! Do you hear? Today this din and clamour of joyful excitement comes from the house of Shrivasa. From this I infer that some immeasurably great, venerable, heart-astonishing deed of His is unfolding.

*(Again behind the scenes, the sounds of various musical instruments together with cries of "ulu-lu".)*

**Kali** *(looking intently)*. Ah! We inferred rightly — as if we were seeing it with our own eyes. Look: to the unrestrained ringing of conches and bells, which swells the thunder of the many instruments echoing the cries of "Jaya! Jaya!" born of supreme joy together with the auspicious cries of "ulu-lu"[^01-47] from the mouths of throngs of young brahmin wives, lovely as goddesses, some great festival is unfolding, like a shower of nectar-juice pouring into the hollows of the ears. This must certainly be observed carefully. *(Does so.)*

*(Again, there, behind the scenes.)*

> **39.** Rama, quickly bring the arghya and the rest! Shripati, good fellow, go faster,
> set out a hundred and eight new pitchers! Shrikanta, send the women out in every direction,
> and let other fortunate brahmin wives bring water from the river of the immortals (the Ganga)!

**Kali.** Friend, I have noted it well: it is Shrivasa giving orders to his brothers. I think the festival of the great bathing of that very Vishvambhara-deva is beginning, whose supreme power blossoms in the manifest possession of Him by His own nature.[^01-48]

**Adharma.** If He is Ishvara Himself, governed by none but Himself, where does this present possession come from?

**Kali.** Friend, listen:

> **40.** Ah, though the divine nature of the Lord is eternal and mighty,
> He, being independent, does not always manifest it.
> Out of playful whim He takes on just the ways of the world —
> and those who know call this His supreme, sweetest lila (play).

*(Again looking towards the back of the stage.)* Friend, look, look! He has entered the Lord's room[^01-49] — as if making the abode of Shrivasa, best of brahmins, visible to the eyes of all people like a corner of Ilavrita-varsha, with a radiance that pours in clusters of waves of rays from the summit of the golden mountain wholly embraced by the rays of the newly risen sun;[^01-50] like bliss gathered into one and intoxicated with great rapture; like a mass of garlands of lightning flashing all at once and breaking loose. He has mounted the great couch of the shalagrama and the other [Deities], moved the shalagrama and the rest aside and seated Himself. And all the others, in a flurry running hither and thither, covered with a thick thrill of hair and with tears, brought the articles of worship that had been named beforehand — as if their senses had lost all their skill and their hearts had been cleansed of thirst for the various objects of the senses — and surrounded Him on every side.

*(Again behind the scenes.)*

> **41.** Rama, prepare fragrant, purified water here;
> Mukunda, gather yourself all that is needed for the bathing;
> Gadadhara, ho, arrange the garments, garlands, ornaments and the rest:
> I shall bathe Hari right here, on the couch.

**Kali** *(looking all around, towards the back of the stage)*. Friend, look, look:

> **42.** Ah, the women, their hands busy with auspicious pitchers,
> go and come — from all ends of the town to the heavenly river, and from its bank to the town;
> on their lips are His deeds, in their eyes tears, their bodies tremble,
> their braids have come loose, on their cheeks the hair stands on end — a wonder!

**Adharma.** Friend, better say: "their braids have come loose" — for all of this is the flashing of Kama. For

> **43.** Wherever the doe-eyed go, there strides the valour of Madana:
> does a general conquer alone, without the army that gives him his superiority?

**Kali.**[^01-51] That too is not so.

> **44.** A mind struck by [amorous] feeling throws both into turmoil;
> but in those who are free of such feeling, the outward appearance is in no way at fault.

*(Again behind the scenes, a din swelling the loud uproar, sweet with the recitation of the "Purusha-sukta".[^01-52])*

**Kali** *(gazing)*. Ah, the greatest wonder!

> **45.** As streams from the kamandalu of Brahma, as from Sumeru, ceaselessly bathed
> by the torrents of the waters of the heavenly Ganga — so from the pitchers, pouring again and again,
> falling from the body of Gauranga and reviving the circle of the earth,
> the streams of the waters of the great bathing flowed out to the four quarters of the world.[^01-53]

And further: look — after the bathing, the bathing water that ran from Sri Vishvambhara like rivers has been wiped away; He is beautiful, clad in garments of His liking; some devotees, beside themselves with joy, have anointed Him all over, others have adorned Him, others have washed His lotus feet; arati has been performed to Him with lamps in vessels of the purest, fire-refined gold, and the golden radiance of His body has flashed forth; His majesty is envied by radiance itself. And all, filled with the weight of love, at the foot of His lotus feet, setting out their wealth as their means allow, putting Advaita at their head and banishing duality,[^01-54] offer Him gold, jewels, garments and the rest.

**Adharma.** Friend, here is a chance for Greed. For

> **46.** Greed — destroyer of steadfastness, hater of happiness in the end, opponent of modesty —
> ah, can anyone at all overcome it?
> Even the god Vishnu coveted the Kaustubha, born of the churning of the Milk Ocean,
> the jewel among jewels — and Shri (Lakshmi).

**Kali.** He is not like that. *(Gazing.)* Look, look! He

> **47.** does not speak, does not look and hears nothing;
> He is stilled in His own bliss — and only His radiance keeps increasing.

**Adharma.** Friend, this too is the usual way of Mada (Intoxication):

> **48.** Ah, the dumb he makes dumb, the sightless blind, the not-deaf deaf;
> this strong one robs the sensible of their sense:
> tell me, whom does the intoxication of wealth not ruin?

So there is no need to worry here. When one has his chance, it must be supposed that all have it: [for it is said that] "those named in a single rule" act together or abstain together.[^01-55] And so:

> **50.** In whom, from intolerance of another's excellence, there arises an impurity of mind that breeds cruelty and cunning,
> him it burns in an instant, as fire in a hollow burns a tree;
> because of it the name "villain", troubler of the world, becomes fixed on those whose hearts it has invaded —
> this is your chief minister named Matsarya (Envy), and he is secretly present here.[^01-56]

*(Again behind the scenes.)* Ho, Advaita!

> **51.** He for whom eighteen watches[^01-57] have passed like a moment —
> how can we, insignificant as we are, serve Him who is full of bliss?

Therefore now all of you glorify Him so that, out of love for His devotees, He may lessen His natural, dense bliss and turn to outward awareness.

**Kali.** Do you hear, friend, do you hear? The devotees simply cannot bear His majesty — that is why Shrivasa speaks so.

**Adharma.** I hear. But these very words betray His inner intoxication, and after it delusion (*moha*) as well. Like this:

> **51a.** If the bliss were natural, He could not give it up;
> so, swollen with inner intoxication, out of great cunning He regards the universe as a straw.
> And if He has no attachment to His own, why then, ah, does He heed their words?

**Kali.** Out of tender affection.

**Adharma.**
> In an insignificant person it is called "delusion"; in another, people call the very same thing "tender affection".[^01-58]

**Kali.** Ignoramus! This reasoning concerns the living soul: for insignificance and its opposite belong to the living soul.[^01-59] *(Again looking towards the back of the stage.)* Ah, how suddenly

> **52.** Advaita and all the others fall at once to the ground like rods,
> and all the women, first among them the wives of the four brothers, Shrivasa and the rest.

Ah, I understand!

> It is the Lord who has opened His eyes — like garlands of full-blown lotuses,
> pouring the honey of mercy, awakened by the waves of His own bliss.

*(Again, gazing for a long time.)* Ah, the greatest wonder!

> **53.** When the Lord, in a voice deep and firm as the thunder of the clouds, said: "Fix your minds on Me!" —
> and placed His lotus feet on their heads,
> they sank into the slumber of bliss — in tears, in longing, trembling,
> sighing, with hair standing on end, exulting and marvelling.[^01-60]

After this they will all come here. So come, let us think of some other place.

**Adharma.** Friend, where then should I seek a dwelling?

**Kali.** It has already been thought out. Listen:

> **54.** Those who, though endowed with learning, character, austerity, noble birth and an ashrama of life, and wholly self-controlled,
> yet revile the sinless deeds of this Lord — live among them.
> And let your wife Mrisha (Falsehood) dwell in the mouths of those turned away [from God],
> and your son Dambha (Hypocrisy) among those devoted to dry rituals alone. Do not grieve over this.

**Adharma.** As you please.

*(Exeunt both.)*

*End of the vishkambhaka (introductory scene).*

*(Then enters the Lord Vishvambhara, seated on the couch, in divine dress and ornaments, as if awakened from the sleep of His own bliss; around Him His retinue, Advaita and the others;[^01-61] at a distance, other devotees singing kirtana.)*

**The Lord.** Advaita!

> **55.** It was you who brought Me down from Goloka by the irresistible,
> supreme flood of the nectar of your love, unequalled in humility and the rest.

**Advaita** *(joining his palms)*.
> Who am I, the most insignificant? It is You Yourself who, by the will of Your own play,
> eager to show mercy to the world, have revealed Yourself on earth.

How else could it be? Kunti said:

> **56.** "How can we women see You, [who have appeared] in order to establish bhakti-yoga
> for the paramahamsas, the sages of pure heart?"[^01-62]

According to her word, in order to make juicy the hearts of such sages, dried up for want of rasa, Bhagavan Himself, the son of the king of Vraja — the auspicious descent who revealed the good of the world — performed His pastimes in Gokula, Mathura and Dvaraka, full of hearing, chanting and the rest, which can render all other goals of man meaningless — pastimes more astonishing even than the bliss of Brahman. And, according to the word of Uddhava:

> **57.** "Fortunately you have established unsurpassed bhakti
> for Bhagavan Uttamashloka, hard to attain even for sages"[^01-63] —

in the abode of Sri Vrindavana, otherwise called Gokula, which lies beyond the ocean of all rasas, in the land of boundless, unsurpassed bliss, the timid cowherd maidens — embodied potencies of bliss, whose portion is the highest share of ever-increasing love of the heart — established such a truth of divine love; and it then remained there alone. And now You, the Lord rich in good fortune, have taken a fitting body, longing to taste its flavour, which is now being attained; You ceaselessly sip mouthfuls of the nectar broth of its sweetest secret rasa — how else could we too taste the drops that seem to trickle through the gaps between Your fingers?

**The Lord.** Shrivasa! Do you remember how here, with a deft slap, I held back your departing life-breaths?

**Shrivasa** *(acting as if remembering)*.
> **58.** Ah, I know, best of lords! I remember
> that someone forcibly snatched me from death.

*(All act amazement.)*

**The Lord.** Shrivasa, tell it from the beginning. Let everyone listen.

**Shrivasa.** Before the appearance of the Lord, from infancy to the age of sixteen, this creature was very sharp-toothed and in mad arrogance constantly offended brahmins, gurus and others; my heart had reached the utmost limit of restlessness and become merciless; my mind, intoxicated and confused, was occupied with idle waves of quarrels, evil talk and the like; not even in a dream did I hear or chant the qualities of the Lord. But once, by virtue of piety accumulated before, when sleep had robbed me of consciousness, it seemed to me as if someone merciful instructed me in a dream thus: "Hey you, a brahmin in name only![^01-64] Who would speak words of instruction to you, fickle heart? And yet we shall say it: you have one more year to live, and then your life will end. Do not waste your life." When the night had passed, I remembered these words of instruction, lost heart because so little life was left, gave up all frivolity and fasted that day, nourished only by the nectar of that instruction. To decide what is the highest good of man, I found, as was proper, this verse of the *Nārada Purāṇa*:

> **59.** "The name of Hari, the name of Hari, the name of Hari alone!
> In the age of Kali there is no other way, no other way, no other way."[^01-65]

Then, regarding it as if it were the instruction of the Subduer of the Danavas Himself, I gave up all duties enjoined by the Veda and, taking refuge in the name of Hari alone, forgot even my home. Though everyone laughed at me, I, at peace, not distracted for a moment by anything else, kept remembering that day of death, held grief in my heart and counted the months. When the year was over and the day of death had come, I, with what remained of my life, came to the house of Devananda Pandita, the teacher of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, wishing to hear him. While I was listening to the deeds of Prahlada, that very hour arrived: my consciousness clouded over, I fell into the power of death and dropped from the veranda of his house into the courtyard. Then, regarding myself as snatched by someone from the jaws of death and given life once more, I rose as if helpless. At that very moment everyone lifted me up and carried me home.

**The Lord.** It was I who showed you that dream at night; and you, whose life had already gone, I saved, giving you life again.

*(All act amazement.)*

**The Lord.**
> **60.** As iron, by the touch of the philosopher's stone, is turned into gold,
> so your body too is the same, yet, through the entry of Narada's power, as if another.

**Advaita.** So it is: otherwise the dead do not come back to life. But, Lord, all of them are associates of Your own intrinsic nature; and yet the Lord, inexhaustibly blissful, so ordained it as a lesson: "A man who worships Me obtains as it were a new body." In truth this Shrivasa, whose greatness has been sung, is Shri-vasa, the abode (*vāsa*) of the beauty (*śrī*) of devotion to You.[^01-66]

**The Lord.** Advaita, that is true.

**Advaita.** Lord! And Murari, Mukunda and the others — they too impart the mood of service to You and give joy to the eyes.

**The Lord.** Advaita, between these two there is [in each] a great lawlessness.

*(Then both tremble with fear, as if struck by lightning.)*

**Advaita.** Master, what is it?

**The Lord.** In Murari's mind the rasa of bhakti does not take root: in him there is only a possession by the demon of attachment to contemplation of the "supreme Self" (*adhyātma*), spreading a pungency like the stench of garlic — for even now he celebrates [the study of] the "Vasishtha" every moment.[^01-67]

**Advaita.** But what fault is there in adhyatma-yoga?

**The Lord.** How can you say so?

> **61.** "For one who has devotion to the Lord Hari, the master of the highest good,
> who sports in the ocean of nectar — what is the water of little ditches to him?"[^01-68]

**Advaita.** And what fault has Mukunda committed?

**The Lord.** He declares — it pleases him[^01-69] — that only the four-armed form of Bhagavan is to be worshipped.

**Advaita.** Ah, what kind of opinion is this — no opinion at all?

**The Lord.** Bhagavan's four-armed form is by His wish; His natural form is two-armed alone. For it is said: "The Supreme Brahman in human form"; "the hidden Supreme Brahman with the marks of a man";[^01-70] "the Supreme Self in human form".[^01-71] The human form is the two-armed form.

**Advaita.** "You Yourself know Yourself by Yourself, O Supreme Person."[^01-72] So be pleased to tell us the truth about Your own form.

**The Lord** *(graciously)*. That will be shown to you.

**Advaita.** I am favoured. But, Lord, be merciful to these two: they are already pining. Enough of anger in the heart for a grave offence.[^01-73]

> **62.** O parasol of Your feet — which takes away the grief caused by the poison of evil tendencies, grants cool shade,
> pours the honey of abundant mercy, and skilfully puts lotuses to shame! —
> place it on their heads: show them this mercy.

**The Lord** *(doing so)*.
> **63.** "This Bhagavan, the son of the cowherd woman, is not so easily attained by the embodied,
> nor by the learned, nor by those identified with the Atman, as by the devotees."[^01-74]

*(Having recited this, compassionately.)* Henceforth do not be possessed by alien inclinations.

**Both** *(falling prostrate like rods)*.
> **64.** "O Hari, may I become again a servant of the servants of those whose only refuge is Your feet;
> may my mind remember Your qualities, O Lord of my life, may my speech sing them, and my body labour [for You]."[^01-75]

*(So they recite.)*

**The Lord.** So be it.

**Shuklambara** *(humbly)*. Master!

> **65.** I have performed many austerities, O master, visited many holy places,
> and still my mind has found no peace. Cast a merciful glance on me, be merciful!

*(Having recited this, without timidity, with dignity and without embarrassment he lays his head on the Lord's lotus feet.)*

**Shrivasa** *(looking; aside, to Gadadhara)*.[^01-76] Gadadhara, look, look!

> **66.** The heart of this brahmin, hard as a thunderbolt from the proud zeal grown out of the severest austerities,
> suddenly, as soon as it touched the feet of the Lord, has as it were melted
> and is pouring out through his eyes and the hairs of his body.

And now there is this: Shachi-devi, seeing in Him only her son, has scolded us many times for sporting with Him in the bliss of kirtana: "It is they who by their evil influence have driven my son mad; his path of worldly affairs has been cut off." But if she now sees her son — who has set aside the thought that He is [her] son, helpless in the possession of the bliss born of Himself — then she will give up her motherly feeling for Him in favour of [revering Him as] God. How can this be brought about?

**Gadadhara.** If the Acharya were to tell [her]…

**Advaita.** What are you talking about?

**Shrivasa** *(in his ear)*. Just this.

**Advaita.** Very well. Let her be brought now. Why worry? Let her delusion be dispelled.

**Shrivasa.** As you command. *(Goes out, brings her and enters again.)* Master Acharya! After due thought, present this mother of the world [to the Lord]; let the mercy and favour of the Lord rejoice here.

**Advaita** *(joining his palms)*. Master! Devahuti, they say, was blessed by Kapila with the yoga of knowledge and the yoga of bhakti.[^01-77] But now let this mother of Vishvambhara, surrounded by the art of the good conduct of Vishvambhara's people,[^01-78] be blessed by nothing but the ever-increasing bliss of divine love. *(Taking her by the tips of her fingers, with the greatest joy he leads her to the Lord.)*

**Shachi** *(gazing with amazement and wonder at her son, lovely in His absorption in His own bliss, wondrous in His acts; filled with compassion for Him, her body trembling, as if illumined by inspiration — the descent of the goddess of speech — she recites something).*

> **67.** "You are the Supreme Person who at the end of the night [of the world] holds in Your body
> this whole universe, as much room as there is in it —
> and You have come to be in my womb! Ah, this is a mockery of the world of men!"[^01-79]

*(Glorifying Him thus, beside herself, as if possessed by a spirit, she tries to seize His feet by force.)*

**Advaita** *(holding her back, with amazement)*.
> **68.** Whence has this illumination come to her? For in her is that very natural motherly feeling:
> a former, unconditioned feeling passes over into another body too.[^01-80]

**The Lord.** Lady! Though you are the mother of the world, still, when your offence committed against Shrivasa and the others has ceased, the mercy of the Supreme will be yours. For offence against a devotee is the enemy of the waves of bhava.[^01-81]

**Advaita.** Lord, not so!

> **69.** This mother of the world is in no way at fault,
> since You, the Lord, were born from her womb.
> Ah, sons may commit offences against their mother,
> but a mother against her sons — never.

*(Turning to Shrivasa.)* Ho, Shrivasa!

> **70.** Although the daughter of Devaka[^01-82] saw the Lord in her son,
> yet there was not born in her such bhakti
> as in this one, who wished here to seize the feet of her son.

**Shrivasa.** Now we are at peace.

**Advaita.** Ho, ho, Shrivasa and the others! In His possession by ever-increasing majesty alone, even His filial feeling for His mother and the mother's motherly feeling have been cast aside. If so, glorify Him, so that this possession may cease.

**All.** Master, so we shall. *(Together with Advaita they do so.)* O Lord!

> **71.** Eternal is the divinity of Bhagavan, O Lord, eternal His nature of knowledge, bliss and consciousness;
> and yet, ah, whatever body He assumes, He assumes its nature too.

Therefore now, protecting us, be pleased to show this mercy: the form You have worn from childhood, revealing Your descent as a devotee, in keeping with the good fortune of such as we — the form by which You have gladdened us and cut away all our sufferings — reveal that now. As Arjuna said:

> **72.** "Seeing this gentle human form of Yours, O Janardana,
> I have now come to myself and returned to my own nature."[^01-83]

But in essence:

> **73.** In a transcendental object the worldly does not contradict the transcendental:
> the presence of the properties of other gems does not diminish the chintamani stone.[^01-84]

**The Lord** *(remembering the mood of a devotee, entering it and returning to His ordinary nature)*. Ho, Advaita and the others! Have I been all this time as if in a dream? Why did you not wake Me?

**All.** Lord, we were afraid to interrupt Your sleep of bliss.

**The Lord.** Alas, alas! So much time I have wasted, bereft of understanding. Come, then, let us sing of Sri Krishna!

**All** *(joyfully)*. As the master commands.

*(Exeunt all.)*

*Thus ends the first act of the* Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya, *called "Absorption in His Own Bliss".*

[^01-1]: A play on words connected with the moon (*candra*). The *nidhis* are the nine treasures of Kubera (*kumuda, padma, śaṅkha* and others); but *kumuda* is also the night-lily and *padma* the day-lotus, which closes when the moon rises. The "moonstones" (*candra-kānta*) of bhakti rob worldly riches of their charm. The chakravaka (*koka*) is a bird which, by poetic convention, is parted from its mate at night and grieves; the rising of the moon, Gaura, is grief for Kali.

[^01-2]: A description of Jagannatha (Purushottama) in Puri: dark blue like a sapphire, on the shore of the ocean; Nilagiri ("the Blue Mountain") is the hill on which the temple stands; the great banyan (*mahāvaṭa*) is the sacred, imperishable banyan (*akṣaya-vaṭa*) within the temple grounds. The long prose of the prologue is built on unbroken sound-repetitions (*yamaka*: *-mukhara-mukha-ramamāṇa-*, *-rasāvibhakta-bhakta-*, *sevādhikāritayā ratayā* and so on), which cannot be reproduced in translation; the sense is rendered.

[^01-3]: That is, Mahaprabhu's departure from the world (1533). The prologue is set in Puri after His disappearance.

[^01-4]: According to the Puranas, the universe is enclosed by seven (or eight) coverings (*āvaraṇa*) of earth, water, fire and so on. The poet jests: Vishnu "plastered" the universe beforehand, fearing that the heat of Prataparudra's valour would burst it.

[^01-5]: Paramananda Dasa (Puri Dasa) is the name of the author himself, known by the title Kavi Karnapura; Srinatha (Srinatha Pandita) is his guru.

[^01-6]: *Bhūtāraṇa-kṛtaḥ* (= *bhū-tāraṇa-kṛtaḥ*), "saviours of the earth, of the world": so the Bengali translation of M. Guha ("who came for the deliverance of the world") and the Hindi translation of Krishnadasa Baba ("deliverers of the world") understand it. Grammatically "who give refuge (*araṇa*) to beings" is also possible.

[^01-7]: The image of a tree: Madhava is Madhavendra Puri, the guru of Ishvara Puri; the Avadhuta is Nityananda. The Kāvyamālā edition reads *munīndraḥ* (nominative), which is grammatically more exact.

[^01-8]: The Kāvyamālā edition reads *caitanya-kalpa-druma iva* ("like a wish-fulfilling tree") for *iha* ("here"), and *bhinna-bhāvena dīnam* for *hīnam*; the reading of the main text is followed.

[^01-9]: A play on words: *kiṁ teneha tene* — "has He (*tena*) here (*iha*) composed (*tene*)…".

[^01-10]: *Yo 'gocaraḥ śāstra-kṛtām*, "which is beyond the reach (*agocaraḥ*) of the authors of the shastras": so M. Guha's Bengali translation and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understand it. Grammatically *yo gocaraḥ*, "known to the authors of the shastras", is also possible.

[^01-11]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.2.40.

[^01-12]: A pun, *bhakti-yogasya yo 'gasya*: *aga* means "mountain", i.e. "unshakeable as a mountain"; then comes the sound-repetition *pārṣada-bhāvaṁ bhāvaṁ bhāvam*.

[^01-13]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 3.25.35; just before it 3.25.36 (*tair darśanīyāvayavaiḥ*) is mentioned. These are the words of Kapila to His mother, Devahuti.

[^01-14]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 3.25.32–33.

[^01-15]: Words about Ajamila: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa*, Canto 6 (the source gives the references 6.3.24 and 6.3.43; in the accepted numbering, 6.2.44 and thereabouts).

[^01-16]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 12.3.43. In the source verses 10–12 are unnumbered; the numbers are restored from the gap in the numbering.

[^01-17]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 9.24.61.

[^01-18]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.5.38 (in the source the half-verses are transposed).

[^01-19]: In the electronic text this speech is not separated from the assistant's question; in the Kāvyamālā edition the verse is spoken by the stage-director.

[^01-20]: A double meaning. *Kṛṣṇa-pakṣa* is "the dark fortnight" and "the side of Krishna"; *doṣākara* is "the maker of night", i.e. the moon, and "a mine of vices", i.e. Kali; *viṣṇu-pada* is "the sky" and "the feet of Vishnu". The moon wanes in the dark fortnight and does no harm to the sky; so Kali grows weak when people are on Krishna's side and cannot harm those who have taken refuge at the feet of Vishnu. Kali, from behind the scenes, is offended at being called a *doṣā-kara* and at the same time compared with the moon (the "nectar-maker", *sudhākara*).

[^01-21]: In the electronic text this speech is wrongly assigned to Kali; but the address "king of the age" (*yugarāja*) and the sense of verse 14 show that Adharma is speaking (in the Kāvyamālā edition the speaker's name has been lost in the OCR).

[^01-22]: The construction is *yenaikena mayā sa yasya vaśagaḥ*: "he by whom alone — by me — [all this was done] is subject to him", i.e. Adharma, who crushed Dharma, is merely Kali's servant.

[^01-23]: The Kāvyamālā edition reads *pakṣe yasya kaleḥ kṣayaḥ*, "…with him on whose side is the destruction of Kali?"; the reading of the main text (*kaleḥ svayam*) is followed.

[^01-24]: A play on words: *kumāraka* "boy" — *ku-māraka* "vile killer" or "killer of *ku* (the earth)"; *nobhayam* "neither" — *no bhayam* "not fear".

[^01-25]: Dharma in the form of a bull: in the Satya age he has four legs, in the Kali age only one remains (*Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 1.16–17).

[^01-26]: A pun: *bhū-deva-bāla*, "child of a god of the earth", i.e. of a brahmin; *bāla-deva-deva*, "the God of Baladeva", i.e. Krishna Himself.

[^01-27]: "Hari, Hari!" (like "Shiva, Shiva!" in v. 26) is an exclamation of vexation and dismay (so the Kāvyamālā edition also explains); the irony is that Kali, against his will, utters the names of God.

[^01-28]: Mahaprabhu was born on the full moon of the month of Phalguna (1486) during a lunar eclipse, when people, as was the custom, were bathing and chanting the names of Hari.

[^01-29]: *Kāka-tālīya-nyāya*, "the maxim of the crow and the palm fruit": a crow alighted on a palm, and at that very moment a fruit fell; i.e. a chance coincidence.

[^01-30]: *Bhagavad-anavamam*, "not inferior to Bhagavan"; the Kāvyamālā edition explains: "equal to Sri Narayana".

[^01-31]: Shrikanta, Shripati and Shrirama (Rama) are the three brothers of Shrivasa (so too in M. Guha's Bengali translation); in later Bengali tradition one of the brothers is usually called Shrinidhi. Shrivasa is regarded as an incarnation of Narada.

[^01-32]: Nrisimha is probably Nrisimhananda (Pradyumna Brahmachari); Damodara and Shankara are the brothers Damodara Pandita and Shankara Pandita.

[^01-33]: The Kāvyamālā edition reads *smṛti-mati-rati-vidyā-*, "…memory, intelligence, attraction (*rati*), learning…".

[^01-34]: *Anaikāntika*, a term of Nyaya: an "inconclusive" argument, whose mark occurs also where the thing to be proved is absent.

[^01-35]: *Bhagavad-gītā* 10.41 (10.42 in the source). The number of verse 27 is not given in the source and is restored from the gap.

[^01-36]: Lakshmipriya, Vishvambhara's first wife, left this world after a snakebite while He was in East Bengal.

[^01-37]: *Viṣṇu Purāṇa* 1.9.143 (about Lakshmi).

[^01-38]: Vishvarupa, Vishvambhara's elder brother, took sannyasa as a youth and went away. *Purīśvara* is a respectful designation of a sannyasi of the Puri order: M. Guha's Bengali translation understands "passed his splendour on to Paramananda Puri", and the drama itself confirms this (Act 8: "all the divine power" of Vishvarupa "entered" Paramananda Puri). Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understands "to Ishvara Puri". According to Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition Vishvarupa entered into Nityananda.

[^01-39]: The stories: Brahma desired his daughter Sarasvati (Vak); Shiva ran after Mohini, the female form of Vishnu (*Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 8.12). Kali's six "ministers" are the six enemies: lust, anger, greed, delusion, intoxication (pride) and envy.

[^01-40]: Kama tried to disturb the sage Narayana (Nara-Narayana) at Badarika and was defeated (*Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.4.7–16). Krishna as "the enchanter of the enchanter of the world" is Madana-mohana.

[^01-41]: The Kāvyamālā edition reads *dyutimatīṁ … bhāryām* and *yātaś* ("went"); the electronic text has *jātaś*; *yātaś* is followed.

[^01-42]: The ten-syllable mantra of Gopala, received by Ishvara Puri from Madhavendra Puri.

[^01-43]: The number 37 is not given in the source; it is restored from the gap.

[^01-44]: An allusion to irascible sages (Durvasa and others), who conquered lust but fell victim to anger.

[^01-45]: Jagai and Madhai, brahmin brothers, robbers and drunkards of Navadvipa, converted by Mahaprabhu. The prose is built on sound-repetitions: *brāhmaṇa-celayoḥ kucelayoḥ*, "brahmins (only) by dress, in shabby dress".

[^01-46]: That is, they performed the rite of gift-giving, in which the giver pours water from his hand (here, taking water from the Lord's hand as a sign of handing over their sins).

[^01-47]: *Ulūlu*, an auspicious festive cry raised by women in Bengal (so the Kāvyamālā edition also explains).

[^01-48]: The episode of the "great manifestation" (*mahā-prakāśa*) in the house of Shrivasa, when Vishvambhara sat on the altar of Vishnu and accepted worship as God Himself. *Āveśa*, "possession, entering": here the manifestation in Him of His own divine nature.

[^01-49]: *Bhagavad-geham*, the room of the Deities in Shrivasa's house.

[^01-50]: Ilavrita-varsha is the central region of Jambudvipa around the golden mountain Meru (Sumeru).

[^01-51]: The speech and verse 44 are missing in the electronic text (there verse 45 follows straight after verse 43); they are supplied from the Kāvyamālā edition (v. 39): *bhāvenopahataṁ ceto dvayeṣāṁ kṣobha-kārakam | nirbhāvānāṁ punas teṣām ākāro nāparādhyati*; the same text is in the editions of M. Guha (v. 44) and J. Vidyasagara. The understanding ("feeling" is the attraction of man and woman; "both" are men and women; in those free of it loosened braids and the like are not sinful) rests on Guha's Bengali translation and commentary, Vidyasagara's commentary and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation.

[^01-52]: The "Purusha-sukta" is the hymn of the *Ṛg-veda* (10.90) recited at the bathing of Deities.

[^01-53]: According to the Puranas, the Ganga, flowing out of the kamandalu (water-pot) of Brahma, falls on Sumeru and spreads to the four quarters.

[^01-54]: A play on words: *puraskṛtyādvaitaṁ*, "putting Advaita at their head" and "putting non-duality at their head". The electronic text has *dvaitaṁ pūrayadbhiḥ* ("filling duality"), the Kāvyamālā edition *dvaitaṁ dūrayadbhiḥ* ("removing duality"), which makes sense; the latter is followed.

[^01-55]: A rule of interpretation (*paribhāṣā*): what is mentioned together in one rule applies together. Here, Kali's six "ministers" act jointly. The number 49 is missing in the source; in the Kāvyamālā edition too there is no verse between "The dumb he makes dumb" and "In whom, from intolerance…", evidently a slip in the numbering.

[^01-56]: The electronic text has *khalu iti valate*; the Kāvyamālā edition *khala iti*, "villain"; the latter is followed.

[^01-57]: A watch (*yāma*) is about three hours. In the source the number 51 is repeated twice (here and at Adharma's next verse); the second is marked 51a.

[^01-58]: The last line of verse 51a, interrupted by Kali's remark.

[^01-59]: The Kāvyamālā edition reads *ajña-jīvāśrayo 'yaṁ vicāraḥ*, "this reasoning concerns the ignorant living soul".

[^01-60]: "Fix your minds on Me" (*mac-cittā bhavata*) echoes the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.57, 18.65 and elsewhere).

[^01-61]: In the electronic text the stage direction is corrupt (*vibhavaś cādvaitodayaś ca*); following the Kāvyamālā edition: *vibhavaś cādvaitādayaś ca*, "His retinue (splendour), Advaita and the others".

[^01-62]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 1.8.20. In the source verses 56, 59, 63 and 72 are unnumbered; the numbers are restored from the gaps.

[^01-63]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 10.47.25, the words of Uddhava to the gopis.

[^01-64]: *Brāhmaṇa-bruva*, "one who calls himself a brahmin", i.e. an unworthy brahmin (so the Kāvyamālā edition explains).

[^01-65]: The famous verse of the *Bṛhan-nāradīya Purāṇa* (38.126).

[^01-66]: A play on the name: *Śrīvāsa*, "the abode of Shri (beauty, Lakshmi)".

[^01-67]: That is, the *Yoga-vāsiṣṭha*, a treatise in the spirit of Advaita Vedanta. The Kāvyamālā edition takes *anukṣaṇa-kṣaṇa* as "ever celebrating" (*kṣaṇa*, "festival"). *Anaya* in the Lord's preceding speech means "misconduct, lawlessness" (a pun, *antar anayor anayo*).

[^01-68]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 6.12.22.

[^01-69]: A pun: *nirucyate rucyate*, "it is declared — it pleases".

[^01-70]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 7.10.48.

[^01-71]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 7.15.75.

[^01-72]: *Bhagavad-gītā* 10.15.

[^01-73]: The electronic text has *nayanayoḥ prasīda* ("be merciful to [their] eyes"); the Kāvyamālā edition *anayoḥ prasīda* ("be merciful to these two"), which fits the context; *kṛtaṁ mano-ruṣā paruṣāparādhena*, "enough of anger in the heart for a grave offence".

[^01-74]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 10.9.21.

[^01-75]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 6.11.24, the prayer of Vritrasura.

[^01-76]: The electronic text has a lacuna here (only "*śrīvāsaḥ:* +"); Shrivasa's speech with verse 66 and the following prose is supplied from the Kāvyamālā edition (v. 58). M. Guha's edition (v. 66) has here *nirbhartsitāḥ smaḥ*, "we were scolded" (corrupted in the Kāvyamālā OCR into *nirmitāḥ smaḥ*); Guha explains *dito* as "cut off" and *anayopacityā* as "by evil instruction/influence"; *deyatvena* in the Kāvyamālā OCR is an error for *devatvena* ("as God"). "Seeing in Him only her son" (*asmin putra-dhiyā*) refers to Shachi: so Guha's Bengali translation and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understand it.

[^01-77]: Kapila, the son of Devahuti, expounded to her Sankhya and bhakti-yoga (*Bhāgavata Purāṇa*, Canto 3).

[^01-78]: A pun: *viśvambhara-jananī*, "the mother of Vishvambhara" — *viśvambhara-jana-nīti*, "the good conduct of Vishvambhara's people". The passage admits another understanding as well.

[^01-79]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 10.3.31, the words of Devaki after the birth of Krishna.

[^01-80]: A hint that Shachi was Devaki in the past.

[^01-81]: According to the *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Shachi believed that Advaita had led her elder son Vishvarupa astray, and Mahaprabhu required her first to make amends for this offence before Advaita. Here the offence is related to Shrivasa and the others.

[^01-82]: Devaki, the mother of Krishna.

[^01-83]: *Bhagavad-gītā* 11.51.

[^01-84]: The chintamani is the wish-fulfilling gem.

---

# Act 2. The Vision of All the Avataras (Sarvāvatāra-darśana)

*Viraga, wandering over the earth seized by Kali, meets Bhakti-devi, who tells him of the deeds of Gaurachandra in Navadvipa; then the Lord reveals to Advaita the form of Shyamasundara.*

*(Enter Viraga.)*

**Viraga** *(looking all around)*. Ah, the world is full of those turned away from God![^02-1]

> **1.** No purity, no truthfulness, neither peace of mind nor control of the senses, no self-discipline either,
> no tranquillity, no forbearance — Shiva, Shiva! — neither friendliness (Maitri) nor compassion.
> Ah, these friends of mine, who love without any self-interest — have the people of Kali torn them up by the roots?
> Or do they live somewhere in secret, unrecognised by anyone?[^02-2]

Alas, how can one suppose that they live in secret? For there is no fit place for it. Look:

> **2.** The twice-born think only of the sixth of their duties, and their sole mark is the sacred thread;
> those born of the arms are distinguished by name alone, and the vaishyas are like Buddhists;
> the shudras, deeming themselves pandits, are eager to teach dharma as gurus —
> to such a state, alas, alas, has Kali brought the varnas![^02-3]

And further:

> **3.** Some here remain in the first ashrama only because they are unfit for marriage;
> the householders are devoted only to feeding wife, sons and their own belly;
> ah, the vanaprasthas are dear only to the ear — one only hears of them;
> and the wandering renunciants are recognised, at best, by their dress.[^02-4]

*(Walking a few steps.)* Ah, here is a fine region! Many scholars shine here, whose talent sparkles with swiftness.[^02-5] I must test them — whether they are outsiders[^02-6] or not. *(Observing for a long time.)* Ah!

> **4.** From their very birth they practise the string of words "upādhi", "jāti", "anumiti", "vyāpti" and the rest;
> they are endlessly, endlessly far from any talk of God.
> Whoever is the most skilful in inventions is, among them, the most learned;
> they take their own inventions for shastra — ah, these logicians![^02-7]

Enough of talk with them. I shall go from here to another place. *(Going some distance.)* Ah, these seem free of ailments[^02-8] and as if intent on welfare. Let me examine them. *(Does so.)* Ah, these are Mayavadis! Look:

> **5.** "We are pure being, without distinctions, consciousness free of limitations, beyond differentiation, without desire;
> we are Brahman alone" — with such words, Shiva, Shiva, they are at enmity with the form of God;
> rejecting all the attributes of God famed in the shruti — His inconceivable power and the rest —
> they cast away love (*rati*). Alas, obeisance to you — from afar![^02-9]

*(Leaving them, he again goes in another direction and looks about.)* Ah, these are disputing with one another; I must find out what the matter is. *(Gazing.)* Ah, these are experts in the doctrines of Kapila, Kanada, Patanjali and Jaimini, disputing with one another. None of them knows the truth about God. I must leave this place. *(Going a few more steps.)* Ah, I have strayed into the southern quarter![^02-10] For these are Arhatas, Saugatas and Kapalikas — fierce heretics. And these Pashupatas, cursed wretches,[^02-11] will even kill me. So the only refuge is to flee from here. *(Moving off, going some distance and noticing something.)* Ah, this must be a saint: comfortably seated on a flat stone that can be seen right by the river bank, free of suffering, he passes his time as if meditating on something beyond the gunas. Let me examine him. *(Does so.)* Ah!

> **6.** He displays great skill, blocking with the tip of his tongue the path of the nectar that flows from the moon in the forehead;
> closing his eyes and sitting in a posture, he meditates here on the river bank —
> why, then, has his samadhi been broken?

*(Reflecting with surprise.)* Ah, I understand!

> …Because he heard the jingle of the conch-shell bracelets of the young women who have come for water![^02-12]

So all this is merely a show for filling his belly. *(Again going in another direction and looking about.)* Ah, this one looks like a man without any possessions. He must be a pilgrim. I shall learn his heart from his talk — he is just conversing with a traveller who has happened by. *(Listening, he repeats it himself.)*

> **7.** "Gangadvara, Gaya, Prayaga, Mathura, Varanasi, Pushkara,
> Sri Ranga, Northern Koshala, Badarika, Setu, Prabhasa and the rest —
> going round this row of tirthas three or four times a year,
> how many hundreds of years I have spent so! Who can compare with the likes of us?"[^02-13]

*(Having repeated it.)* Excellent, sir, excellent! Truth, harassed by Kali, has found a safe hiding place in you![^02-14] Let us move away from here. *(Doing so and looking ahead.)* Ah, this ascetic, it seems, will prove worthy. Let me look closely. *(Does so.)* Alas, alas, this one is even worse than that! Look:

> **8.** Fiercely shouting "hum! hum! hum!" in a sharp, harsh voice, with a savage glance
> driving people far away, flinging his feet wide at every step,
> with clay on his forehead, arms, sides, neck, throat, belly and chest,[^02-15] his palm gleaming with kusha grass —
> who is this approaching: hypocrisy embodied, or pride?

Ah, here is a marvel now!

> **9.** Without unconditional devotion to Vishnu, firmness in concentration and meditation,
> skill in the labours of studying the shastras, in japa, austerity and rites —
> all these are only particular arts of superior dexterity, as with actors,[^02-16]
> various ways of filling the whirlpool of the pot of the belly.

Ah, Kali, well done! You have brought the whole earth under a single parasol.[^02-17] Look:

> **10.** Tranquillity, self-control and the rest are banished; and somewhere, tightly fettered,
> they are turned — alas! — into servants for earning money;
> the tree of dharma has been torn up completely, by the roots —
> and with it friendliness (Maitri) and the rest; what more could one wish?

*(Reflecting a moment.)* I am in great distress — from the pain of separation from such kinsmen, from weariness after searching for them so long, and from grief at seeing the earth become empty and worthless. I must rest somewhere, if only for a moment. *(Doing so, with tears.)*

> **11.** I have seen all this: everything is thrown into confusion by one discord alone — between mind and speech, between intention and act —
> from the exhaustion of the accumulated filth of Kali.
> When, alas, shall we see Vaishnavas who sing of Krishna and serve Him likewise,
> with tears, with hair standing on end — the same outside and inside?

*(Listening to the sky.)* What do you say? "Where bhakti is, there you must look for them"? *(Reflecting a moment.)* Ah, so Bhakti-devi is somewhere! *(Again fixing his gaze on the sky.)* What do you say, sir?

> **12.** "Glory to a certain land of Gauda, almost the crown of holy tirthas,
> which bears on itself the city named Sri Navadvipa.
> In it is the descent of the Lord who shines like a heap of gold,[^02-18]
> and there in every house[^02-19] Bhakti-devi stirs in bodily form."

*(Joyfully.)* Ah, ah! What will the living not see! I shall go from here straight there. *(Walks a few steps.)*

*(Then enters Bhakti-devi.)*

**Bhakti-devi** *(looking ahead)*. Oh, who is this? His mind is worn out by ceaseless heavy anxiety, his face has faded from the loss of honour and happiness; I seem to recognise him, and he, seeing me, falls into a state unlike the usual, shows joy and as if casts off his pain of himself — and is coming this way! Alas, now that all is well with us and we have found prosperity, the company of brother Viraga has been beyond our reach.[^02-20] I do not know whether he survived when misfortune overtook him from the wicked people of Kali, or not.

**Viraga** *(looking)*. Why, this is Bhakti-devi! For:

> **13.** She makes the heart serene, purifies the senses,
> makes liberation itself insignificant — what to speak of wealth and pleasures;
> as soon as she draws near, she at once brings living beings to their goal,
> plunging them into the depths of the ocean of bliss.

I shall approach her. *(Approaching.)* Goddess, I, Viraga, bow to you.

**Bhakti-devi** *(tenderly)*. Brother, you are alive! You are the life of the self-controlled.[^02-21] Come here, come! *(Touches him with her hand.)*

**Viraga** *(bowing at her feet)*. Goddess, tell me: how is it that Kali has not overcome you[^02-22] as he overcame Truth and the others?

**Bhakti-devi.** Viraga, do you not know? Listen. For our sake there has descended a certain greatly merciful Lord — Gaurachandra, whose deeds cut the bonds of worldly existence.

**Viraga.** Lady, tell me, what is the secret here?

**Bhakti-devi.** Viraga, in this age of Kali there is no other dharma — not even a particle of a bud;[^02-23] nothing here is lasting. Only the dharma of the Lord adorns this age of Kali and repels both bondage and delusion; therefore it alone is the true dharma, both the means and the goal. And so the Lord has made His descent in the form of a devotee, bringing with Him the yoga of pure bhakti — which carries away sins, crushes the filth of Kali and destroys, down to the chandala, the deep imprints of evil desires that even a chandala shuns[^02-24] — and together with it such as I, Bhakti-devi, with all our limbs and subsidiary limbs.[^02-25]

**Viraga.** That I have already understood from the heavenly voice, which spoke of it briefly. But:

> **14.** What do you desire, and what does that God desire?
> And will He become a refuge for me, who have no refuge?[^02-26]

**Bhakti-devi** *(changing to Sanskrit)*. Viraga, listen:

> **15.** We purify even a chandala, shake off every impurity,
> cut away in his heart even the firmest imprints of the past;
> and if the goddess Mercy (Kripa-devi) of this Lord casts her glance here,
> then we make grow in their hearts a certain indescribable experience of rasa as well.

**Viraga.** So without His goddess Mercy you yourselves have no such power?

**Bhakti-devi.** Without the ever-new mercy of Him or of His people we do not exist at all — what to speak of acting so!

**Viraga.** And what is the answer to the second question?

**Bhakti-devi.** Viraga, listen. *(In Sanskrit.)*

> **16.** Ah, there was no one in Navadvipa in whose house there was not a temple of Hari;
> there was no temple without a Deity of the Lord;
> there was no Deity that was not served; there was no service devoid of rasa;
> and there was no rasa in which sankirtana and dance were not the chief.[^02-27]

That is His desire.

**Viraga.** Does He Himself command it so, or do they themselves, grasping His intention, act so by their own understanding, in accordance with the essence?[^02-28]

**Bhakti-devi.** Such is His greatness: because of this greatness people, as soon as they see Him, become as if possessed — so that all know His intention of themselves and act accordingly. Together with His descent Kamala descended too:[^02-29] for no one there was poor. And what He Himself has been doing since His very childhood[^02-30] — listen.

> **17.** Now in the house of Shrivasa, now in the dwelling of Acharya-ratna,
> now in the abode of Sri Vidyanidhi, and now in the house of Murari —
> while His dear associates sing, God, filled with dense bliss,
> with trembling, stupor, tears, sweat and the rest, dances wildly day after day.

**Viraga.** Lady, does He always show only the conduct of a devotee? Or sometimes divine power as well?

**Bhakti-devi** *(in Sanskrit)*. Viraga, although

> **18.** this humanlike play of Hari is in a certain way more attractive
> than the superhuman —
> just as the Ganga, descending from the head of Mahesha into the midst of the earth,
> brings still greater joy[^02-31] —

yet sometimes He reveals the superhuman too. Thus, once in the abode of Shrivasa, when the Lord Vishvambhara was circumambulating the shrine, He was seen by a certain mleccha who was in the southern courtyard: by the will of fate he lived by the needle,[^02-32] a great drunkard and a sinner;[^02-33] he was sewing clothes for amusement and had been drunk for a very long time. Seeing the Lord, he was at once overwhelmed by the intoxication of this sight, which intoxicated more than the drunkenness of wine: his eyes opened wide — "hee-hee! I have seen, I have seen something!" — his whole body thrilled, his chest was drenched by unceasing streams of tears; throwing aside his clothes and the rest, he raised his arms and broke into a dance. Then, seeing him so, the Lord, concealing nothing, said to Shrivasa: "Shrivasa, what is this? How has he suddenly become like this, with a festival in his eyes?" And Shrivasa answered, as if giving an example of the rasa of humour: "Lord, unheard-of is the greatness of this intoxicant of Yours! For this man, though he is even now addicted to jugs of wine, never lost his senses from them; but from this glance at You — the size of a sesame seed — he has become sinless, has been freed from the torments born of ignorance,[^02-34] and, becoming a benefactor of others, is utterly intoxicated."

**Viraga.** And then, and then?

**Bhakti-devi.** And so from that time, taking refuge in the name of the Lord alone, he has left all his household, put on the dress of an avadhuta,[^02-35] and, dancing and singing, though he is beaten by the acharyas of the Yavanas,[^02-36] unfailingly performs the sankirtana of the Lord's name. And if anyone asks him, he answers: "Vishvambhara alone is God, and no one else." All the Bhagavatas take care of his food, and he wanders like a siddha.

**Viraga.** Goddess, in what form did he see Him?

**Bhakti-devi.** Viraga, the form of the Lord is bliss itself. That form in which the greatest bliss is born is His form. Thus bliss and forms depend on each other, and so the degrees of beholding the form correspond to the degrees of bliss.

**Viraga.** How did this man of low birth become the vessel of such happiness?

**Bhakti-devi** *(in Sanskrit)*.

> **19.** The mercy of Hari does not look to birth, character, ashrama, dharma, learning,
> lineage and the rest;
> it is self-willed — alas, it has no notion
> of the difference between the worthy and the unworthy.

**Viraga.** So it is. And then, and then?

**Bhakti-devi.** Then, on another day, in the courtyard of Murari's house, on a night bathed in the light of the full moon, all the Bhagavatas sitting there saw that same God in the form of Sankarshana.

**Viraga.** Goddess, tell it in more detail.

**Bhakti-devi.** Before them the sky was veiled in darkness: swarms of intoxicated bees, circling, were drinking the streams of moonlight, and the sky grew dark at that moment;[^02-37] and then all the people perceived a certain fragrance of *kādambarī*.[^02-38]

**Viraga.** And then, and then?

**Bhakti-devi.** And so, perceiving this fragrance, all asked the Lord: "Lord! How is it that this sudden, pure fragrance of *kādambarī*, able to blind the swarms of flower-drinkers, awakens amazement in us? And further on a plough appears, rousing curiosity, and a club that crushes the enemy's host!"[^02-39]

**Viraga.** And then, and then?

**Bhakti-devi.** Then Sri Vishvambhara-deva said: "Oh! Today the Lord, the protector Sankarshana, who draws all hearts to Himself, must appear.[^02-40] Here before Him have appeared the excellent *kādambarī*, dear to His friends, and His weapons, the plough and the club, gleaming even without a battle."

**Viraga.** And then, and then?

**Bhakti-devi.** And before He had finished saying this, that God — Gaurachandra Himself — with eyes rolling and reddened, joyful from the fragrance of the wine, with an earring dancing at the edge of one ear, pale as the orb of the moon, in the highest degree[^02-41] became Talanka[^02-42] Himself, sporting to His heart's content. Then they, singing ever new songs of His deeds, made Him dance and glorified Him long, and for a moment He threw everyone into amazement.[^02-43]

**Viraga.** And then, and then?

**Bhakti-devi.** So, having portrayed in turn the chief avataras — Buddha, Varaha, Nrisimha[^02-44] — after some days, out of causeless mercy, He revealed to Nityananda the six-armed form.

**Viraga.** What was it like?

**Bhakti-devi** *(in Sanskrit)*.

> **20.** With two hands He held a lovely flute,
> with four — the club, conch, disc and lotus;
> He wore a crown, necklaces, armlets,
> the Vaijayanti garland and the Kaustubha gem.

And further:
