[^03-61]: The hidden sense: what Krishna has touched is already sanctified.
[^03-62]: *Mūla*, "root, base, original price, capital": the flowers (the principal) have already been given to Krishna.
[^03-63]: *Anayā*, "with her (the old woman)", and *anaya*, "unlawfully, as contraband".
[^03-64]: *Avicāra*, "without judgement" and "not a judge".
[^03-65]: *Are yaśodā-mātar*: the same reading in M. Guha; he translates "son of Yashoda", i.e. he understands it as the vocative of a bahuvrihi, "He whose mother is Yashoda". Further on he has *mā taralo bhava*, "do not be flighty" (corrupted in the electronic text to *tarao*).
[^03-66]: A dialogue of double meanings (*vakrokti*): Madhava is a name of Krishna and the name of a spring month (Vaishakha); Janardana is "He who stirs people" (a name of Vishnu) and "robber of people" (one who lives in the forest is a bandit); Govardhana is the hill lifted by Krishna, while *vardhana* from the root *vardh*, "to cut", gives "the slaying of a cow"; "the slayer of the bull" is Krishna, who slew the bull-demon Arishta. Lines 3–4 are read according to the Kāvyamālā.
[^03-67]: According to the Kāvyamālā: *katiṣu*, "to some", and *hantobhayatve*, "but here, when they are both". Cf. the theory of rasa: rasa is experienced by the spectator (*sāmājika*), not by the actor.
[^03-68]: The five "great sins" (*mahāpātaka*): killing a brahmin, drinking wine, stealing gold, intercourse with the wife of one's guru, and association with such sinners. Each is turned upside down: *dvija-rāja*, "king of brahmins" and the moon; *madirā*, "wine" and "intoxicating (glance)"; *kāñcana-hāriṇī*, "stealing gold" and "taking away (the glory) of gold"; *gurv-aṅganā*, "the wife of a guru" and "a broad-hipped woman".
[^03-69]: According to the Kāvyamālā: *sāvaśeṣa eva*, "left unfinished"; the electronic text has *saviśeṣa*.
[^03-70]: A foreshadowing of the next act, Mahaprabhu's acceptance of sannyasa.

---

# Act 4. The Acceptance of Sannyasa (Sannyāsa-parigrahaḥ)

*Shachi is anxious about her son's inclination to sannyasa; Advaita comes from Shantipura to the sankirtana at Shrivasa's; the Lord secretly leaves for Katva and accepts sannyasa from Keshava Bharati.*

*(Enter the lady Shachi, followed by the wife of Acharya-ratna.)*

**Shachi.** Sister! Why does Sri Vishvambhara have such reverence for sannyasis? For a sannyasi's inclinations are of quite another kind.[^04-1]

**Sister.** And how did you learn of His reverence?

**Shachi.** Because that day, full of faith, He asked me for alms for a certain sannyasi named Keshava Bharati. And He Himself showed him great devotion and attachment.

**Sister.** Evidently he too is a devotee.

**Shachi.** My heart trembles at the very name "sannyasi". His elder brother has already taught me that lesson.[^04-2] That is why I shall ask Him Himself about this matter.

**Sister.** That is right.

**Shachi.** Then find out where He is, my sandal tree that gladdens the heart.

**Sister** *(looking ahead)*. Lady! Look, look — here is your son rising like the full moon in the east.

*(Shachi gazes tenderly. Then enters the Lord Vishvambhara.)*

**Vishvambhara** *(joining His palms)*. Mother, I bow to you.

**Shachi** *(saying "Live long!", she smells the crown of His head)*. My son! This is the wife of Acharya-ratna; bow to her.

*(The Lord does so. She shrinks back in embarrassment.)*

**Shachi.** My son! I want to ask You something.

**The Lord.** Command Me.

**Shachi.** My little son! Why do You have such reverence for sannyasis that that day You showed such devotion to Keshava Bharati?

**The Lord.** Mother! Why, they are the greatest devotees of Bhagavan.

**Shachi.** Tell the truth: are You not intending to take sannyasa?

**The Lord** *(laughing)*. Mother! Whence this delusion of yours? Can such a thing be?

**Shachi.** Child, the book that that very elder brother of Yours gave You — I threw it into the hearth while cooking and burnt it.

**The Lord.** What book? And why did you burn it?

**Shachi.** Vishvarupa said to me: "Mother! When Vishvambhara becomes learned, give Him this book." I kept it only until he himself went off wandering. And when he had gone, I burnt it — I was afraid that You, getting that book, would also go off into sannyasa.

**The Lord** *(saddened for a moment, then laughing)*. Mother! Though you yourself are knowledge embodied, still, out of love for your son, you did this, yielding to the play of ignorance.[^04-3]

**Shachi.** My son! Do not count this as my fault.

**The Lord.** What fault can a mother have towards her sons? But if there is a fault on My part, be merciful: may my mother's venerable feet surely forgive it.

**Shachi.** Child! Never shall I count any fault against You — You simply have none.

**The Lord.** Mother! For a few days I shall have to go somewhere. Do not grieve over it in your heart.

**Shachi.** Where must You go?

**The Lord.** Mother! To seek that by which you and all our kinsfolk will always be happy.[^04-4]

**Shachi.** Child! Act so that I may have no sorrow.

**The Lord.** Mother!

> **1.** Sri Krishna is your protector,[^04-5] your father, mother and son,
> your kinsman and wealth, your friend who grants eternal happiness, and your deity.
> Always, day and night, remembering in your heart Him with whom your bond is eternal,
> you have attained fullness; know, then: now the whole circle of the quarters is full of happiness for you.

**Shachi.** Child! You are everything to me; by Your mercy I have no sorrow. But act so that I may always see You.

**The Lord.** Always look upon Krishna. He alone will destroy all your sorrows.

**Shachi.** So be it. You are my Krishna. So get up: it is already midday. You too perform Your bathing, worship and the rest, and I shall go and cook. Sister, you too go home: for you too the time has come to prepare food for the service of Bhagavan.

*(Exeunt all. Then enters Advaita.)*

**Advaita.** One whose senses are all helplessly subjected to possession by a spirit, and one who has lost his steadiness through the entry of Bhagavan into him, show one and the same state. For how I danced that day when Bhagavan entered into me, I hear only now from people's lips — and I both believe and doubt. So the power of this Lord Vishvambhara is in no way restricted. But in truth it is simply the play of Bhagavan, skilled in the endless series of performances in which He moulds and breaks the pots of the universal eggs — myriads upon myriads — and who with the nectar of the lovely fame of His deeds has washed away the bitter impurity of the darkness lurking in the wells of the hearts of the people of the world.[^04-6] It truly cannot be measured by any means of knowledge — perception, inference, comparison, verbal testimony, presumption, tradition and the rest — without the special knowledge born of His mercy alone. Therefore that dancing play of the other day, the cause of a transcendental wonder, though it took place in me, did not become accessible to my experience — it was performed by Him. Therefore:

> **2.** Some will be bewildered, others will dispute with them,
> but certain knowers of the connection of events will understand: "This is one of the secrets."

*(Looking up.)* Oh, here is the divine sun in its crown of rays bending down to kiss the summit of the Western Mountain. For:

> **3.** "You are mine in name only, but in fact a resting place for all the planets"[^04-7] —
> so her husband accused her; and, to prove her fidelity,
> the Western Quarter, the wife of Varuna, as if holds red-hot iron
> in the guise of the orb of the evening sun.

Or else:

> **4.** From the West, whose mind has lost itself in the bliss of union with Evening,
> the garment of red clouds has slipped from her hips —
> and the sun, the jewel of the day, which had taken the form of the chief ruby of her girdle,
> has in its turn begun to fall.[^04-8]

So the weariness of the road has passed; now, having performed the evening worship, I must see the most beautiful of all who are worth seeing — the Lord Vishvambhara. *(Prepares to do so.)*

**Behind the scenes.** Ah, Advaita went away saying that, after visiting his own town, he would return at once — why has he not yet come?

**Advaita** *(acting as if listening)*. Oh, seeing that I delay, the Lord Himself reproaches me. I had better go quickly. *(Walks about the stage.)*

**Shrirama**[^04-9] *(entering, tossing aside the curtain)*. O Advaita! The Lord, Bhagavan, commands: "I am going from here to the house of Shrivasa; you too go there."

**Advaita.** As the Lord commands. *(Walks about the stage with him and, looking ahead.)* Here is the house of Shrivasa; let me enter. *(Acting entering and looking to the east.)* Oh, how beautiful:

> **5.** Gladdening the eyes of the people of the whole world, with cool rays for feet,
> pouring forth the nectar of love, making the night-lilies — the joy of the earth[^04-10] — open,
> both the moon and the moon Vishvambhara are rising.

**Vishvambhara** *(rising to meet him)*. Welcome, oh, welcome!

**Advaita.** It is a blessing to me — the sight of Your beautiful moon-face.

**The Lord** *(bowing respectfully and embracing him)*. Venerable sir, sit here.

**Advaita.** As You command. *(All act sitting down comfortably.)*

**The Lord** *(to Advaita)*. We have all already eaten and drunk. Only you are tired from the road and hungry, so there must be no delay. Shrivasa, you are the master of the house: show them hospitality.[^04-11]

**Advaita.** Do not worry about that: we, like you, have already fulfilled all the day's duties.

**The Lord** *(joyfully)*. Then now, in the courtyard of Shrivasa's house, washed with the silver water of the moonbeams, accept the auspicious festival of the sankirtana of Bhagavan.

**All** *(joyfully)*. Lord! Rise up Yourself too.

**The Lord.** Here I come. *(All go out to the place of sankirtana.)*

**Gangadasa** *(entering)*. They say that the venerable Advaita has arrived from Shantipura. I do not know whether he is staying in the house of the Lord Vishvambhara or in the house of Shrivasa; I must find out how matters stand. *(Takes a few steps. Behind the scenes, the noise of sankirtana.)*

**Gangadasa** *(listening)*. Oh, I hear near the house of Shrivasa the roar of sankirtana, born of joy and gladdening all the devotees who have gathered there in time.[^04-12] So Advaita must be there too. Let me stand here and watch. *(Gazes.)* Oh, all of them, singing, are making the Lord Vishvambhara dance — and are dancing themselves. Therefore I think this:

> **6.** Although the heavy burden of the earth, made up of the hosts of invincible Daityas,
> was removed by Bhagavan Himself,
> now the Lord, dancing with His devotees, shakes off from her the remnant of her weariness
> with the heavy blows of His feet, wild in the dance.[^04-13]

*(Gazing again.)* Oh!

> **7.** Is this not the supreme bliss that has taken form? Is it not love itself that has taken a body?
> Faith embodied, or compassion itself, appearing on earth in its own form?
> Sweetness that has taken a body? Or ninefold bhakti come together in one body? —
> It is Vakreshvara dancing, a festival of bliss equal in rapture to the Lord Himself.

*(Again behind the scenes, a united thunder of bliss and the roar of cries of "Jaya! Jaya!")*

**Gangadasa** *(gazing)*. Oh, a great marvel!

> **8.** When Vakreshvara dances, Gaurachandra sings loudly, clapping His hands;
> when Vakreshvara sings, Gaurachandra dances — and their joy is one.

*(Again behind the scenes, the same roar of cries of "Jaya! Jaya!" and jubilant cries of "ulu-ulu".[^04-14])*

**Gangadasa** *(gazing for a long time)*. Oh, now the Lord Vishvambhara Himself has broken into a dance. For:

> **9.** With deep peals of roaring He turns His people into peacocks,[^04-15]
> with streams of tears He ceaselessly makes the world a rainy day,
> with floods of radiance He girds all the quarters with lightning —
> gladdening the universe, there dances before me the cloud Vishvambhara.[^04-16]

And further:

> **10.** In all the quarters and intermediate quarters scattering with His gaze a garland of lotuses,
> with the tears of His eyes pouring honey into them,
> with His brows a swarm of bees —
> in a dance whirling like a wheel, Gaurachandra is victorious here.[^04-17]

And further:

> **11.** Victorious be the wheel-dance of the Lord Vishvambhara —
> who with the blows of His feet causes a flood of bliss in the city of the serpents,
> who with the waving of His arms seems skilled in the tandava in the city of the gods,
> who seems to turn the circle of the quarters with whirling rods of radiance.[^04-18]

*(Gazing again.)* Oh! Next the venerable Advaita has broken into a dance.

> **12.** Shrivasa with his three brothers — Rama and the others, with the loveliest voices —
> sings sweetly; the Lord Himself sings too, and ardent Vakreshvara;
> and Advaita — with anklets, armlets, necklace, bracelets, a girdle of little bells and the rest —
> like the bliss of service embodied, dances and dances.

> **13.** His head is beautiful with an elegant large turban, in both ears
> pearl earrings sway in the tandava, on his chest a splendid golden garland swings,
> at his feet anklets jingle, the hair on his body stands on end, his face is bathed in tears —
> and now magnanimous Nityananda is dancing in great rapture.

*(Looking at the sky.)* Oh, only one watch of the night is left. No wonder my eyes are closing: the lady Drowsiness has overcome me. Let me sleep here a little. *(Acting sleep, he speaks in his sleep.)* O Lord Vishvambhara, where are You, where are You? *(Having said this in his sleep and waking again.)* Oh, what an evil dream I have had! *(Meditating for a moment in his heart on the Lord's feet, he again looks towards the back of the stage.)* Oh, no one is to be seen. It seems the Lord and all the others, at the end of the sankirtana, have gone off to sleep here and there. So be it — I too shall go home. *(Taking a few steps.)* Oh, the night has already dawned! *(Looks to the east.)*

> **14.** Having only just stepped over the crest of the rampart of the Eastern Mountain,
> holding on to the edge of the sky-garment of the eastern quarter —
> though he still clumsily stretches out his ray-feet —
> look: the child sun is rising, obedient to time.[^04-19]

*(Walking a few steps and looking ahead.)* Oh, who is this hurrying this way, as if wanting to ask something?

*(Enter a certain man, alarmed.)*

**Man.** Hey, Gangadasa! Is the Lord Vishvambhara in your garden?

**Gangadasa** *(joyfully)*. Oh, what good fortune for me! He whom I was going to see has come to my garden Himself!

**Man.** No, no, I am asking: has He come to your garden?

**Gangadasa** *(dejected)*. Why do you ask so?

**Man.** On other days He comes home in the morning and performs His duties. But today He has not come, and so the lady Shachi has sent me to look for Him. *(Having said this, he goes off to look elsewhere. Again, from another side, an alarmed man hurriedly enters, asks in the same way and goes out. And again from another side, and again from another, and again from another — they ask and ask in the same way and go out.)*

**Gangadasa** *(dejected)*. Oh! It seems the evil dream has come true. What am I to do? I shall go to where Advaita and the others are. *(Takes a few steps.)*

*(Then enter Advaita and the others, despondent, acting deep thought.)*

**Advaita.** Shrivasa, what is this?

> **15.** We thought the Lord had gone home in the morning,
> and His mother thought He had stayed in the house of Shrivasa or with others —
> so we were all deceived, suspecting nothing. How could we know
> that today this thunderbolt would suddenly strike?

**Shrivasa.** Of all who were sent to look for Him, not one has returned.

**Advaita.** They will return when they have found and seen Him. But no one has learned anything — how can that be?

> **16.** Who in this village could hide Him who reveals Himself?
> And could He hide Himself?
> People cannot conceal the sun with its blazing rays;
> how, then, will it hide itself in the sky by day?

**Shrivasa** *(looking ahead)*. Here comes Gangadasa. We must question him.

**Gangadasa** *(approaching)*. O venerable ones, O greatly fortunate ones! What is this sudden commotion?

**All.** Oh! He too is searching for Him with all his might — since he is asking us himself.

**Advaita** *(with tears)*.

> **17.** O Lord Vishvambhara, O treasury of virtues, O ocean of love,
> O Bhagavan, who descended to raise the wretched, O wish-fulfilling gem of the devotees!
> Blinding our eyes, plunging the quarters into pitch darkness,
> emptying the minds of all living beings — for what fault are You leaving us?

**Murari.** O venerable Advaita! You are inconceivably deep — how can you lament so without having found anything out yet? From such remorse of people like you, poor lady Shachi will waste away all the more.

**Shrivasa.** Murari speaks the truth. For:

> **18.** Alas, He is her only son, her only eye,
> the only thing in which she feels her happiness;
> though she is His mother, she sees in Him her guru and her god,
> and without Him she will not live a moment.

Therefore now only one thing befits us — to preserve her life. So, O Gangadasa, she trusts your words: speak to her so that she does not part with her life.

**Gangadasa.** As you command. *(Goes out.)*

**Gadadhara** *(with compassion)*. O Master!

> **19.** One watch has passed, ah, two watches have passed, alas, these watches too
> have passed; woe, and the day too has almost passed;
> little by little, alas, ah, together with the breath of life the noose of hope is breaking —
> and still no news of You has stepped onto the road of our ears.

*(Faints.)*

**Vakreshvara.**

> **20.** "Will You really go away and leave us?" — O ocean of mercy, why, last night
> You showed us so many mercies, juicy with the abundant radiance of love!
> What then is this mercy of Yours — and what is this great neglect?
> Alas, O lord of my heart, O Master — this duality of Yours is beyond the world![^04-20]

*(Faints.)*

**Murari** *(in agitation)*.

> **21.** The steadiness one puts on outwardly
> is undermined by the weight of tears hidden within —
> so a swollen stream of water washes away a dam of sand,
> however often one builds it up again.

*(Sobbing and sobbing, he falls to the ground weeping.)*

**Shrivasa** *(looking at him)*. Oh, even he, so deep, has become irrepressibly tremulous with love. So it is:

> **22.** A stream of water remains calm only
> until it breaks the dam;
> but when the dam is broken, it is irresistible
> and able to flood everything.

O Lord Vishvambhara! Where are You, where are You?

> **23.** Before, I was dead — why, alas, was I brought back to life?
> Having revived me, are You now killing me again?
> Your cruel play, O Almighty, is beyond the grasp of the mind;
> truly the Lord has nothing but childish games.[^04-21]

*(Weeps.)*

**Mukunda.**

> **24.** Will our eyes no longer see Your face?
> Will our ears no longer hear Your speech?
> O lord of our life, Bhagavan — what use to us, abandoned by You,
> is this tormenting, accursed life?

**Jagadananda** *(with tears)*.

> **25.** We never thought that such as we, deprived of nearness to Your lotus feet,
> would live even a moment;
> that is why we are ashamed, O beloved, O Lord —
> for we go on living whole days without seeing You.

*(Loses consciousness.)*

**Damodara.** O lord of my life! Where are You, where are You?

> **26.** Breaths of life, why do you not depart? Cast off your torpor altogether:
> the lord of life is wandering now all alone.
> Go to His lotus feet and truly serve them —
> ah, let no stain fall on the family of those whose soul is love![^04-22]

*(Loses consciousness.)*

**Haridasa.** Oh, woe!

> **27.** If the breaths of life did not depart at once together with the lord of life,
> now they can no longer depart in a single moment:
> ceaselessly they endure the bitterness of myriads of reproaches;
> they themselves do not weaken, but wear out the one who bears them.[^04-23]

So be it — let me think a little.

> **28.** If that Lord does not come onto the road of my eyes,
> if in mercy He does not cast a glance at one such as I,
> then you, breaths of life, hard as the diamond thunderbolt, were you a thousand,
> I shall cast off in an instant like a straw, in my longing to reach His feet.

*(Collecting himself, he acts deep thought.)*

**Vidyanidhi**.[^04-24]

> **29.** O love, obeisance to you — for nowhere are you born
> without pretence! Alas, alas, what shall I say?
> Even towards Him whose mercy is without pretence you are pretended —
> otherwise how could I still remain bound to life?

*(Falls to the ground and, losing control of himself, sobs.)*

**Murari** *(collecting himself)*. Hey, venerable ones! Consider this: has the Lord gone somewhere alone, or has someone else gone with Him?

**Advaita.** How can that be determined?

> **30.** No one on the road saw the moon Gaura departing:
> like a mass of lightning, He suddenly vanished from sight.[^04-25]

**Murari.** There is a way to determine it.

**All.** What is it?

**Murari.** Let us check among ourselves: who is not here?

**All.** Well said. *(They confer among themselves.)*

**Murari.** Oh, I understand!

**All.** What is it?

**Murari.** Magnanimous Nityananda and Acharya-ratna are not here.

**All.** What does that signify?

**Murari.** If they were here, in such a calamity they would surely have come here.

**All** *(somewhat reassured)*. Ah, whatever becomes of us — if He is not alone, it is already a little easier for us.

**Advaita.** Mukunda! Console His mother with this news. Say this: "Mother, do not worry about Him. The Lord has gone somewhere with Nityananda and Acharya-ratna on a special errand and will soon return."

**Mukunda.** As you command. *(Goes out.)*

**Advaita.** Ho, favourites of the Lord Vishvambhara! Some steadiness seems to have come into my heart, since these two faithful ones are with Him. As long as they are with Him, the Lord, though independent, will not act wilfully. But, alas, what is His purpose, for which He has conceived this?

> **31.** If it is a pilgrimage, why hide?
> We could all have gone with Him.
> And if it is a desire arising from special love for those two,
> it could quite well have been fulfilled here.

*(All, after reflecting a moment, stand silent.)*

**Behind the scenes.** Ah, alas, woe!

> **32.** Woe, alas, oh, three days have passed, and they have not heard even a word;
> are they alive — or already dead? Or have they found refuge in a swoon?
> If they are still alive — having seen their beloved Lord like this,[^04-26] O fate,
> how shall I, returning, appear before them?

So better to remain here and try to part with the body.

**All** *(acting as if listening)*. Oh, it sounds like the voice of Acharya-ratna. For tears and sobbing one cannot make it out properly. Let us listen attentively. *(All act attention.)*

**Again behind the scenes.** Ah, ah, why did I, wretch, not go with Him? Or rather:

> **33.** Shiva, Shiva! The Lord cannot be overcome by force:
> the Lord Himself places His will in the hearts of His own.
> The sun displays its radiance in the sunstone —
> and the stone cannot prevent it, though it burns the stone itself.[^04-27]

**All** *(having listened)*. It really is Acharya-ratna. It seems he has come back after parting from the Lord — for he says: "Shiva, Shiva! The Lord cannot be overcome by force." Ah, so somehow we have obtained a seed of consolation — but one as if roasted by the forest fire of evil fate.[^04-28]

**Murari.** I think this: Nityananda is with Him, and this one seems to have been sent here on some other errand.

**Advaita.** What errand could He have here? He needs no money, to send for it; nor has He such attachment to His mother as to send him to console her; nor have we had such good fortune that He should send him to bring us.[^04-29] Enough of these doubts! Only I do not know what further fruit the poison tree of evil fate has borne for such as I. *(Stands absorbed in thought.)*

**Again behind the scenes.** Ah, woe! I am truly wretched.

> **34.** Why did I not follow Him, following and following? Alas, ah, having seen
> Him in such a form, why were my eyes not burnt with pain?
> When the Lord said "Go!", why did life not leave me?
> Ah, ah, Vishvambhara, I am deceived by that maya of Yours!

**All** *(having listened, turning towards the back of the stage)*. Ho! We are all eagerly waiting to see your face — delay no longer.[^04-30] *(They stand watching for him.)*

*(Then enters Acharya-ratna.)*

**Acharya-ratna.**

> **35.** Where are those glossy dark curling locks? And where is this rite of shaving the head?[^04-31]
> Where the weight of the hips in fine clothing? And where — Shiva, Shiva! — this loincloth?

*(Pausing a moment and reflecting.)*

> But this is only what appears to the onlookers; in reality it is not so:
> for all things shine forth in consciousness (*cit*), which is the support of all.

**All** *(approaching, eagerly)*. Acharya! Speak, speak, where is the Lord?

**Acharya-ratna.** Ah, ah, what can this wretch tell?

**Advaita.** Speak, what has happened?

**Acharya-ratna** *(in his ear)*. This.

**Advaita.** Ah, can such a matter be covered with the palm of one's hand? Say it plainly, let everyone hear.

**Acharya-ratna** *(aloud, with tears)*.

> **36.** All those skilful sports of kirtana and dance — alas, woe! — have come to an end;
> all those words, sweet with the play of love and laughter, remain only in our hearts;
> that love and that compassion — Shiva, Shiva! — have become a mere memory:
> by Your sannyasa, O Lord, we have been deprived of all we had.

*(All, hearing it, act fainting.)*

**Gangadasa** *(entering hurriedly)*. Acharya! The Lord's mother asks: "Tell me, is the blessed Lord well?"

*(The Acharya stands, his throat choked with tears.)*

**Advaita.** Tell her in my name:

> **37.** Rama's life in the forest, Krishna's departure to Mathura,
> and this one's rite of sannyasa — three lots that three mothers were destined to bear.[^04-32]

**Gangadasa.** Ah, she herself guessed it long ago. And she said this:

> **38.** "What are you hiding? Why, He has gone the way of His elder brother.
> For those whose deeds transcend the world, harshness and compassion are equal."

**Advaita.** So it is — she is steadfast. Otherwise would she have had such a son? *(Pausing a moment and reflecting.)* So it is:

> **39.** "Maker of sannyasa, Peace, the Tranquil, Steadfastness, the Supreme Refuge of the world" —
> these names the Lord has now made true.[^04-33]

And further:

> **40.** In this Bhagavan alone has the great saying come true in the direct sense:
> it applies to Him in its primary meaning, and an interpretation that discards its own sense is not needed here.[^04-34]

Acharya! Tell everything from the very beginning.

**Acharya-ratna.** Ah, it is only for this that I have stayed alive. Well, at the end of the night, just when the dancing had ended, He took me by the hand and walked a few steps; seeing Nityananda ahead, He said: "You come too," took him along, crossed the heavenly river[^04-35] and went on. I said: "Lord! Tell me, where are You going alone?" Not listening, He went on in silence; the two of us followed Him step by step, and, coming to a village called Katona,[^04-36] He approached Keshava Bharati, the king of renunciants. Then the two of us thought to ourselves: "The Lord wishes to accept the fourth ashrama." But even thinking so, we, overpowered by the Lord's might, could not utter a word. The next day He said to me: "Acharya-ratna! Perform the preliminary rites for this ceremony." I asked the Lord: "Lord, what ceremony?" Then the Lord said: "I must do this." At that I, not knowing what to do, was struck dumb, answered nothing and only wept. And then, without my taking part, the whole ceremony was performed according to the rules. What happened afterwards cannot be told in words.

**All** *(having heard, despondently)*. O Lord! How could You decide such a thing? Or is it simply the season when the fruits ripen on the tree of grief for such as we — why reproach the Lord's feet? Ah, ah, even remembered, it cuts our hearts. How could you, Acharya, bear to see it? *(They act agitation.)*

**Advaita.** What name befitting that ashrama did the Lord receive?

**Acharya-ratna.** "Sri Krishna Chaitanya."

**Advaita** *(in amazement)*. Oh, that is just what befits Him!

> **41.** Chaitanya (consciousness) is the essence of Krishna; He is named "Krishna Chaitanya";
> that is why the meaning of the great saying bears fruit here.[^04-37]

For Keshava Bharati is the Shruti herself, since she is the speech (*bhāratī*) of Keshava, as it is said: "…which I first spoke to Brahma, in which is the dharma whose essence is Me."[^04-38] So what Keshava Bharati taught is what the Shruti teaches. Speak, then, Acharya, speak:

> **42.** **Advaita.** Is He, Bhagavan, there, or in some other place?
> **Acharya-ratna.** At once the Lord,
> taking on the form of a staff-bearing ascetic,[^04-39] set out on His way.
> **Advaita.** And said nothing to you?
> **Acharya-ratna.** Blinded by love, stumbling, His chest bathed in streams of tears,
> He does not remember even Himself — ah, alas, what could He have said to me?

**Advaita.** And why did you not follow Him?

**Acharya-ratna.**

> **43.** Nityananda said this: "I shall follow the Lord,
> and then, leading Him from road to road by various devices,
> I shall bring Him to Advaita's house too. But you go, take this news
> to those who suffer, and give comfort to Advaita and the others."

**Advaita.** Blessed are you, Nityananda, blessed! You have conquered by your guileless friendship. Come, let us console the lady Shachi with this news and ourselves act as is fitting.

*(Exeunt all.)*

*Thus ends the fourth act of the* Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya, *called "The Acceptance of Sannyasa".*

[^04-1]: *Vijātīya-vāsana*, literally "with impressions (inclinations) of another kind", i.e. alien to family life.
[^04-2]: Prakrit *assa aggaeṇāhaṁ paḍhāidā* (*chāyā* *asyāgrajenāhaṁ pāṭhitā*), "I have been taught by His elder brother": an allusion to Vishvarupa, Vishvambhara's elder brother, who left for sannyasa. *Tassa ṭhāṇe pucchissaṁ*, "I shall ask Him": so M. Guha's Bengali translation understands it ("I shall speak with Him about this"); and indeed Shachi at once asks her son about it.
[^04-3]: The Kāvyamālā has *saṁvid eva bhavatī* ("you are consciousness itself") and *ajñāna-vikasitam* ("a manifestation of ignorance") instead of *ajñāna-vilasitam*.
[^04-4]: In the Kāvyamālā there is a further exchange here. Shachi: "But that is You Yourself." The Lord: "So be it, but still I shall strive for that from which I too shall gain the greatest glory."
[^04-5]: The reading *paripālakaḥ*, "protector, guardian", is adopted: so in M. Guha, in the edition of J. Vidyasagara (commentary: *rakṣakaḥ*, "defender") and in Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation. The electronic text has *paripākaḥ* ("ripened fruit"), the Kāvyamālā OCR the corrupt *pariṣāṅkas*.
[^04-6]: A play of assonances: *-ghaṭa-ghaṭana-vighaṭana-nāṭaka-paripāṭī-pāṭavasya … -ghaṭamāna-tamaḥ-kāṭavasya*. *Kāṭava*, "bitterness, impurity" (*kaṭutva*, *mālinya*): so in the commentaries of M. Guha and J. Vidyasagara — by the nectar of His fame the impurity of darkness in the hearts is washed away (*dhāvita*); so too Guha's Bengali translation ("the dark impurity of hearts is washed away").
[^04-7]: *Graha*, "planet" and "seizer, one who takes possession": the husband accuses the West of being the haunt of all lovers. The western quarter is *vāruṇī dik* (the quarter of Varuna; *vāruṇī* is also "an intoxicating drink"). The red-hot iron (*paritaptam ayaḥ*) is the image of an ordeal (*divya*): it does not burn the innocent.
[^04-8]: *Sāyāhna* ("evening", masculine) is represented as the lover of the Western Quarter (feminine). There is a play on words: the sun is called *dinamaṇi*, "the jewel of the day" (Kāvyamālā *dinapati*, "lord of the day"); the evening glow is the red garment slipped from her hips.
[^04-9]: So in the Gaudiya Grantha Mandira edition (Shrirama, Shrivasa's brother). In the Kāvyamālā the speech is spoken by Shrivasa himself, and before verse 5 there is the stage direction "Then enters the Lord Vishvambhara, accompanied by Shrivasa and the others".
[^04-10]: *Pāda*, "ray" and "foot"; *kaumuda*, "night-lilies" (*kumuda*) and, by the etymology *ku-mud*, "joy of the earth".
[^04-11]: In the Kāvyamālā the words "You are the master of the house… show them hospitality" are given to Shrivasa himself (apparently an error in the distribution of speeches).
[^04-12]: A play of repetitions (*yamaka*): *samayā samayāsādita-… pramodī-pramodīrṇa-…*, "near… who gathered in time…, gladdening… born of joy…".
[^04-13]: An allusion to the descent of Krishna, who removed from the earth the burden of the demon-kings; here the blows of the feet in the dance are like a massage removing the remnant of her fatigue.
[^04-14]: *Ulu-nināda*, the jubilant cries of "ulu-ulu" that the women of Bengal raise at auspicious moments.
[^04-15]: The Kāvyamālā reading *barhiṇayati* ("makes peacocks" — peacocks dance at the thunder of clouds) is followed; the GGM edition has *bahir nayati* ("leads outside").
[^04-16]: *Viśvambhara*, "the one who sustains (nourishes) the universe": this is also an epithet of the cloud that nourishes the world with rain.
[^04-17]: The Kāvyamālā reading: *dṛśā … cakra-bhrami-naṭane*; corrupt in GGM (*dṛśo … cakraṁ bhrami naṭane*). The gaze is a garland of lotuses, the tears honey, the brows bees.
[^04-18]: "The city of the serpents" is the underworld of the Nagas; "the city of the gods" the heaven of Indra; *tāṇḍava*, the wild dance (of Shiva). "Rods of radiance" are the upraised arms.
[^04-19]: *Pāda*, "ray" and "foot"; *bāla*, "young (morning)" and "child": the infant sun cannot yet walk.
[^04-20]: The sense: the generous mercies of the past night did not let them even think of His departure; the Lord's mercy and neglect are at the same time inconceivable.

[^04-21]: Literally "dead before — how, alas, am I revived?". So M. Guha's Bengali translation ("before I had died — why did You revive me?") and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation ("when I was going to my death before, why did You save me?") understand it: the Lord once restored the speaker to life, and now, by departing, kills him again. *Kevala-bāla-līla*, "whose games are nothing but childish sport"; both translations render the last line as a bitter question.
[^04-22]: The Kāvyamālā readings: *sāmpratam eka eva* ("now alone") and *bhajadhvam addhā* ("truly serve"); corrupt in GGM (*eva eva*, *adhvā*).
[^04-23]: The Kāvyamālā reading *punar jhaṭiti naiva bhavanti gantum*; corrupt in GGM (*jhaṭitenaiva*).
[^04-24]: Vidyanidhi is Pundarika Vidyanidhi, a devotee from Chittagong, a close associate of the Lord.
[^04-25]: The Kāvyamālā reading *pathi kenāpi* ("by no one on the road"); GGM has *pathikenāpi* ("even by a traveller"), almost the same sense.
[^04-26]: "Like this", i.e. having already accepted sannyasa. The speaker is Acharya-ratna (Chandrashekhara), returning from Katva.
[^04-27]: The sunstone (*sūrya-kānta*) emits fire under the sun's rays. The Kāvyamālā reading *sva-kānta-maṇau mahaḥ* ("radiance in its own stone"); GGM has *matvā*.
[^04-28]: A roasted seed does not sprout: there is hope, but it is not destined to grow.
[^04-29]: The Kāvyamālā has *asmān lālayitum*, "to caress us"; GGM *ānayitum*, "to bring us".
[^04-30]: *Mātaḥ-paraṁ* in GGM is apparently a misprint for *ataḥ paraṁ* ("any longer, further").
[^04-31]: *Kva sa vidhiḥ*, literally "where is that rite". According to M. Guha's commentary this is the *muṇḍana-vidhiḥ*, the rite of shaving the head at initiation into sannyasa; his Bengali translation has: "where are those curls — and where is this shaven-headed form of a sannyasi". The line, like the next one, is built on the contrast of former and present.
[^04-32]: Kaushalya, the mother of Rama; Yashoda, the mother of Krishna; Shachi.
[^04-33]: Names of Vishnu from the "Thousand Names" (*Mahābhārata*, Anushasana-parva): *sannyāsa-kṛc chamaḥ śānto niṣṭhā śāntiḥ parāyaṇam*.
[^04-34]: The great saying (*mahā-vākya*) of the Upanishads, for example "You are That" (*tat tvam asi*). The Advaitins interpret it through *jahad-ajahal-lakṣaṇā*, a figurative meaning in which part of the words' own sense is discarded and part retained. This is the Kāvyamālā reading; GGM has *jahat-svārtha-lakṣaṇā*, "with the discarding of the own sense". The irony: the Lord is God who has taken on the form of a sannyasi, and to Him the identification applies literally.
[^04-35]: *Suradhunī*, the Ganga.
[^04-36]: Katona (*kaṭoṅā*, Kāvyamālā *kāṭoyā*) is Katva (Katoya), where the Lord accepted sannyasa.
[^04-37]: *Caitanya*, "consciousness". The great saying (*prajñānaṁ brahma* and the like) identifies consciousness with Brahman; here it comes true literally: consciousness (Chaitanya) is Krishna Himself.
[^04-38]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.14.3. The name *Keśava-bhāratī*, "the speech of Keshava", is played upon.
[^04-39]: *Maskarin*, a mendicant ascetic with a bamboo staff, i.e. a sannyasi. Verse 42 is divided between Advaita and Acharya-ratna.

---

# Act 5. Pastimes in the Town of Advaita (Śrīmad-advaita-pura-vilāsa)

*Seized by ecstasy, the Lord wanders towards Vrindavana; Nityananda by a stratagem brings Him to Shantipura, to Advaita, where the people of Navadvipa, headed by mother Shachi, flock together.*

*(Enter Sri Krishna Chaitanya, followed by Nityananda.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.**

> **1.** Firmly fixed in this devotion to the Supreme Self,
> held by the great sages of the most ancient times,
> I shall cross over the darkness, whose far shore is hard to reach —
> by service to the feet of Mukunda alone.[^05-1]

*(Acts stumbling.)*

**Nityananda** *(to himself)*. Oh, a marvel!

> **2.** The nectar of love is of course the same, but, thickened on the fire of the grief of renunciation[^05-2]
> and as if stirred without pause, it is turning into a lump;
> in time it will, I fear, become as painful as an ulcer in the heart.

What am I, alone, to do? Well, let me think. *(Gazing again.)* Oh, a marvel!

> **3.** The dance is its waves, the loud roar of joyful cries of "hum!" its sound,
> sweat, stupor and other flashing feelings its strings of pearls:
> in this ocean of the Lord's bliss a deep current has arisen —
> and we do not know, ah, what its outcome will be!

Ah, He moves like a heap of flower-pollen driven by a strong wind! Even I, hurry as I may, cannot keep up with Him. All His senses have ceased to function: He goes now here, now there, but not at all towards any goal. Look:

> **4.** Road or roadlessness — nothing becomes an object of His gaze:
> what is high, what is low, where there is water, where forest.
> Like a wild elephant in rut He goes on and on,
> noticing neither what is ahead, nor what is behind, nor even Himself.

So it is:

> **5.** The self-satisfied[^05-3] attain a certain state in which the senses are without function;
> and those intoxicated with love are absorbed in the one form of Bhagavan alone.
> But if the Lord too abides in His own bliss, where then is the difference?
> Ah, I understand:
> in Bhagavan bliss is subject to Him, while the living being is subject to bliss.

What am I to do now? *(Pausing a moment.)*

> **6.** For three days now He has been without food, not even drinking water or milk — what to speak of the rest;
> a loincloth is all His clothing, and He follows only His absorption in His own joy;
> He goes on and on, noticing neither day nor night — what are we to do?
> O Gauranga, treasury of mercy, have mercy, be gracious to me who suffer!

*(Pausing a moment.)* But at this thought my heart grows a little calmer:

> **7.** This state of Mahaprabhu, helpless with bliss,
> has now become for us the healing medicine of life:
> leading Him along a road He cannot distinguish,
> I shall bring Him to the house of the lord Advaita.

*(Taking heart, he follows Him.)*

*(Behind the scenes, a united noise of voices: "Say 'Hari'! Say 'Hari'!")*

**Nityananda** *(looking ahead)*. Oh, here is a marvel: these cowherd boys, seeing the Lord, with curiosity and respect, with devotion, faith, joy and amazement, are loudly crying: "Say 'Hari'! Say 'Hari'!"

*(The Lord, by His old habit, hearing the sound of the name of Hari, as if slightly awakened from a blissful sleep, opens the lotuses of His eyes and looks in the direction from which the name of Hari comes.)*

**Nityananda** *(looking closely)*. Ah, how these cowherd boys have helped! Hearing their cry of "Hari", He, as if slightly awakened from a blissful sleep, is drawn along this very road, like one bitten by a snake who is drawn by the great mantra of a charmer.[^05-4]

**The Lord** *(approaching)*. Say it, say "Hari"! *(Repeats this again and again.)*

*(Enter cowherd boys; on every side they fall prostrate like rods and, clapping their hands, sing the sankirtana of the name of Hari. The Lord, listening eagerly, halts His march for a moment.)*

**Nityananda** *(joyfully)*.

> **8.** The madness caused by bliss is of different kinds: one produces restlessness,
> another a deep stupor, and yet another produces restlessness and stupor together,
> and at times it is like possession by an evil spirit.

And now the Lord's madness of bliss seems to have reached its last stage. Look:

> **9.** Opening His eyes, He looks, but makes nothing an object of His perception;
> like one half-deaf, He hears something but does not grasp the meaning.

**The Lord** *(touching their heads with His lotus hand)*. Ah, you have sung the Lord's name well, and I have obtained the fulfilment of My desires. Tell Me, then, which road leads to Vrindavana?

**Nityananda** *(joyfully)*. Here is my chance! *(Leads one of them out of the crowd.)* My boy, say: "Here is the road to Vrindavana."

**Boy.** As the master commands. *(Approaches.)* O master, here is the road to Vrindavana. *(He points out the desired road that Nityananda had pointed out to him.)*

*(The Lord, in blissful ecstasy, goes along that very road. The boys bow and go out.)*

**Nityananda.** Well, I am saved! Now my wish will be fulfilled: by this road one can reach Advaita's homestead. *(Follows Him along this road; having gone some distance — reflecting.)* Ah, why does He not recognise me? However, the ability to recognise others seems to be returning to Him little by little. Well, let me try my luck. *(Comes nearer.)*

**The Lord** *(again reciting "Firmly fixed in this devotion to the Supreme Self…" and so on)*. Ah, well sang the mendicant monk! Only by service to Mukunda shall I cross over the darkness, not by this devotion to the Supreme Self: one need only be fixed in it, but it is not the goal.[^05-5] So I shall go to Vrindavana and make service to Mukunda my service of the mind. *(Fixing His gaze on the sky.)* Ho, is it far to Vrindavana?

**Nityananda** *(approaching)*. Master, to Vrindavana is a day's journey.

**The Lord** *(as if between sleep and waking, with amazement)*. What? Ah, is it you, Shripada Nityananda?

**Nityananda.** Master, it is I. *(Unable to finish, he falls silent: tears choke his throat.)*

**The Lord.** Shripada, tell Me, where have you come from?

**Nityananda.** Hearing of the master's wish to go to Vrindavana, I, wishing to see it myself, set out and joined the Lord.

**The Lord.** Good, ah, good! Let us go together, then. *(Walks on joyfully.)*

**Nityananda.** Master, this way, this way! *(Having led Him some distance.)* Master, not far from here flows the divine Yamuna. It would be good to bathe in her.

**The Lord.** What, today I shall see the Yamuna?

**Nityananda.** Of course!

**The Lord** *(acting joy)*.[^05-6] Shripada, where is she, where is she?

**Nityananda.** This way, this way! *(Having led Him some distance, he comes to the Ganga.)* Master, here she is, here is the Yamuna!

**The Lord** *(bowing joyfully, He glorifies her)*.

> **10.** Vessel of the highest love for the Sun of consciousness and bliss, the eternal son of Nanda,[^05-7]
> whose body is liquid Brahman,
> cutter-away of sins,[^05-8] bestower of good on the world —
> may the daughter of Mitra purify our bodies![^05-9]

**Nityananda.** Master, immerse Yourself in her.

**The Lord.** As you please. *(Acts bathing.)*

**Nityananda** *(to himself)*. Ah, I am happy: a huge, maddened wild elephant has been tamed by a mere mantra![^05-10] But something of this business still remains — I shall arrange that too. *(Looking around, he calls someone.)*

*(Enter a certain man, who bows.)*

**Nityananda** *(aside, to him alone)*. Listen, here, not far off, on the other bank of the Ganga, is the town of the venerable Advaita. Go there quickly and report: "Nityananda is here nearby with a certain other sannyasi and is waiting for you. So make haste."

**Man.** I shall do so at once. *(Goes out hurriedly.)*

**Nityananda** *(to himself)*. Ah, today it is three days since I have touched even water. I too shall bathe. *(Does so.)*

*(Behind the scenes.)*

> **11.** Our lives, bound by His virtues as by ropes[^05-11] twisted double with the fetters of hope,
> though worn out in separation from Him, could not — alas! — depart.
> But now they have done us a service, by revealing His face —
> for when fate is favourable, even the unfavourable suddenly becomes favourable.[^05-12]

**Nityananda** *(listening from afar)*. Oh, it is the Acharya himself beginning to sing! So fate has arranged everything well. Now my heavy burden has become light. *(Looking at the Lord.)* Ah, woe!

> **12.** His body is drenched by streams of water; from shyness, being unaccustomed,
> He has not even taken off His loincloth and upper cloth to wring the water out of them;[^05-13]
> His head is covered with a heap of red lotus petals —
> I see the Lord, golden-fair, like the best of elephants coming out after bathing.

Let me see whether Advaita will come soon. *(Looks ahead.)*[^05-14]

*(Enter Advaita, acting impatience, surrounded on every side by a countless retinue.)*

**Advaita** *(looking ahead)*. Ah, here He is, here is the Lord!

> **13.** Although from afar, without His tuft of hair and in reddish garments, He seems another,
> yet by His radiance like that of a golden image and by the abundance of His beauty
> He appears to us the very same.

Ah, how beautiful!

> **14.** Look: this red garment makes that same body of the Lord,
> yellow as gold, like the beauty of a mango fruit —
> the essence of renunciation, golden-red,
> filled to the brim with great juice.[^05-15]

*(Hurriedly approaching and standing before the Lord, whose eyes are closed in meditation, he sobs aloud.)*

**The Lord** *(quickly opening His eyes)*. What? Is it magnanimous Advaita Acharya?

**Nityananda.** Master, so it is.

**The Lord** *(embracing him tightly)*. Tell Me, how did you learn that I am here? Or how did you, following Me, come to be in Vrindavana too? Or am I dreaming this?

**Advaita** *(with tears, to himself)*. Ah, the Lord is really convinced that He is in Vrindavana! *(Aloud.)* Master, this is no dream: it is I, the same wretched man. *(Acts falling down.)*

**The Lord** *(raising him in His embrace, with tears)*. So be it. O Advaita, you yourself are Vrindavana, for the Lord's lotus feet abide in you constantly. Tell Me, then, where have I come?

**Advaita.** Here is the divine Bhagirathi,[^05-16] and here, on the other bank, is my town.

**The Lord** *(acting the return to outward consciousness)*. Shripada, but you said that this was the Yamuna.

**Nityananda.** Whether the Yamuna flows in her or not — let the master know for Himself.[^05-17]

**The Lord.** Shripada by his play has made Me act a part.[^05-18]

*(Advaita again recites the verse "Our lives, bound…" (5.11), substituting at will the word "Your" for the words "His" and "that".)*[^05-19]

**Nityananda.** O Advaita, ever since He took up the staff, the staff has become a punishment[^05-20] for me: three days without food! But the Lord is satisfied merely by tasting His own bliss. So enough of lofty talk here.

**Advaita** *(taking from a servant's hands a new loincloth and upper cloth, he bathes the Lord again and, full of compassion, has Him put them on)*.

> **15.** Before, I clothed You in garments worthy of God,
> and now I clothe You in the garb worthy of a mendicant monk.
> Your beauty is the same, and the same the serenity of Your face —
> alas, alas, only to our eyes it is unbearable![^05-21]

My house is quite near. May the Lord's feet deign to adorn it.

**The Lord.** So that is why Shripada deceived Me!

**Advaita.** No one can deceive the Lord. However:

> **16.** "The Lord can be constrained by nature — but only by His own maya;
> being independent, He, like crystal, takes colour from contact with what is near" —
> so some of us say here. But in truth everything in the Lord — ah! —
> is the flashing of a play like a child's game.

In any case, You have not been deceived. And there is no fault of his in this, for he is Shripada: "*śrīpa*" is the one who bestows Shri, that is, Krishna; and "*śrīpāda*" is the one who takes Him away.[^05-22] That is just what he did. So let the venerable ones go ahead. Today let the Lord accept His first alms in my house.

**The Lord.** As you please. Show the way, then.

**Advaita.** This way, this way. *(Seats them in a boat.)*

**Nityananda** *(having listened)*. O Advaita, have you sent anyone to Navadvipa?

**Advaita.** Of course. Everyone has almost arrived.

**The Lord.** O Advaita, to come to your house is something unprecedented for us.

**Advaita.** How can I compare with the good fortune of Shrivasa, in whose house the Lord played every day!

**Nityananda.** O Advaita, now a great throng of people will begin. For news of the Lord spreads of itself, and all the more has the news spread that the Lord is going to Mathura. Now, when the blessed Lord is gracing your house,[^05-23] all the people — children, old men and young — will gather at once to see this ocean of compassion. So while they have not yet found out, let us enter your house unnoticed.

**Advaita.** Let us do so. *(Walking a few steps.)* O Lord, here, here is our house. Enter, then. *(All act entering.)*

*(Behind the scenes.)*

> **17.** That very Lord Vishvambhara, having deceived His mother
> and taken the form of an ascetic, wanted to go to Mathura,
> but Nityananda by a stratagem has brought Him to Shantipura —
> ho, come, ho, come and see Him!

**Nityananda** *(having listened and looked)*. Advaita, do you hear and see? Many thousands of the local people have suddenly gathered here. A little more — and there will be hundreds of thousands. So let doorkeepers stand at the doors.

*(Advaita, placing doorkeepers at every door and letting the Lord go first, acts entering together with Nityananda and goes out. Then enter people longing to see the Lord.)*

**Some.**

> **18.** Although that He is now in another form than the one in which He appeared to our eyes in Navadvipa
> does trouble the heart,
> yet our thirst — Shiva, Shiva! — only grows,
> for the true essence, which cannot be taken away, is equally sweet in its natural and in its altered state.

Let us find out now where the Lord is. *(They walk about.)*

**Others** *(from another side)*.

> **19.** The great sweetness that this Lord had in His former ashrama
> did not become a guest of our eyes;
> if we do not see it now either, then shame on our birth,
> shame on our body, ah, shame on our life, shame on our eyes!

**Yet others.** Hey, this way, this way! We have heard that the Lord has just entered the town of Advaita. Let us go in there. *(Approaching impatiently.)* Ah, these doorkeepers let no one in. Well, we must persuade them and give them something. *(They come nearer.)*

*(Enter the doorkeepers, canes in hand, standing at the gates.)*

**Doorkeeper.** Hey, people, wait a little. Since the Lord accepted the fourth ashrama He has eaten nothing. Today He will accept alms. So do not make a noise now. Sit down and wait: after the meal you will be able to see the Lord.

*(Behind the scenes.)* Master, this way, this way!

**Doorkeeper.** Let me have a look. *(Climbing onto a raised place and stretching his neck.)* Ah, the Lord has already accepted alms, for here He is:

> **20.** His body anointed with sandal paste, wearing a new red garment,
> a white garland shining on His chest, golden in lustre,
> He triumphs like a jewelled mountain[^05-24] that has taken on the beauty
> of the snows, of the evening glow and of the stream of the Ganga.

How will these eager people see Him? *(Gazing again.)* Ah, the lord Advaita has arranged things well: he has seated the Lord in the middle of a high pavilion. Now all will see Him without difficulty, and this pavilion has justified its name.[^05-25]

*(All the people impatiently cry in chorus: "Say 'Hari'!")*[^05-26]

*(Then enter the Lord, seated in the said pavilion, Advaita and the others.)*

**Advaita.**

> **21.** What play is this You have staged: You have accepted the fourth ashrama,
> which is so dear to the adherents of *advaita*?[^05-27]

**The Lord** *(laughing)*.

> Remember, O Advaita — are We not, alas, adherents of Advaita?
> For the whole difference between it and you lies only in form and gender.[^05-28]

**Advaita.** Is it fitting for the lord of speech to play with words?

**The Lord.** Let them understand it in essence:

> **22.** Without renouncing everything, worship of the Lord of life is impossible —
> that is why I have made the renunciation; what has talk of *advaita* to do with it?
> "This staff is a very strong punishment[^05-29] for the beast that is the mind" —
> with this thought alone I, without special deliberation,[^05-30] took up the staff.

**Advaita.** All this is merely a pretext. In reality it is only to reveal the meaning of such names as "Maker of sannyasa, the Restrained, the Tranquil, the Refuge of steadfastness and peace".[^05-31]

**Doorkeeper** *(looking over everyone in front)*. Ah, a wonder!

> **23.** Even in such an impenetrable crush, uneven with the jostling of the throngs of people,
> this Lord is seen without difficulty by all, though they are past counting:[^05-32]
> unhurried, glad to have found a little place, and comfortably settled,
> they gaze at Him to their heart's content, honoured with His glance full of mercy.

And these are no idle words, for:

> **24.** "Today the ocean of existence has been crossed, today the gates of Yama too are closed,
> the fruit of human birth is attained and all the feats of austerity are accomplished,
> for I have seen God, full of the sweetness of merciful glances" —
> so all, each one separately, proclaim, ah, their happiness.

*(Behind the scenes, noise on every side.)*

**People.** Ah, all the people of the Lord's birthplace are coming! Now there will be a great crush. Let us get out of here. *(Exeunt all.)*

*(Then enter all the people of Navadvipa.)*

**They.**

> **25.** Today blindness has left our eyes, today the quarters have become clear,
> today the dried-up creepers of the thirst for life are putting forth shoots,
> and into the ruined heart someone has placed consciousness (*caitanya*) —
> ah, so that is it: today for us the Moon Chaitanya will rise![^05-33]

*(They approach impatiently.)*

**Advaita** *(seeing them)*.

> **26.** Letting the Lord's mother go first, there have come
> these friends headed by Shrivasa Pandita,
> and others — children, youths, old men. What more is there to say?
> It is the same land arisen anew, it is the same time![^05-34]

*(The Lord hurriedly acts descending. The doorkeepers respectfully let the newcomers through. The Lord bows to His mother.)*

**Mother** *(with tears, trembling and with a faltering voice, in which fear, reverence, motherly tenderness and joy are mingled — in Sanskrit)*.[^05-35]

> **27.** Who are You — renunciation itself, or realisation,
> or bhakti, or the highest rasa that has taken a body?
> My son, I see in You only the infant at my breast.
> Now that I have found You, I shall never let You go again!

*(Impatiently embraces Him.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.**

> **28.** Divine mother of the universe, O mother! Can the creeper called motherly love
> bear[^05-36] any fruit higher and better than this?
> You, lady, love the whole world without self-interest —
> surely the Lord has given a body to Forbearance herself![^05-37]

*(He recites it again. His mother embraces Him again and bows. Advaita raises her and, himself leading her into the inner apartments, goes out. The Lord gladdens everyone, each in his own way, with embraces, touches, glances, questions and the rest.)*

**Advaita** *(hurriedly entering again)*. Ho, who is there? Serve everyone — children, old men, youths, down to the chandalas — with whatever they wish: lodging, food, drink and the rest.

**One of the servants** *(entering, tossing aside the curtain)*. Here I am, here, I shall do it all at once. *(Goes out.)*

**Advaita** *(approaching the Lord)*.

> **29.** I am the same, they are the same, You are the same, and their love is the same,
> and Your compassion is the very same;
> all is as before, the single source of happiness;
> only Your other form is the cause of our sorrow.

**The Lord.** Advaita, Advaita, not so!

> **30.** My body has been cast into the stream of dark nectar[^05-38]
> and is borne along swiftly by its high wave;
> whatever state it comes into — auspicious or inauspicious —
> each only kindles love in Me more and more.

Come, let us sit in private with those whom we have not seen for so long.

*(Exeunt all.)*

*Thus ends the fifth act of the* Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya, *called "Pastimes in the Town of Advaita".*

[^05-1]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.23.57 (11.23.58 in GGM), the words of the Avanti brahmin, a mendicant monk (bhikshu) who had taken up renunciation. Chaitanya repeats them, having just accepted sannyasa.

[^05-2]: *Nirveda* is both "despondency, grief" and "renunciation of the world"; *śṛta*, "boiled": the image of milk which, after long boiling and stirring, thickens into a lump (*piṇḍa*). Nityananda fears that the Lord's love, "boiled down" in the grief of sannyasa, will become a torment to Him.

[^05-3]: *Ātmārāma*, those who delight in the Atman (cf. *Bhāg.* 1.7.10). In the last line there is the play *ānanda-nighna*: Bhagavan is the one to whom bliss is subject (*nighnānanda*, so the Kāvyamālā; GGM has the misprint *nighānando*), while the jiva is itself subject to bliss.

[^05-4]: *Mahā-mantrākṛṣṭa iva phaṇi-daṣṭas*, literally "like one bitten by a snake, drawn by a great mantra"; cf. the belief that a charmer by his mantra makes the snake that has bitten return. The verb *anusarpati* ("creeps after") plays on the image of the snake. *Phaṇi-daṣṭa*, "bitten by a snake" (a man), not the snake itself: so M. Guha's commentary and Bengali translation (*phaṇinā daṣṭaḥ*) and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation explain it; the "great mantra" is here at once a charm and the cry of the name of Hari.

[^05-5]: A play on words: in verse 5.1 *samāsthāya* ("firmly fixed"); the Lord interprets: for *parātma-niṣṭhā* only *āsthā* (support, adherence) is needed, while the goal is service to Mukunda. GGM has the corrupt *mukundasyevaiva mānasī*; translated according to the Kāvyamālā, *mukunda-sevaiva mānasīkartavyā*.

[^05-6]: So according to the Kāvyamālā (*harṣaṁ nāṭayitvā*); GGM has *iyaṁ nāṭayitvā*, an obvious corruption.

[^05-7]: A play: *sadā nanda-sūnoḥ* can also be read *sadānanda-sūnoḥ*, "of the son of eternal bliss".

[^05-8]: GGM has *lavitrī* ("cutter, reaper"), the Kāvyamālā *khanitrī* ("digger, underminer").

[^05-9]: The Yamuna is the daughter of the Sun god (Mitra, Surya). In GGM this verse is wrongly numbered 11 (the number 10 is missing), and Advaita's verse 5.13 is missing altogether; here the numbering of verses 10–13 follows the Kāvyamālā; GGM's numbers 11–13 correspond to 10–12. The verse is quoted in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 3.28; there, as in GGM, *lavitrī*).

[^05-10]: *Mantra* is both a charm and "secret counsel, stratagem": the elephant has been tamed by Nityananda's device.

[^05-11]: *Guṇa* is both "virtue" and "thread, rope". The verse is sung behind the scenes by Advaita, approaching the bank.

[^05-12]: Following GGM, *diṣṭe hīṣṭe* (= *hi iṣṭe*); the Kāvyamālā has *diṣṭe dṛṣṭe*, "when fate is revealed".

[^05-13]: Translated according to the Kāvyamālā (*na nirgālya*); GGM has *nirgīlya* without the negative. The sannyasi, unaccustomed to a mere loincloth, is too shy to take it off to wring it out.

[^05-14]: This sentence, Advaita's entrance and verse 13 are found only in the Kāvyamālā; in GGM they have dropped out, and the next verse (5.14) is wrongly attributed there to Nityananda. Verse 13 is also in the edition of Jivananda Vidyasagara; its second quarter reads *dūrād asau ced anasāv ivekṣyate*, "if from afar He seems not Himself [but someone else]" (Jivananda's commentary: *anasau = itara*); Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understands the passage the same way.

[^05-15]: *Rasa* is both "juice" and aesthetic/loving experience: the Lord is the "ripe fruit" of renunciation, full of great rasa. The saffron garment of the sannyasi is compared to the red skin of a ripe mango.

[^05-16]: The Bhagirathi is the Ganga (the name of its western branch in Bengal). Shantipura lies on its left bank.

[^05-17]: An ambiguous answer: below Prayaga the Yamuna does flow in the waters of the Ganga, so Nityananda has not, formally, lied.

[^05-18]: *Nāṭya*, "acting, theatrical play": the Lord says He has been "played a trick on".

[^05-19]: So according to the Kāvyamālā (*tvac-chabdaṁ dattvā*: "inserting the word 'You'"), that is, "by Your virtues", "in separation from You", "Your face". GGM has *yac-chabde tac-chabde ca svacchandaṁ dattvā*, "freely changing the words 'which' and 'that'".

[^05-20]: A play on words: *daṇḍa* is both the sannyasi's "staff" and "punishment".

[^05-21]: *Viṣama* is both "unequal, incongruous" and "hard, unbearable": the beauty is the same, but the eyes of His friends cannot bear this change.

[^05-22]: A jesting etymology: *śriyaṁ prāti iti śrīpaḥ*, "the bestower (or 'filler') of wealth/Lakshmi", is Krishna; *śrīpaṁ ādadāti iti śrīpādaḥ*, "the one who takes the Shripa". The honorary title of a sannyasi, "Shripada", is interpreted as "the one who carries Krishna off".

[^05-23]: *Tad-bhavana-subhagaṁbhāvuka-bhāvuke bhagavati* (in M. Guha *bhavad-bhavana-…*), "when the Lord is inclined to bless your house"; *subhagaṁbhāvuka* in Guha's commentary means "inclined to bestow good fortune" (*saubhāgya-bhāvana-śīla*), and in his Bengali translation: "when your house is sanctified by the touch of the Lord's feet".

[^05-24]: That is, Mount Meru; the snow is the white garland, the evening glow the red garment, the stream of the Ganga the sandal paste (or the other way round).

[^05-25]: *Upakārikā*, "pavilion, marquee", but literally "the helper": the pavilion has helped the people to see the Lord.

[^05-26]: This and the next stage direction are found only in the Kāvyamālā.

[^05-27]: A play on words: *advaita-bhāj* means both "adherents of *advaita*" (non-dual Vedanta, among whom sannyasa is held in honour) and "devotees of Advaita".

[^05-28]: That is, *advaitam* (the doctrine, neuter) and *advaitaḥ* (Advaita Acharya, masculine) differ only in the form of the word and grammatical gender (*liṅga*, also "outward mark"). The Lord jests that He is an "advaitist" only in the sense of devotion to Advaita.

[^05-29]: See note 20: *daṇḍa*, staff and punishment.

[^05-30]: *Aviśeṣāt*, "without special deliberation": in M. Guha's commentary *viśeṣa-vivecanā-rahitatvāt*, in his Bengali translation "I had no time to consider further"; Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation: "simply, in the ordinary way". Another possible understanding, "not as an adherent of any school" (i.e. not for the sake of Mayavada), is not supported by the translations.

[^05-31]: Names from the "Thousand Names of Vishnu" (*Mahābhārata*, Anushasana-parva): *saṁnyāsa-kṛc chamaḥ śānto niṣṭhā-śānti-parāyaṇaḥ*.

[^05-32]: Verses 23–24 are lost in GGM (broken off at *etāvaty api durgame jana-ghaṭā-sa…*) and are translated according to the Kāvyamālā. Both verses are complete in M. Guha's edition; the second line of verse 23 reads there: *sarvair eva yathāsukhaṁ prabhur asau pāre-parārdhair nṛbhiḥ*, "by all the people, more than a *parārdha* in number (i.e. countless), this Lord [is seen] without difficulty" (Guha's commentary: *pāre-parārdhaiḥ = saṅkhyātītaiḥ*); the verse is understood the same way in Guha's Bengali translation and in Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation.

[^05-33]: A play on the title of the drama: *caitanya*, "consciousness", and the Lord's name; "Chaitanya-chandrodaya", "the rising of the Moon Chaitanya".

[^05-34]: That is, Shantipura has become the same Navadvipa, and the present time the former time.

[^05-35]: Women in Sanskrit drama usually speak Prakrit; the stage direction stresses that Shachi, contrary to this, speaks Sanskrit.

[^05-36]: *Phalituṁ* ("to bear fruit"): so in GGM and in Jivananda Vidyasagara, and M. Guha translates likewise ("to give fruit"); *phaṇituṁ* in the Kāvyamālā OCR is a misprint. The first half of the verse is understood as a rhetorical question: the creeper of motherly love can bear no fruit higher than this. So M. Guha's Bengali translation ("higher than you yourself") and Jivananda's commentary (higher than this meeting and embrace) understand it. Grammatically an affirmative understanding is also possible (*kim api*, "a certain"): "the creeper of motherly love is able to bear a certain highest, best fruit".

[^05-37]: A play on words: *bhavati bhavatī*, "you, lady (*bhavatī*), are…"; *kṣamā* is both "forbearance, forgiveness" and "earth": Shachi is Forbearance embodied (or Mother Earth).

[^05-38]: *Śyāmāmṛta*, "dark (blue) nectar": both Krishna (Shyama) and the waters of the Yamuna.

---

# Act 6. Mercy to Sarvabhauma (Sārvabhaumānugraha)

*The Ganga tells the Ocean of the Lord's journey from Shantipura to Purushottama; the meeting with Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya and his conversion.*

*(Enter Ratnakara.[^06-1])*

**Ratnakara.** Ah, why did my beloved, the river of the gods, seem to me so suddenly dejected today? I must ask her the cause of her heart's grief. *(Looking ahead.)* What, is the goddess still in the same state? I shall approach and ask. *(Approaches.)*

*(Enter the Ganga in the state described.)*

**Ganga.** Alas, alas! That Lord, thanks to the water that washed whose feet such good fortune fell to my lot — for a long time I enjoyed the touch of His body. What lot could be higher? And now, having had Him, it seems I shall have Him no more. What grief could be greater? What am I to do, unfortunate that I am? *(Stands for some time, lost in thought.)*

**Ratnakara** *(approaching)*. Bhagirathi,[^06-2] why are you so sad?

**Ganga.** What, is it you, noble husband? What are you asking, my husband? For I am unfortunate.

**Ratnakara.** Bhagirathi, why is that?

**Ganga** *(in Sanskrit)*.

> **1.** I, whose fame resounded through the whole world only because I am the water that washed His feet —
> me this very Lord, eager for rasa, by the art of His daily bathing filled with relish[^06-3] —
> and today He is leaving me; alas, that is why I suffer.

**Ratnakara.** Yes, I know. I have heard that He, turning back from the road to Mathura, has now come to the house of Advaita. Why, then, do you suffer?

**Ganga.** That is so. But when His intimates — Shrivasa and the others — came from Navadvipa, one day the Lord, after eating with all His own, amid laughter and merry sport, the food cooked by Sri Shachi-devi, sat down with them and began to say something.

**Ratnakara.** What was it?

**Ganga.**

> **2.** "O Advaita and the rest, hear this: at the bidding of My mother
> and of you, loving friends, I did not go.
> Because of this a hindrance arose, and so I cannot go to Mathura.
> So all of you, in your mercy, give Me leave — let Me go now!"[^06-4]

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then Advaita Acharya said:

> **3.** "If all the people say to You, 'Go, since it pleases You' —
> then, before Your departure, the breath of life will depart from all.
> For, alas, we have no strength to bear such reproach of ourselves[^06-5] —
> and so these souls will depart first, having tasted calamity."

**Ratnakara.** Well said, Advaita, well said! And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then the Lord lovingly spoke again: "O Advaita and the rest!

> **4.** Since I have accepted this garb, to remain here, near My native land,
> together with those who love Me, is now improper.
> You are wise and learned, and there is the mother of this body too —
> consider it yourselves; ah, what more should I say!"

**Ratnakara.** The Lord too spoke rightly. And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then the Acharya, together with all the Lord's kinsmen, went to His mother, Shachi-devi, and they held counsel.[^06-6]

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Since no one could agree in this matter, His mother said: "Oh, if [His staying here] is a sin against dharma, how can I, for my own happiness, bring on Him the gossip of the wicked? Let happen to me what may. So let Him rather go to the very field of Jagannatha[^06-7] — that is good. There is hope that news of Him will reach us sometimes."

**Ratnakara.** Splendid, mother, splendid! You have surpassed Devahuti.[^06-8] And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then all, deeply moved, said again: "Mother! How could you say such a thing? From now on we cannot dispute your word, as if it were established by the shruti."

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then she said: "Let happen to me what may. But if the wicked find fault with Him — that is unbearable. If He goes to Jagannatha, you will be able to go there from time to time, and I shall get news."

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then all came to the Lord and told Him what the goddess had said.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then the Lord said joyfully: "As the mother of the universe commands, so do you command. I am going."

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then all said: "Master! Stay a few days. Let us gaze at Your lotus feet."

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then, to gladden His mother and them, He stayed there three days; as before, He ate together with everyone the food cooked by His venerable mother and the mother of Achyutananda,[^06-9] and gladdened all. On the fourth day, when He was about to leave, all took counsel and gave Him as companions Nityananda, Jagadananda, Damodara and Mukunda. Then, when the people of Navadvipa had gone and the Lord had set out with them, Advaita Acharya wept bitterly. The Lord left without taking leave of him.

**Ratnakara.** Alas! Now, when the Yavana king, the lord of Gauda, is at war with the Gajapati,[^06-10] no one goes back and forth. How then does He go with only four companions?

**Ganga** *(changing to Sanskrit)*. Noble husband, there is nothing surprising in that. Look, look:

> **5.** He is the inner ruler of the worlds, the disinterested friend;
> there is no man in the world to whom He is hateful — who would hate Him?
> In this realm of two kings,[^06-11] right between two armies, fearsome and skilled,
> He — mark it — passed unhindered with five or six friends.

And further:

> **6.** In every village there are crafty cheats,[^06-12] guards of the crossings,
> roamers of forests and mountains, highway robbers
> who terrify travellers — as soon as they saw Him with their own eyes,
> with tears, trembling all over, they rolled on the ground.

**Ratnakara.** Why, the Lord does not gladden only those whose hearts are steeped in an innate demonic nature; all others, even the lowest, He purifies with a single glance and colours with the rasa of bhakti. And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Just so.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then, leaving the royal road, impassable because of the many huge elephants, tall horses and foot soldiers of the endless train of the great vassals of the Gajapati moving along it without a break, He went deep into the forest and took a forest path.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga** *(in Sanskrit)*. Then —

> **7.** Behold a wonder! Once, ah, when the lord of the Raghus[^06-13] entered the thicket,
> leopards, elephants, buffaloes and rhinoceroses of gigantic bodies,
> struck with terror of His bow, fled; but now these same creatures,
> receiving a drop of the moisture of His sweetness, only stood stock-still.

So for several days He went from forest to forest, bestowing His mercy on the beauty of various trees and creepers, and then came out again onto the royal road.

**Ratnakara.** Why?

**Ganga** *(in Sanskrit)*.

> **8.** In the district of the town of Remuna there is a Deity (*pratimūrti*) of the Lord with a flute in hand;
> it is "ancient", and so, out of great reverence,
> to bow to it, He then went by the royal road.

**Ratnakara.** What does "ancient" mean?

**Ganga.** Some say that this God, though in four-armed form, is in truth the two-armed Gopinatha, who is the one the devotees must worship. Others say that everywhere only four-armed Deities are seen to be ancient, while the two-armed Deity of Gopinatha is not ancient.[^06-14]

**Ratnakara.** They are mistaken: in Kataka and other places Sakshi-gopala and other [two-armed Deities] are very ancient. And then, and then?

**Ganga** *(in Sanskrit)*.

> **9.** He fell to the ground like a rod and bowed to Him, and He honoured Him highly:
> on His head suddenly fell a garland, slipping from the head [of the Deity].[^06-15]

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga** *(in Sanskrit)*. Then the Lord lovingly composed a few verses in His honour. Namely:

> **10.** The left arm — the elbow lowered, the shoulder bent, the hand raised,
> the forearm lying across slightly covering the broad chest,
> the bracelet on it reddening — reveals a certain indescribable beauty
> of Him at whose lips is the flute.[^06-16]

And further:

> **11.** And the right arm: from the tightly bent elbow and other joints, as from behind a dam,
> a stream of the nectar of sweetness has burst forth[^06-17] —
> and, flooding the earth with it, it eclipses
> the beauty of Him at whose lips is the flute.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Taking the forest path once more, He went to the capital called Kataka to see Sakshi-gopala.

**Ratnakara.** He is truly worth seeing: only in appearance is He a *pratimā* (a Deity), but in truth He is Bhagavan Himself. For —

> **12.** Chosen by a brahmin as witness, He slowly walked behind him,
> stepping with His beautiful tender lotus feet, the ankle-bells jingling close by;
> but when the brahmin, turning his neck, looked at Him at the boundary of the land of Mahendra,[^06-18]
> the Lord, taking on the form of a *pratimā* (a Deity), unhurried, remained standing on that very spot.

And then, a long time afterwards, the great king Gajapati Purushottama-deva[^06-19] brought Him and installed Him in his capital.

**Ganga.** So it is.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then, looking at Him, the Lord, as though considering that He had come out of His own heart and was standing before Him, for a moment seemed to enter into Him again. *(In Sanskrit.)* And then He composed this verse:

> **13.** Its petals are the toes, reddish and tender; its pollen sparkles
> in the broken lines of saffron designs from the breasts of the intoxicated cowherd women;
> its honey is pure consciousness; its filaments are the clusters of radiance of the jewel-nails;
> its stalk is the shank: may the lotus foot of the Enemy of Putana protect us!

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then all saw this.

**Ratnakara.** What was it?

**Ganga** *(in Sanskrit)*.

> **14.** He, though engaged in playing the flute, for a moment lowered the flute from His lips,
> and it seemed as if, filled with pure faith,
> He wished to enter into conversation with Him.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Staying there that day, on the next day, with the words "I must see the lotus-eyed Sri Jagannatha!", His heart as if whirling with great longing, He hurriedly set out.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then, coming to the village called Kamalapura and bathing in the river, the Lord went ahead to see the temple of Bhagavan, while Nityananda-deva, saying, "What is this staff — a fragment of unexpected calamity?",[^06-20] broke the Lord's staff, which was in his hand, and threw it into the middle of the river.

**Ratnakara.** And then, and then?

**Ganga.** Then Mukunda, seeing the temple of Bhagavan Jagannatha, said to the Lord: "Master! Look, look:

> **15.** Is it not an arm raised by the earth to draw the sun to herself?
> Or is it Shesha risen from Patala to ascend to Satyaloka?
> Or is it the mass of radiance of the royal jewels on the serpent's hoods ascending to heaven? —
> It is, it is, O Master, the wondrous temple of the Lord shining forth!"

**Ratnakara.** Goddess, I have understood everything. Why do you grieve here? My happiness is your happiness too. Look, look:

> **16.** For whose sake — O Shiva, Shiva! — I was bound, for whose sake I was churned,
> that Lord who took no account of my being the father [of His consort][^06-21] —
> ah, what good fortune is mine! — that very Hari, leaving even that beloved,
> has settled, like one of the family, by the banyan on our shore!

So come, let us go nearer and look.

**Ganga.** As my noble husband pleases.

*(Exeunt both.)*

*End of the introductory scene (praveśaka).*

*(Enter the Lord in deep self-forgetfulness, surrounded by Nityananda and the others.)*

**The Lord.**

> **17.** Though it touches the clouds, it has entered the hearts of the worlds;
> though it is huge, it fits between two eyes;
> though built of stones, it pours down a rain of rasa —
> this palace of the Lord shines before us.

*(Walks about the stage with longing.)*

**All.** Ah, this path, which can be covered in a moment, seems to the Lord longer than long! What are we to think of here? To see the Lord, the Moon of Nilachala, is easy only for His servants, not for others. It is especially hard for us, foreigners: without the help of the king's people it is not easy.

**Mukunda.** There is a way.

**The others.** What is it?

**Mukunda.** Here lives Gopinatha Acharya, the son-in-law of Visharada and brother-in-law of Sarvabhauma,[^06-22] a very close friend of the Lord; like you, he knows well the special pastimes of the Lord in Navadvipa.

**The others.** And what of it?

**Mukunda.** Through him, by means of Sarvabhauma, everything can be arranged.

**All** *(acting joy)*. Well said! Then first of all we must seek out his dwelling. *(They walk about the stage.)*

*(Enter Gopinatha Acharya.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(to himself)*.

> **18.** My right eye throbs,[^06-23] my mind is clear and serene —
> I do not know what happiness the sight of the Lord of the universe will bring today.

*(Goes to see Jagannatha.)*

**Mukunda.** Here he is, Gopinatha Acharya!

**Nityananda.** Mukunda, go, go quickly, before he enters the Lion Gate!

*(Mukunda does so.)*[^06-24]
