**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking ahead)*. Ah, who is this? Probably some Gaudiya. *(Looking closely.)* Ah, from Navadvipa! *(Looking still more closely.)* Ah, it is Mukunda, the beloved servant of the Lord Vishvambhara! The omen has come true! *(Approaches.)* Ho, is it you, Mukunda?

**Mukunda.** Acharya, I bow to you.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Is the Lord well?

**Mukunda.** The feet of Sri Prabhu have come right here.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(joyfully)*. What are you saying? Where, where? *(Embraces him again.)*

*(Mukunda leads him back with him.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking ahead)*. Mukunda, who is this lord of ascetics?

*(Mukunda tells him everything.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(with amazement)*.

> **19.** What was then the pure rasa of love is now mixed with the rasa of renunciation;
> to the eyes its taste is still the same, but to our heart
> it has become sweet and sour.

**Mukunda** *(approaching)*. O Master! This is Gopinatha Acharya, sent by Sri Jagannatha-deva Himself, as if His representative, to meet the Lord.

**The Lord** *(acting the return to outward consciousness)*. Where is he, where?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Here I am.

*(Falls at His feet. The Lord embraces him. Gopinatha Acharya bows to Nityananda, then bows to Jagadananda and Damodara.)*

**Mukunda.** Acharya, how can it be arranged that the Lord may see Bhagavan Jagannatha without hindrance, as much as He wishes?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Only if such good fortune dawns for Sarvabhauma.

**All.** Then let it be reported to the Lord Himself.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Svami! We think that without a talk with Sarvabhauma it is not easy to behold Sri Jagannatha. What is the Lord's will?

**The Lord.** Your will is My will.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Then the tree of Sarvabhauma's good deeds has borne fruit. Master, be pleased to direct Your feet this way, this way.

**The Lord.** Show the way.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** This way, this way.

*(All walk about the stage. Then enters Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya, teaching his students, together with them.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Ho, who is there? Find out whether the midday incense offering to Sri Jagannatha has been made or not.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Here, here, the Bhattacharya's lessons are over. Now he will go inside [the temple]. Let me approach quickly. *(Reflecting.)* Master, please rest here a little until I return. *(Approaching hurriedly.)* Bhattacharya, a certain great soul has arrived. Go out to meet him and bring him in.

**Sarvabhauma.** Is he far?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Quite near.

*(Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya rises and goes to meet Him, the students following him.)*

**Nityananda.** Ah, here he is, Bhattacharya Sarvabhauma. Since he has come himself, so much the better.

**Sarvabhauma** *(approaching)*. Obeisance to Narayana. *(Bows.)*[^06-25]

**The Lord.** Love for Krishna, thought of Krishna!

**Sarvabhauma** *(to himself)*. Ah, what an unusual blessing! So in His former ashrama He must have been a Vaishnava.

*(The students smirk.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Svami, this way. *(Leads the Lord, seats Him in a fitting place and sits down himself. All act sitting down.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Acharya, in His former ashrama He was evidently a Gaudiya?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Bhattacharya, in His former ashrama He is the daughter's son of Nilambara Chakravarti of Navadvipa and the son of Jagannatha Mishra Purandara.

**Sarvabhauma** *(with love and respect)*. Ah, why, Nilambara Chakravarti studied together with my father, and Mishra Purandara was highly respected by my venerable father.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Their aim is that it should be easy for them to see Sri Jagannatha.

**Sarvabhauma.** So it shall certainly be. Ho, who is there? Call Chandaneshvara.

*(Enter Chandaneshvara hurriedly; he bows to the Lord, then to his father.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Chandaneshvara, follow the Shripada.[^06-26] [Let everyone arrange it so that every day, as much as He wishes, He may calmly see the holy face and no one may hinder Him. He is most honoured by me.][^06-27]

**Chandaneshvara.** As your holy feet command.

**Sarvabhauma.** Svami, stay here [with us].

*(The Lord with Nityananda and the others goes out, taking Chandaneshvara with Him.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Acharya, stay.

*(Gopinatha Acharya, already halfway out, takes Mukunda by the hand, comes back and sits down.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Acharya, looking at Him, I am stirred with love and sorrow. Because of His kinship with Nilambara Chakravarti He is very dear to me. How is it that at so young an age He has accepted the fourth ashrama? Who instructed Him in the great saying?[^06-28]

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Keshava Bharati.

**Sarvabhauma.** Alas! How did He enter the tradition of the Bharatis?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** He does not look much to externals. He values only renunciation itself.

**Sarvabhauma.** What is external here?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** The superiority of a tradition and the like.

**Sarvabhauma.** Wrongly said. The lustre of an ashrama is not external.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** That is only for the sake of honour, and therefore external.

**Sarvabhauma.** What fault is there in honour? That is why I say: He should take the *yoga-paṭṭa*[^06-29] again from a monk of a better tradition, and then be refined by hearing Vedanta.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(as if vexed)*. Bhattacharya, you do not know His greatness. But I, from what I have seen, conclude that He is God Himself.

**Mukunda** *(to himself)*. Splendid, Acharya, splendid! You have cooled my burning life.

**Students.** By what means of valid knowledge[^06-30] have you learned that He is God?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** By a special knowledge born of the Lord's mercy — that is, by a transcendental means of knowledge. The essence of the Lord cannot be grasped by a worldly means of knowledge, for it is transcendental.

**Students.** That is not the sense of the shastras. Why should God not be proved by inference?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Let God be proved by inference, if you like; but His essence cannot be proved so. It [is grasped] only by knowledge born of His mercy, for that alone is the means of its valid knowledge.

**Students.** It is not seen to be a means of valid knowledge.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** On this there are the words of the Purana.

**Students.** Recite them.

**Gopinatha Acharya.**

> **20.** And yet, O God, only he who has been touched by even a particle of the mercy of Your lotus feet
> knows the essence of the greatness of Bhagavan — no other, though he search for long, knows it.[^06-31]

"Though he search" — that is, by the paths of the shastras and the rest.

**Students.** So, then, His mercy is not obtained through the shastras?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Of course not. Otherwise why is it said "though he search"?

**Students** *(laughing)*. Why, then, did you study in vain for so many days?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** That is simply a particular craft.

**Sarvabhauma** *(laughing)*. Evidently His mercy has descended on you. Since you know this essence, tell us at least something.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** It is not a subject for telling; it is known only by experience. If His mercy descends on you, you will experience it yourself.

**Students** *(to themselves)*. How unabashedly he chatters something irrelevant to him! Or perhaps, as brother-in-law, he is jesting a little.[^06-32]

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Bhattacharya, you said something unbefitting about Him, about God, and I, unable to bear it, answered something. It does not befit such deep people as you to speak so. But there is no fault of yours in this:

> **21.** To Him whose powers become the ground both of dispute and of agreement among disputing talkers,
> and again and again plunge them into self-delusion — to the Infinite, the possessor of infinite qualities — obeisance![^06-33]

**Sarvabhauma** *(laughing)*. I see: you are a Vaishnava.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(humbly)*. If He is merciful, you too will become one.

**Sarvabhauma.** Enough of idle talk. After the darshan of the Lord, go to the house of my maternal aunt:[^06-34] let your "God" with His companions stay there in my name, and invite Him [to a meal] with the prasada of Bhagavan.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** As you command.

**Sarvabhauma.** And I shall go to perform the midday rites. *(Goes out with the students.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Mukunda, come, come. *(Walks about the stage.)* O Mukunda! The thunderbolt of the Bhattacharya's words still cuts my heart. Only if the all-merciful Lord removes it will my mind be at peace.

**Mukunda.** What is impossible for this Lord?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Come, let us follow the Lord, who has gone to behold Sri Jagannatha. *(Exeunt both.)*

**Behind the scenes.** Ah, a wonder!

> **22.** The beauty of the wondrous eyes — full-blown white lotuses on which young stately bees have alighted[^06-35] —
> and the face, the orb of the moon of the bright fourth night, bathed in vermilion!

And further:

> **23.** This [face] rises from the tree that is Brahman — charming, merciful, with a wondrously beautiful body;[^06-36]
> by the clusters of the shoots of its radiance the pride of a mirror of sapphire is crushed.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Ah, evidently they have already beheld the holy face, since Nityananda and the others are describing it to one another.

*(Again, there, behind the scenes.)*

> **24.** Coloured with love by gazing at each other, both, with unblinking eyes,
> are shining — both Lords of the world — on either side motionless in every limb.
> Is this Brahman-in-a-man being absorbed into the Brahman-in-wood, or else —
> O Shiva, Shiva! — is the wooden Brahman itself being absorbed into the Brahman-in-a-man?[^06-37]

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Splendid, splendid! The one Lord has as it were become twofold — the one relished and the one relishing.

*(Again, there.)*

> **25.** Both are full of compassion and long to deliver the world,
> both have entered the path within reach of the world's eyes;
> within the one is the son of Nanda, within the other is Brahman in wood:
> only in this is there a difference between them.[^06-38]

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Splendid, Nityananda-deva, splendid! You know the truth of the Lord Gaurachandra. I think they have all had darshan and are already coming back. So let us too look upon the Lord Sri Jagannatha and then quickly follow them. *(Exeunt both.)*

*(Enter the Lord, motionless with the bliss of beholding the Lotus-eyed One,[^06-39] with Him Nityananda and the others, and also Chandaneshvara.)*

**Chandaneshvara.** O great souls! Did your darshan of the Lord go comfortably?

**Nityananda and the others.** Everything has been fulfilled entirely as our hearts desired.

**Chandaneshvara** *(to himself)*. Ah, why does Gopinatha Acharya delay? What am I to do now? My venerable father said nothing [more]; I think he told the Acharya everything. Why has he not come yet? *(Looks about.)*

*(Enter the Acharya with Mukunda, acting haste.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Quickly, Mukunda, here they come. So both have come to pass: we have seen the Moon of Nilachala, and we are seeing the Golden One, golden as a golden mountain. *(Approaching hurriedly.)* Chandaneshvara, go home from here. From now on I shall arrange everything myself.

**Chandaneshvara.** As you say. *(Bowing to the Lord, he goes out.)*

*(Gopinatha Acharya approaches and bows to the Lord.)*[^06-40]

**The Lord** *(acting the return to outward consciousness)*. Come here, come! *(Embraces him.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Master, the Bhattacharya has invited You together with Your companions. So be pleased to come this way.

*(Having brought Him to the dwelling mentioned before and performed for the Lord every service — from washing His feet to cleaning His teeth — after the meal, when He has sat down comfortably, dejectedly:)*

Master, the Bhattacharya has "invited" You to something else as well.

**The Lord.** To what?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** He wants You to take the *yoga-paṭṭa* from a sannyasi of a fitting tradition, and then he will teach You Vedanta.

**The Lord.** He has shown Me mercy. So be it; I shall do so.

**Mukunda.** Master, ever since then the Acharya has been going about with his heart burnt by that shower of word-sparks. He did not even take mahaprasada today.

**The Lord.** Acharya, I am just a boy. He said it because he loves Me. Why do you suffer?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Master! If Your holy feet remove this thorn from my heart, then I shall preserve my life. *(Weeps.)*

**The Lord.** The Lotus-eyed One will fulfil your desire. Damodara, let him be served [the prasada] of Bhagavan.[^06-41]

**Damodara.** As You command. *(Leads him away.)*

**The Lord.** Jagadananda, try to arrange that we may see the pastime of the awakening of the Lord Pundarikaksha at the end of the night.

**Damodara** *(entering together with the Acharya)*. Master, as commanded: he has partaken of mahaprasada and wishes to spend the night right here.

**The Lord.** Good, good.

*(All pass the hours in friendly conversation.)*

*(Behind the scenes, the sound of the hand-conch.[^06-42])*

**All** *(in amazement)*. Ah, only the last watch of the night remains! For this is —

> **26.** Is it not the jingling of all the ornaments of the goddess of the city, swaying as she rises from her couch?
> Or the trumpeting, trumpeting of the royal elephant at the temple?
> Or is it the goddess Mercy calling the living beings of the world: "Come to the treasury of compassion!"? —
> so draws near the sweet sound of the sacred hand-conch.

**The Lord.** Ah, the night has truly passed. Acharya, come: let us together behold the time of the awakening of the Lotus-eyed One.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** As You command. *(Goes out to do what is fitting at this time.)*

**Someone** *(entering hurriedly)*. Ho, who is here? Where is Gopinatha Acharya? Do you know what has happened? *(Addressing the air.)* What do you say? The Acharya is with Sri Krishna Chaitanya-deva? Very well, I shall go there. *(Walking a few steps, he looks ahead.)* Ah, that must be the Acharya. *(Approaches.)*

*(Enter the Acharya.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Ah, it is almost the time of the awakening. I shall hurry the Lord.

**Man** *(approaching)*. Acharya, the Bhattacharya sends word: take Sri Krishna Chaitanya-deva with you and see to it that He sees the awakening of the Lord Jagannatha-deva.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** I shall do as I am told. *(Approaches to hurry Sri Krishna Chaitanya-deva.)*

*(Enter the Lord with His companions.)*

**The Lord.** Mukunda, see why the Acharya is delaying.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Here I am, waiting for the Lord.

**The Lord.** Then go ahead.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(doing so)*. Master, this way, this way. *(Acting entering and reaching the jagamohana.[^06-43])* Master, look:

> **27.** When at this hour the heavy panels of the doors are thrown open,
> breathing out fragrance with the stream of dense perfume that has burst from the sanctuary,
> this palace of the Lord, as if languid from interrupted sleep at the end of the night,
> seems to open its mouth and begin to yawn.

And further. Master, a wonder, a wonder!

> **28.** In the depth of dense darkness, where there are no lamps,[^06-44] in the womb of the deepest Gambhirika,[^06-45]
> the eyes of the victorious lord of Lakshmi, risen from His couch,
> are like two lovely white lotuses victoriously swayed by the wind amid the waters of the Kalindi,
> whose inner parts are licked by swarms of intoxicated bees.

*(The Lord, standing behind the Garuda pillar, gazes eagerly.)*

**Mukunda.** Acharya, look, look:

> **29.** In an instant rows of lamps lit up all around in the cave of the Gambhirika,
> but their light was dimmed by the streams of tears pouring from His eyes —
> and they seemed merely painted.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Look, look:

> **30.** First the washing of the face, then anointing with oil, bathing, dressing and the rest;
> then the play of the "child's meal",[^06-46] and after it this meal, the "Hari-vallabha".

Now behold the special worship called the "morning incense offering".

*(The Lord, motionless with bliss, His hair standing on end and with tears, gazes and gazes.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Ho, Jagadananda and the others! Look, look! One, holding in his joined palms a little rice prasada from the morning incense offering, and another with a garland in his hands, are coming together. Are they not going to give it to Mahaprabhu, Sri Krishna Chaitanya? Who has sent them? Or perhaps Sri Jagannatha Himself.

*(Enter two temple servants, who approach Sri Krishna Chaitanya. The Lord approaches and bows His head. One offers the garland. The Lord spreads out the edge of His upper cloth. The other offers the rice prasada. The Lord, receiving it in the edge of His cloth and bowing to Sri Jagannatha, goes out swiftly, like a lion.)*

**All.** Ah, what is this? Why has the Lord suddenly gone? Come, let us see where He is going. *(Coming out of the temple, walking a few steps and looking ahead.)* Ah, the Lord has turned off the road to His lodging!

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking closely)*. Ho, Damodara and the others! The Lord has headed for Sarvabhauma's house. [So the tree of the Bhattacharya's good deeds has borne fruit. Let Damodara and Jagadananda go with the Lord, and I with Mukunda will stay nearby.][^06-47]

**Both.** As you please. *(Exeunt.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Come, Mukunda, let us stand in Sarvabhauma's second courtyard. *(Doing so, he looks ahead.)* Ah, here are two of Sarvabhauma's servants coming this way in amazement. Let us stand aside.

*(They stand at the edge of the door. Enter two servants.)*

**First.**[^06-48] Hey! That sannyasi knows some bewitching mantra: because of him the Bhattacharya has become like one possessed.

**Second.** Hey, what has he become like?

**First.** Hey, don't you know? The Bhattacharya had not yet got out of bed, when this one suddenly came up to the door of the bedroom. Then a boy student says: "Bhattacharya, Bhattacharya! Get up, get up! That sannyasi has come." Then the Bhattacharya — thump! — jumped up and fell at his feet. And he put Jagannatha's rice prasada into his hand and says: "Eat!" And our master, like a madman, without thinking, swallowed that rice in a flash — without bathing, without even rinsing his mouth! And having swallowed it, like a madman, he broke out in gooseflesh all over, his clothes got soaked with tears, his throat is gurgling — and he's rolling on the ground as if he'd had a fit. What will happen, we don't know.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(having listened)*. Mukunda, did you hear?

**Mukunda.** It is because of your grief that the Lord resolved on this.

**Servants.** Let's go and look for Gopinatha Acharya. *(Exeunt.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Good, good that they did not notice me. Let us wait here for Damodara and Jagadananda. *(They do so.)*

*(Enter Damodara, acting amazement.)*

**Damodara.**

> **31.** Without a pen the Lord has bound the mighty wild elephant intoxicated with rut;
> without sprinkling water He has as it were quenched the flame of His own people's burning heart —
> for He, as if by chance, has made the heart of this lord of pandits,
> harder than a thunderbolt, juicy as nectar.[^06-49]

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(approaching)*. Damodara, what is this?

**Damodara.** It is a secret; we shall tell it. But I have come here for your sake. Let us go out by another door and follow the Lord, who has already returned to His lodging.

*(All three walk a few steps. Damodara, having recited the verse "Without a pen…", tells everything.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** I have already heard everything from the lips of those two servants who were talking together.

**Damodara.** It is only by your mercy that this good fortune has fallen to him. Come, let us go quickly to the Lord. For the Bhattacharya too, having performed his daily rites, will soon come there; then it will become clear what is in his heart. So far not a word has been said.

*(They walk about the stage.)*

*(Enter the Lord, already seated in His place, Nityananda and Jagadananda.)*

**The Lord.** Jagadananda, where is Gopinatha Acharya?

**Jagadananda.** Here, here the Acharya is hurrying with Damodara and Mukunda.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(approaching)*. Glory, glory to the all-merciful One!

**Jagadananda.** Ah, what is this play of words of yours, never heard before?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** That is known only to the Lord.[^06-50] *(Approaches and bows.)*

**Behind the scenes.** Master! This is not the road to the temple of Sri Jagannatha!

**The Lord** *(having listened)*. Acharya, find out what is there.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking towards the back of the stage)*. I see, I see! Here is the Bhattacharya, not even having seen Sri Jagannatha, coming to fall at the Lord's feet.

**Damodara.** That was known beforehand.

*(All stand looking into his face. Enter Sarvabhauma, his mind opened by amazement.)*

**Sarvabhauma** *(to himself)*. Ah, Gopinatha Acharya spoke the truth. If even our heart has become like this, then He is God Himself. *(Walks about the stage with longing.)* Ah, here is my maternal aunt's house. Let me enter. *(Acts entering.)*

*(The Acharya rises and goes out to meet him.)*

**Sarvabhauma** *(looking ahead)*. Acharya, what is Svami doing?

**Acharya.** This way, this way, please, sir.

**Sarvabhauma** *(approaching, he prostrates himself like a rod before the Lord and joins his palms)*.

> **32.** When the Lord, overcome by the rasa of various pastimes, plays His play like a man,
> even seeing Him face to face one does not grasp His truth:
> ah, a man cannot recognise the philosopher's stone by sight
> until by its touch it turns mere iron into gold.

And further:

> **33.** Abode of the hearts of Your own, O Lord, master of Padma,[^06-51]
> You, Padmanabha, wander the earth in the guise of a king of ascetics.
> How can we, like beasts, perceive You, all-powerful, though manifest?
> Alas, fate is hostile to us!

**The Lord** *(covering His ears)*. Bhattacharya, I am only an object of your fatherly affection. Why do you say such things? *(To Himself.)* Ah, now I must test what is in his heart. *(Aloud.)* O great soul, explain, then: what is the meaning of the shastras?

**Bhattacharya** *(joining his palms)*. Master!

> **34.** The shastras, though full of different views, are so composed, each according to its own taste;
> otherwise how would their experts be skilled in refuting one another?
> But the supreme aim in them is something special: the unselfish bhakti-yoga of Murari,
> and it is obtained only by the mercy of the Lord.

And further:

> **35.** The Vedas, the Puranas, the *Bhārata*, the tantras and mantras — all of them
> teach that the reality is Brahman alone; but in its truth all go astray.

For —

> **36.** Its primary meaning, from [the root meaning] "being great" and "making great",[^06-52] is the possession of attributes;
> and those who proclaim it attributeless are unable to prove it.

So it is said in the *Hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātra*:

> **37.** Every shruti that speaks of the attributeless in fact names the one possessed of attributes;
> and when they are examined, the one possessed of attributes, ah, for the most part proves the stronger.

For look: from the shruti "From bliss indeed these beings are born, by bliss, once born, they live, into bliss they go and enter"[^06-53] it follows that it possesses attributes, since it stands in the [case-]roles of source, instrument, object and the rest. So too from the words "That from which these beings are born…",[^06-54] and in the sayings "It saw…" and "He desired…"[^06-55] — where "seeing" is deliberation and "desire" is resolve — from these two too the possession of attributes follows; so attributelessness cannot be established. And once attributes are established, form too follows from the attributes. And this form is not material, for [in the *Brahma-sūtras*] "light" and "foot" are spoken of:[^06-56] as the non-materiality of the light is proved, so too is the non-materiality of this form. If it were merely attributeless, the way would be opened to the doctrine of the void. Therefore the word "Brahman" is used in its primary sense, and in the primary sense Brahman is Bhagavan — that is what remains [in the end]. As it is said:

> The knowers of the truth call that non-dual knowledge the truth,
> which is named Brahman, Paramatma and Bhagavan.[^06-57]

But those who are possessed by the demon of defending their own side and, although the primary meaning is by no means absent, proclaim through a secondary meaning[^06-58] an attributelessness that can never be defined — theirs is mere obstinacy. In reality:

> **38.** Bliss is said to be twofold: embodied and unembodied.
> The refuge of the unembodied is the embodied; the embodied bliss is Achyuta.

> **39.** The unembodied is the Paramatma, whose nature is knowledge, devoid of gunas,
> self-existent, unchanging — and that is Brahman: such is the view of the saints.

> **40.** When the truth is examined there is no difference between the unembodied and the embodied:
> the difference is only conceived by the Vedas — as between a gem and its radiance.

So in the *Hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātra*. And in the *Kapila-pañcarātra* too, the words of Kapila addressed to Agastya:

> **41.** Two Brahmans are to be known — the embodied and the unembodied;
> the all-pervading Narayana, by nature embodied and unembodied, is the object of meditation.[^06-59]

Only the doctrine of the Pancharatra is free from partisanship. But the adherents of the doctrine of a purely attributeless Brahman, defining Brahman as unembodied bliss alone, reveal only the coarseness of their impressions,[^06-60] but cannot establish attributelessness. But if the doctrine of the Pancharatra is accepted, both the sayings "Bliss is the form of Brahman" and "Brahman is one without a second"[^06-61] are established: embodiment by [bliss being called] form, and non-duality by the comparison "as a gem and its radiance". Therefore that Bhagavan is Brahman is the view of all the shastras. Some, however, owing to the peculiarity of their impressions, thinking that in Bhagavan — embodied bliss — the form is merely assumed for the sake of play, say that Brahman is only unembodied bliss. But the followers of the Pancharatra are faultless *śiṣṭas*,[^06-62] for they worship Bhagavan; therefore it is by their conduct that the meaning of the Vedas is to be inferred. For —

> **42.** The branches of the tree of the Vedas are thousands; it is not all open to direct perception:
> its parts are to be inferred from the words of the Puranas and the conduct of the faultless *śiṣṭas*.

Here are words of the Puranas, for example: "…whose friend is the supreme bliss, the full, eternal Brahman"[^06-63] and so on. "Full", because possessed of form; the attributeless Brahman is not full, that is, devoid of form. And the *śiṣṭas* are the Sattvatas.[^06-64] Their doctrine is this: "The supreme deity is Vasudeva; from Vasudeva, the supreme Soul, comes Sankarshana, the 'jiva'" and so on. "Jiva" here is the one who enlivens, who creates the jivas, not a jiva himself. And He is the Atman, for [the Lord] Himself said: "The Brahman of sound and the supreme Brahman are both My eternal bodies."[^06-65] So from Him the creation of the jivas proceeds. Thus the meaning of the shastras is that the embodied bliss is Krishna.

**The Lord.** Splendid, splendid! And now go and behold the Lotus-eyed One.

**Bhattacharya.** As the Lord commands. *(Goes out, taking Damodara and Jagadananda with him.)*

**Mukunda.** Why has he gone, taking those two?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** There is a secret in this. But, Master — is this the same Bhattacharya?

**The Lord.** From association with you, a great Bhagavata, he has become quite different.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(laughing)*. Just so.

*(Enter hurriedly, tossing aside the curtain, Damodara and Jagadananda.)*

**Damodara and Jagadananda.** Master! The Bhattacharya has sent two verses and alms-food — the remnants of the meal of the Lord Jagannatha.

**The Lord.** I am favoured.

**Mukunda.** Let me look at these two verses. *(Taking the leaf from his hand, he reads to himself.)*

> **43.** That one Primeval Person who, to teach renunciation, knowledge and His own bhakti-yoga,
> has assumed the body of Sri Krishna Chaitanya — the ocean of mercy: in Him I take refuge.

> **44.** He who has appeared under the name of Krishna Chaitanya to reveal
> His own bhakti-yoga, lost in the course of time — into His lotus feet
> deeper and deeper sinks[^06-66] the bee of my mind.

*(Having read them, he copies them onto the wall and gives [the leaf] into the Lord's hands. The Lord, glancing at it, tears it up.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Master, it is midday. Let Your holy feet prepare for what is to be done next.

**All.** Just so.

*(Exeunt all.)*

*Thus ends the sixth act of the* Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya, *called "Mercy to Sarvabhauma".*

[^06-1]: Ratnakara ("the mine of jewels") is the Ocean, the husband of the rivers, the Ganga among them.
[^06-2]: Bhagirathi is a name of the Ganga, brought down to earth by King Bhagiratha.
[^06-3]: A play on words: *rasa* is both "moisture, water" and "taste, aesthetic feeling"; *rasa-kautukī*, "eager for rasa" (or "fond of water"); *rasayāṁ cakāra*, "relished / made me relish".
[^06-4]: That is, the Lord stayed at the request of His mother and friends, and as a result His journey to Mathura (Vrindavana) was interrupted. The reading *vyajani* ("arose") follows the Kāvyamālā; GGM has *vyajati*.
[^06-5]: *Dhikkāra*, the exclamation "shame!", reproach of themselves for letting Him go.
[^06-6]: GGM has the run-together *ācārio-jaṇaṇīe* ("to the Acharya's mother"), which gives an unclear sense. M. Guha reads *saddhaṁ ācārieṇa jaṇaṇīe* (Sanskrit *sārdham ācāryeṇa jananyāḥ*), "together with the Acharya … to the mother"; the Acharya is Advaita. So Guha's Bengali translation and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understand the passage.
[^06-7]: Shri-kshetra is Puri, the abode of Jagannatha.
[^06-8]: Devahuti, the mother of the sage Kapila, let her son go to the forest after his instruction (*Bhāg.* 3.33). Shachi surpasses her, letting her son go for the sake of His good name.
[^06-9]: Achyutananda is Advaita's son; his mother is Sita-devi, Advaita's wife.
[^06-10]: The Muslim sultan of Bengal (Gauda) and the king of Orissa (Gajapati, "lord of elephants", the title of the kings of Utkala), i.e. Prataparudra.
[^06-11]: According to the note of the Kāvyamālā edition, "between two kingdoms, Gauda and Utkala".
[^06-12]: *Paṭu-kapaṭin*, "skilful cheats" (so we divide *paṭukapaṭino*); *ghaṭṭa-pāla*, toll-collectors at the crossings; *vāṭa-pāṭac-cara*, according to the Kāvyamālā, "highway robbers".
[^06-13]: Rama, when He entered the Dandaka forest.
[^06-14]: That is, the dispute is whether the worship of the two-armed Krishna with the flute (Gopinatha) is ancient, in contrast to that of the four-armed Vishnu. Remuna (near Balasore) is the site of the temple of Kshira-chora-Gopinatha.
[^06-15]: *Śekhara*, a garland or head-ornament of the Deity.
[^06-16]: *Muralī-mukha*, "He at whose lips is the flute", i.e. Krishna the flute-player (here the form of Gopinatha in Remuna); so M. Guha's Bengali translation ("Murali-vadana") and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation ("Krishna with the flute") understand it.
[^06-17]: The reading of GGM and M. Guha, *-bādho labdha-srutā* (in the Kāvyamālā, by the OCR, possibly *-bādhoruddha-srutā*). The sense according to Guha's Bengali translation: the stream of the nectar of sweetness, bursting from behind the "barrier" of the bent elbow and other joints, floods the earth, and the right arm eclipses the beauty of Krishna with the flute. *Vilakṣayati* literally means "puts to confusion, shames"; Guha and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation render it more mildly as "reveals".
[^06-18]: Mahendra is the mountains and land in the south of Utkala (Orissa).
[^06-19]: So according to the Kāvyamālā and M. Guha (*gajapati-mahārājena*), and likewise in Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation; *gaṅgā-pati* in GGM is apparently a corruption. Gajapati ("lord of elephants") is the title of the kings of Orissa. Purushottama-deva is a fifteenth-century king of Orissa who, according to tradition, brought Sakshi-gopala from Vijayanagara (Vidyanagara) to Cuttack.
[^06-20]: *Akāṇḍopaplava-khaṇḍa*, "a fragment of unexpected calamity"; a play *daṇḍa*, "staff" — "punishment" is possible. Kamalapura is a village by the river (Bhargi) near Puri.
[^06-21]: The Ocean was bound by Rama's bridge and churned by the gods for the nectar; from it Lakshmi, the consort of Vishnu, was born, so the Ocean is Hari's father-in-law. Here Hari (Chaitanya) has left Lakshmi too (has accepted sannyasa) and settled on the shore of the Ocean. *Taṭa-vaṭa-kuṭumbī*, literally "a householder of the banyan on the shore"; M. Guha's Bengali translation understands *kuṭumbī* as "by right of kinship, as one of the family" (the Ocean being His father-in-law), Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation simply as "a dweller on my shore". Neither translation explains the banyan; probably the sacred banyan of Puri is meant.
[^06-22]: *Āvutta*, according to the Kāvyamālā note citing the *Amarakośa*, "sister's husband". Visharada is the father of Sarvabhauma.
[^06-23]: For a man, a throbbing of the right eye is an auspicious omen.
[^06-24]: In GGM the speeches from verse 18 to this stage direction are repeated twice (an obvious typesetting error); in the Kāvyamālā, once.
[^06-25]: "Namo nārāyaṇāya" is the usual greeting of Advaitin sannyasis; the Lord answers with a Vaishnava blessing, which surprises Sarvabhauma.
[^06-26]: Shripada, a respectful address to a sannyasi.
[^06-27]: The words in brackets are found only in the Kāvyamālā; they are missing in GGM.
[^06-28]: *Mahā-vākya*, the "great saying" of the Upanishads (such as *tat tvam asi*), imparted at initiation into sannyasa.
[^06-29]: *Yoga-paṭṭa*, the cloth or garment given at initiation into sannyasa; here, a repeated initiation in a "higher" branch of Shankara's order (the Bharatis were held lower than, for example, the Sarasvatis or Tirthas).
[^06-30]: *Pramāṇa*, in Indian logic a means of valid knowledge (perception, inference, testimony and so on). What follows is a parody of a Nyaya disputation.
[^06-31]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 10.14.29 (the prayer of Brahma).
[^06-32]: A brother-in-law is by custom allowed to tease his sister's husband; *vyadhikaraṇa* is a term of logic ("having a different substrate", i.e. "beside the point").
[^06-33]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 6.4.31 (Daksha's hymn "Hamsa-guhya"). The Kāvyamālā has *naiṣa vo doṣaḥ*, "this is not your fault"; GGM has *vā*.
[^06-34]: *Mātṛ-svasur āvāse*, "in the dwelling of the mother's sister". This is Sarvabhauma's own aunt: below he says *asman-mātṛ-ṣvasuḥ puram*, "the house of our (my) aunt", and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation likewise has "my aunt".

[^06-35]: The bees are the dark pupils on the white field of Jagannatha's huge eyes.
[^06-36]: The division *cāru-kāruṇikam ā-rucirāṅgam*, "charming (*cāru = manojña*), merciful (*karuṇāmaya*), with limbs wholly (*ā-*) beautiful", follows the commentary of Jivananda Vidyasagara; M. Guha's Bengali translation ("the all-merciful, captivating Daru-brahman") also sees no name of Lakshmi here. A possible other division *-mā-rucira-* ("beautiful with the beauty of Lakshmi") is not supported by the translations.
[^06-37]: *Dāru-brahman*, "Brahman in wood", is Jagannatha; *nara-brahman*, "Brahman in a man", is Chaitanya (cf. *Bhāg.* 7.15.75: "the supreme Brahman hidden in human form").
[^06-38]: Chaitanya is Krishna hidden within a golden form; Jagannatha is Brahman hidden in a wooden form.
[^06-39]: So according to the Kāvyamālā (*niḥspanda*, "motionless"); GGM has *niḥsyanda* ("an outpouring [of bliss]").
[^06-40]: In the Kāvyamālā there is a speech of Damodara here: "Master, the Acharya bows to You."
[^06-41]: According to the Kāvyamālā: "Damodara, let him be invited by you."
[^06-42]: *Pāṇi-śaṅkha*, the conch blown at dawn in the temple of Puri to awaken Jagannatha.
[^06-43]: The jagamohana is the hall for pilgrims in front of the sanctuary.
[^06-44]: So according to the Kāvyamālā (*dīpābhāva*); GGM has *doṣābhāva*, "[though] there is no night" (or "without fault").
[^06-45]: The Gambhira/Gambhirika is the inner sanctuary of the temple.
[^06-46]: The bala-bhoga is the early morning meal of Jagannatha; the Hari-vallabha is the next offering.
[^06-47]: The sentence in square brackets is found only in the Kāvyamālā; without it the following speech of "Both" (Damodara and Jagadananda) hangs in the air in GGM.
[^06-48]: The servants speak a vulgar Prakrit (Magadhi: *ale* for *are*, *bhaṭṭācālia* and so on).
[^06-49]: *Vārī*, a pen or post for catching elephants. The Kāvyamālā notes here the figure *nidarśanā*.
[^06-50]: So in GGM (*bhagavatām*); the Kāvyamālā has *bhavatām*, "that is known only to you [yourselves]".
[^06-51]: Padma is Lakshmi; Padmanabha, "lotus-navelled", is Vishnu.
[^06-52]: The word *brahman* is derived from the root *bṛh*, "to grow, to be great": *bṛhattva*, "greatness", and *bṛṁhaṇatva*, "the power of making great".
[^06-53]: *Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 3.6.
[^06-54]: *Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 3.1.
[^06-55]: *Chāndogya Upaniṣad* 6.2.3 (*tad aikṣata*); *Taittirīya Upaniṣad* 2.6 (*so 'kāmayata*).
[^06-56]: Cf. *Brahma-sūtra* 1.1.24 (*jyotiś caraṇābhidhānāt*), where "light" is interpreted as Brahman on the ground of the mention of its "feet" (quarters).
[^06-57]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 1.2.11. In the Kāvyamālā the verse is quoted only in part and unnumbered; in GGM in full, also unnumbered.
[^06-58]: *Lakṣaṇā*, the secondary (figurative) meaning of a word, to which the Advaitins resort in interpreting *brahman* as attributeless. The construction *mukhyārthābhāvābhāve 'pi* is understood as "although there is no absence of the primary meaning": by the rules of poetics *lakṣaṇā* is resorted to only when the primary meaning is impossible, while here it is quite possible. M. Guha's Bengali translation renders the passage more freely ("both in the absence of the primary meaning and being unable to define it even by the secondary meaning"), but the general sense — a reproach of forced interpretation — is the same.
[^06-59]: Verses 38–41 are unnumbered in GGM (there 42 follows straight after 37); the numbers follow the Kāvyamālā. The *Hayaśīrṣa-* and *Kapila-pañcarātra* are Vaishnava agamas. Verse 37 is quoted in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 6.142).
[^06-60]: *Vāsanā*, subconscious impressions, tendencies left over from past experience.
[^06-61]: The first saying is attributed to the shruti (in GGM it is marked as *Chāndogya* 6.2.1, which applies only to the second: *ekam evādvitīyam*).
[^06-62]: A *śiṣṭa* is a recognised authority on conduct, a "well-bred" person whose conduct serves as a source of dharma.
[^06-63]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 10.14.32 (in GGM the reference is 10.14.31).
[^06-64]: The Sattvatas are the ancient Vaishnava Pancharatrikas. There follows an exposition of the doctrine of the vyuhas (Vasudeva, Sankarshana and so on), in which Sankarshana is called the "jiva".
[^06-65]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 6.16.51.
[^06-66]: *Līyate*, "sinks into, clings to" (of a bee in a lotus); the Kāvyamālā has *līyatām*, "may it sink (cling)". Verses 43–44 are quoted in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 6.254–255).

---

# Act 7. Pilgrimage (Tīrthāṭanaḥ)

*King Prataparudra questions Sarvabhauma about the Lord, who has gone to the south; the brahmins returning from the Godavari tell of the healing of Vasudeva and of the meeting with Raya Ramananda; Malla Bhatta, envoy of the king of Karnata, relates the wonders of the Lord in the southern lands; behind the scenes the Lord's return to Purushottama is announced.*

*(Enter Sarvabhauma.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Ah, why has the Gajapati[^07-1] summoned me today at an unusual hour? Well, I must go to him. *(Walks.)*

*(Enter the King, and around him his retinue.)*

**King.** Ho, who is there? Call the Bhattacharya.

**Bhattacharya** *(approaching)*. Here I am — I have come without waiting to be called.[^07-2]

**King** *(bowing)*. Here is a seat, sit down. *(The Bhattacharya, pronouncing a blessing, sits down.)* Bhattacharya, I have heard that a certain king of ascetics, of great power and supreme compassion, has now come from Gauda.

**Bhattacharya.** Just so.

**King.** How could I bow to His feet?

**Bhattacharya.** That is extremely difficult. Those who rarely show themselves among people are within sight only of one who has nothing.[^07-3] Besides, it is now several days since He went to the southern quarter.

**King.** Why did He go, leaving the nearness of the great lord Jagannatha?

**Bhattacharya.** "They make holy places holy, bearing within them the Holder of the club"[^07-4] — such is the nature even of ordinary great souls. And He is Bhagavan Himself.

**King** *(with amazement)*. Bhattacharya! Since you say so, it must truly be so. Why did you not try to keep Him here?

**Bhattacharya.** Maharaja!

> **1.** He at the movement of whose brows Brahma and the other guardians of the worlds rise and fall like waves —
> without His goddess Mercy He yields to no one's power.

And yet:

> **2.** What praises did I not offer! What piteous entreaties did I not devise!
> What threats did I not act out — even that I would part with my life!
> How I wept, clasping His feet! — and still He went.
> In the great, by nature, mercy and refusal are one and the same.

**King** *(longingly)*. Will He return here?

**Bhattacharya.** Certainly. For His companions have stayed here.

**King.** How then did He go alone?

**Bhattacharya.** Can such a one as He ever be alone?[^07-5] And still I gave Him worthy brahmins as companions.

**King.** How far?

**Bhattacharya.** They will go as far as the Godavari. And the Lord Himself, I think, will go all the way to Setubandha.[^07-6]

**King.** Why were the brahmins not sent with Him to the end?

**Bhattacharya.** He did not allow it. And He accepted their company as far as the Godavari only for the sake of Ramananda.

**King.** What is this consideration concerning him?

**Bhattacharya.** When He had firmly decided to go, I said: "On the bank of the Godavari lives Ramananda. He must certainly be favoured."

**King.** Whence such good fortune for him?

**Bhattacharya.** Maharaja! He is a born Vaishnava. Before, he was an object of mockery among us; but now, since the Lord's mercy has descended on us, we have come to know his greatness.

**King.** I have heard what mercy has descended on you yourself.

**Bhattacharya.** The Lord's power reveals itself.

**Doorkeeper** *(entering)*. Bhattacharya! The brahmins you sent have returned from the Godavari and are waiting for you.

**Bhattacharya.** The King is pining with impatience. Let them be brought straight here.

**Doorkeeper.** As you command. *(Goes out and enters again, leading them.)*

**Bhattacharya.** Oh, come in, come in!

*(The brahmins approach, honour the King with blessings and bow to Sarvabhauma.)*

**King.** Sit down and tell everything from the very beginning. *(All sit down.)*

**Bhattacharya.** Let one of you tell it.

**One of the brahmins.** From here He first went to the god Alalanatha,[^07-7] beheld Him and offered Him praise. He walked, skilled in His gait like an irresistible leader of intoxicated elephants who knows no goad — so that even we, hurrying with all our might, could barely keep up with Him — moving like a mass of Mount Sumeru split off and driven by a mighty north wind. Ceaselessly chanting the names of the Lord, with a voice broadened by sankirtana, deep and firm, He drowned the rumbling of a train of rain clouds; and the people along the road could not take their eyes off Him, all their thoughts fixed on Him. So in a very short time He reached Kurma-kshetra.[^07-8]

**Sarvabhauma.** And on the way did He take no food?

**Brahmin.** No, He took none.

**Bhattacharya.** And what did you do?

**Brahmin.** We snatched a bite of whatever came to hand and followed without losing sight of Him. There, in Kurma-kshetra, He bowed to the god Kurma,[^07-9] offered Him praise and stayed in the house of the best of the twice-born named Kurma. He at once invited Him, and when the Lord had accepted food from him, He did something else — utterly astonishing.

**Bhattacharya.** What was it?

**Brahmin.** There lived a brahmin named Vasudeva, sick with running leprosy: his whole body was wet with thick blood and pus, swarming with a multitude of worms. When the worms fell off him, he picked them up and put them back in the same place — without any disgust, as if his mind were absorbed in the Lord. No one told the Lord about him, but the Lord found him Himself. And at once, as if he were the closest of friends found again after a long separation, He tightly embraced him with His long arms — just as he was. And then this brahmin,

> **3.** "Who am I — a pauper, most sinful — and who is Krishna, the abode of Shri?
> Yet me, 'an unworthy brahmin', He has clasped in His arms!"[^07-10] —

had scarcely uttered this when his body at once became radiant and beautiful, as it had been by nature. Then he said: "Lord! Why have You punished me so? As long as I was like that — diseased — I lived without grief[^07-11] and without agitation. But now, when honour comes to me, all the movements of the mind will rise up again." The Lord said: "Brahmin! From now on, apart from remembrance of the Lord, your mind will turn to no outward affairs. So give up your worry."

**King.** Bhattacharya! Truly He is Ishvara. Otherwise such compassion is impossible for a jiva — while curing leprosy is something even a great yogi can do.

**Sarvabhauma.** And then, and then?

**Brahmin.** And then He, whose power is inconceivable, came to Nrisimha-kshetra,[^07-12] beheld the Lord Nrisimha, offered Him praise, bowed, circumambulated Him and went on.

> **4.** With waves of rays like those of a golden mountain He seemed to make the southern quarter golden (*gaura*),[^07-13]
> and by His appearance — a river surging with mercy — He melted people's hearts everywhere.

And further:

> **5.** "Krishna, Krishna, glory, Krishna, Krishna, oh!
> Krishna, Krishna, glory, Krishna, Krishna, oh!
> Krishna, Krishna, glory, Krishna, Krishna, oh!
> Krishna, Krishna, glory, Krishna — save us!"

> **6.** So with streams of the nectar of words — tender, captivating, in a voice broadening like a cloud —
> He delighted both ears of all who had ears, and stole their hearts as well — and went on.

**Sarvabhauma.** You speak the truth. Such is His nature.

**Brahmin.** Then He came to the bank of the Godavari and settled down to rest — He who gladdens the hearts of the people of the whole world, charming with matchless mercy, rich in the good fortune of prema, the Lord — like a grove of golden ketakas filling the whole circle of the quarters with fragrance, the abode of all virtues.[^07-14] And through His fame, which spread of itself, a throng of "gods of the earth" — brahmins — gathered there, conjecturing: "Who is this? Who is this?"[^07-15] Someone invited Him to a meal. At once the news of Him, full of endless mystery, a delight to the ears of many people, spread everywhere — and Raya Ramananda came, as if drawn by a mantra, as if possessed by a spirit, as if he had lost his balance from the shock of amazement and rapture.

**King.** Ramananda! Blessed are you, blessed! Those feet have been revealed to your eyes!

**Brahmin.** At once, on arriving, he himself fell at the lotus feet, bathed in tears.[^07-16] "Ah, so you are Ramananda!" the Lord said graciously, and he answered: "Yes." Then the Lord said: "At Sarvabhauma's request I wished to see you, and so I have stayed here a little while. You have done very well to come yourself. So tell Me something." And although Ramananda had no special knowledge of the Lord — who He was, what His name, what His greatness, what His purpose, what His essence — still, filled with the bliss of an old, constant friendship, like a man of feeling (*bhāvuka*),[^07-17] without any timidity he began to recite something:

> **7.** If the mind is not conquered, what use are austerity and the rest?
> But what victory over the mind is there if one does not think of Madhava?
> And what thinking is that, if — alas! — the heart does not melt?
> And how can it melt if the *vāsanās*, the traces of former impressions, have not been washed away?

**The Lord.** This is external.

> **8.** **The Lord.** What is knowledge?
> **Ramananda.** Devotion to Hari alone, not skill in the Vedas and the rest.
> **The Lord.** What is fame?
> **Ramananda.** The report "He is devoted to the Lord", not that born of gifts and the like.
> **The Lord.** What is wealth?
> **Ramananda.** To be dear to Him, not an abundance of money, people, villages and the rest.
> **The Lord.** What is sorrow?
> **Ramananda.** Separation from one who is dear to the Lord, not the pain of heart-wounds and the like.[^07-18]

**The Lord.** Good. Who is liberated?

**Ramananda.**

> **9.** Those whose nearness is to the feet of Hari, full of love (*anurāga*), not to passion (*rāga*);
> whose fondness is for the bhakti-yoga of Hari, abounding in prema, not for yoga;
> whose support is in the fragrant anointing of the surge of love, not in the body —
> they, juicy by nature, are the liberated (*muktā*) — not the pearls (*muktā*).[^07-19]

**The Lord.** So be it.

> **10.** **The Lord.** What should be sung?
> **Ramananda.** The deeds of the pastimes of Vraja.
> **The Lord.** What is the highest good here?
> **Ramananda.** The company of saints.
> **The Lord.** What should be remembered?
> **Ramananda.** The name of the Enemy of Agha.[^07-20]
> **The Lord.** On what should one meditate?
> **Ramananda.** On the feet of Murari.
> **The Lord.** Where should one dwell?
> **Ramananda.** In Vraja alone.
> **The Lord.** What is a delight to the ears?
> **Ramananda.** The pastimes of Vrindavana alone.
> **The Lord.** What should be worshipped?
> **Ramananda.** Here — the two radiances called Sri Krishna and Radha.

**The Lord.** Good. Speak, speak further.

**Ramananda** *(to himself)*. What can be said beyond this? What has been said already answers the questions. And what must now be said — whether it will please Him or not, I do not know. *(Reflecting a moment, aloud.)*

> **11.** Let those who do not know rasa suck the bitter nimba fruit, nirvana;
> but we, knowers of the essence of rasa, drink the dark nectar
> that remains after it has been sipped by the cupped hands of the eyes
> of the cowherd maidens, languid with love.[^07-21]

**The Lord.** Say something more. This is about the same thing.

**Ramananda.** Beyond this there is nothing more to expound. What more can be said? *(Reflecting, aloud.)*

> **12.** Those that make the roads seem to have been licked by flocks of young chakoras;[^07-22]
> those that in an instant turn into crystal the rows of footstools adorned with jewels;
> those that on feet washed and wiped give rise to the suspicion that drops of water are trickling from them —
> may these moonbeams of the jewel-nails of Krishna's lotus feet protect you[^07-23] for ever!

**The Lord.** This is mere poetry. Say more.

**Ramananda** *(reflecting a moment)*.

> **13.** The juice of the red lac from Radha's lotus feet on Hari's chest,
> putting to shame the Shrivatsa, the Kaustubha and the goddess Rama[^07-24] —
> like the orb of the radiance of the morning sun taken captive by darkness under a veil,
> like a red lotus in full bloom in the waters of the Kalindi — may it protect us!

**The Lord.** This too is the same.

**Ramananda** *(clasping His feet)*.

> **14.** Friend! There is no difference between us: "He is the beloved, I am the beloved one";
> it is as if Madana himself had forcibly ground both our hearts together in the juice of love.[^07-25]

Or else:

> **15.** Then there was no thought: "I am the beloved, You are the lover";
> all the movements of the mind had gone, and even the notion of "you" and "I" had vanished for us.
> But now there is the conviction: "You are the husband, I am the wife" —
> and still life holds on in me. What could be more astonishing?[^07-26]

**Sarvabhauma.** And what did the Lord say to that?

**Brahmin.** Hear what was seen then:

> **16.** Like a serpent with raised hood listening to the song of a snake-charmer,
> He listened to what was said with great delight and attention;
> and then the Lord — whether because what was said was out of place, or from helplessness in bliss —
> covered his mouth with His lotus hand.[^07-27]

**King.** Bhattacharya! What is the meaning here?

**Bhattacharya.**[^07-28] Maharaja! Love free of all conditions (*upādhi*) does not tolerate the slightest condition. Hearing in the first half of the unconditioned love of the divine couple, Krishna and Radha, the Lord acknowledged it as the supreme goal of man. And that He covered Ramananda's mouth shows that it is a most intimate secret.

**Brahmin.** Then Ramananda parted his hair in two, wound it round both His feet, fell prostrate and spoke:

> **17.** Crest-jewel of the great knowers of rasa, preceptor of the pastimes in the drama full of rasa —
> You are the lord of my heart: how then can we praise You?
> To take on various roles — that too is natural to You;
> therefore the role of an ascetic is not so astonishing to us.[^07-29]

*(And for a long time he wept, holding the lotus feet, and from time to time said something.)*

> **18.** Has not fate unexpectedly brought me a treasure?
> Has not the moon of nectar broken, that it pours forth so?
> For I have beheld Your lotus feet, O God —
> the beautifully ripened fruit of the tree of bliss![^07-30]

"Lord, as I saw You today in a dream, so I see You awake." *(And again, clasping His feet, he weeps. The Lord embraces him tightly.)* After this that best of brahmins who had invited the Lord came and said: "Master, it is already past noon." And the Lord went to perform the midday rites. And we, bowing to His lotus feet, set out on the return journey that same day.

**Sarvabhauma.** Then rest now.

**Brahmin.** As you command.

*(The King gives them a reward. The brahmins accept it respectfully and go out. Then enters the doorkeeper.)*

**Doorkeeper.** Sire! The minister of the king of Karnata, the great maharaja, a king of pandits named Malla Bhatta, has brought a gift sent by him and stands at the door.

**Sarvabhauma.** I know him: he is a great pandit.

**King.** Let him enter.

**Doorkeeper.** As the King commands. *(Goes out and enters, leading him.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Welcome, venerable Bhatta! *(Acts rising.)*

**Malla Bhatta** *(honouring the King with blessings)*. Bhattacharya! What are you doing? It is not for you to rise before me. However —

> **19.** Ever lofty is the golden mountain, ever deep are the rain clouds,
> ever humble are the wise, whose sole ornament is modesty:
> greatness in people is bound up with their very nature.[^07-31]

**King.** Here is a seat.

**Malla Bhatta.** I sit, as bidden. *(Sits down.)*

**King.** Is the king of Karnata well?

**Malla Bhatta.** One who has friends like you is always well. And now even more so.

**King.** How so?

**Malla Bhatta.** Maharaja!

> **20.** Since the time when from this land, on the pretext of a holy pilgrimage,
> a certain unique king of ascetics, shining like molten gold —
> ah! — came to the southern quarter, since then he too has found peace.

**Sarvabhauma** *(with eager impatience)*. Venerable Bhatta, tell it, tell it!

**King.** In what way?

**Malla Bhatta.** In the southern quarter, in ascending order: a few people are devoted to rites, very few to knowledge, the Sattvatas[^07-32] can be counted on one's fingers, the Pashupatas[^07-33] are more numerous, and most numerous of all are the heretics. And because of association with them — constantly confused by mutual disputes, foremost in the art of every kind of interpretation[^07-34] — our sovereign was almost always in great anxiety.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Malla Bhatta.** And then, as soon as this lord of ascetics unexpectedly appeared, people began to flock together from all the quarters and intermediate quarters — old and young, youths — with joyful amazement; and the hosts of pandits who had come out of curiosity also experienced His greatness, revealed by the mere beauty of His body, the highest delight for the eyes — and even without any instruction, from a special impression arising at that moment: "Where have we been until now!"[^07-35] — with trembling hair and tears, every one of them fell away from his own doctrine and entered upon His path. Hearing of this from one to another, our protector of the world of men attained the highest bliss and, to learn the details of His deeds, sent brahmins in disguise — as far as Setubandha and until His return from there. Hearing from their lips of the wonders of His transcendental deeds, he forgot the flame of the forest fire of worldly existence.

**King.** Minister, blessed is your sovereign!

**Sarvabhauma.** What, then, did you hear? Tell us something.

**King.** Minister, tell it.

**Malla Bhatta.** Once the Lord was walking, absorbed in His own nectar: His whole golden body was bathed in the tears streaming from His eyes; with the hairs standing on end in multitudes, He looked like a kadamba tree covered with buds; His voice was broken by sobs from chanting the Lord's name; with no knowledge of the road He walked by His own impulse, helpless with His own bliss — and yet He walked straight along the road. Seeing Him, the heretics said: "This is a Vaishnava, a mendicant monk; this at least he will accept under the name of 'the Lord's prasada'. Let us feed him this."[^07-36] Putting on a plate the most impure food, fit only for dogs, they came out to meet Him and, crying "Svami, accept this prasada of the Lord!", handed it to Him. The Lord, though all-knowing, unable to reject what had been called the Lord's prasada, took it in His hand and walked on, raising His hand with it. And at once some huge bird snatched the food in its beak from the Lord's palm and flew away.

**Sarvabhauma.** Oh, how great is their blindness!

> **21.**[^07-37] Him by whose maya even the lords of the worlds wander deluded —
> Him these insignificant ones thought to delude here!

**Malla Bhatta.** On another day He happened to enter the house of a certain brahmin in another place and, seeing that the brahmin was devoted solely to repeating the name "Rama, Rama, Rama", went away. On the way back He went in there again to see him and, seeing the same brahmin repeating "Krishna, Krishna, Krishna", asked: "Brahmin! Why, when I was going there, did I see you repeating 'Rama, Rama, Rama', and now I see you repeating 'Krishna, Krishna, Krishna'? Tell Me, what is the matter." Then he said: "Lord! This is Your own power. From my very childhood I was devoted to repeating only the name of Rama, but since I saw You, Lord, only 'Krishna, Krishna, Krishna' comes from my lips. Even by force I cannot stop it. So the fault is that of Your glance, not mine."[^07-38]

**King.** Bhattacharya, what is the meaning here?

**Bhattacharya.** Maharaja! Although both names are equal in meaning:

> **22.** "Yogis delight (*ramante*) in the Infinite, in the Real, in the Soul that is bliss and consciousness" —
> so by the word "Rama" the Supreme Brahman is denoted.[^07-39]

And likewise:

> **23.** The root *kṛṣ* denotes being, and *ṇa* bliss;
> their union, the Supreme Brahman, is called "Krishna"[^07-40] —

so in that both denote the Supreme Brahman, they are equal. But still, in those who have love (*rati*), at the word "Rama" the form of the Lord of the Raghus appears, and at the word "Krishna" the form of the prince of Vraja. And the secret is that Sri Krishna Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, and therefore at the sight of Him it is the form of Krishna that appears. And further:

> **24.** "The name of Rama, O fair-faced one, is equal to a thousand names";[^07-41]

> **25.** "The fruit that three recitations of the thousand holy names give
> is bestowed by a single repetition of the one name of Krishna"[^07-42] —

so the name of Krishna is higher than the name of Rama.

**Malla Bhatta.** That is true. We too came to this conclusion in our sovereign's assembly. Thus all the worshippers of Vishnu in the southern quarter come to Him through devotion to the Lord of the Raghus: seeing the beautiful places of the deeds of the Lord of the Raghus during His exile in the forest — Panchavati[^07-43] and the rest — they are naturally attached to them. But now, having seen this king of ascetics, they have all become partisans of Krishna.[^07-44] Also, in one place there was a brahmin who, out of great ignorance, understanding neither the words nor the meaning, read the *Bhagavad-gītā* with mistakes, and almost everyone laughed at him; but while he read, he was overcome by trembling of the hair and tears. Seeing him, the Lord thought: "Oh, here is the highest of those entitled (*adhikārī*)!" — and said to him: "Brahmin! What does what you are reading mean?" He answered: "Svami, I know no meaning at all. But while I read, I see Sri Krishna, dark as a tamala, goad in hand, on the chariot of Partha." Then the Lord said: "You are the highest of those entitled to read the *Gītā*," and embraced him. Experiencing the bliss of this touch, he attained a bliss even greater than from reading the *Gītā* and, with the words "Svami, You are He!", fell to the ground bowing, utterly beside himself.

**Sarvabhauma.** Such knowledge befits him. To one whose heart has been purified by the constant manifestation of the Lord, the true form appears.

**Malla Bhatta.** So it was discussed in our sovereign's assembly too. Thus the wondrous stories brought back by those secret envoys are endless — how many of them can be retold?

**Sarvabhauma.** So it is.

**King** *(longingly)*. Ah, when shall I be able to see the Lord?

**Behind the scenes.** Ah, here is the time for beholding! So do not delay![^07-45]

**King** *(joyfully)*. Bhattacharya! Since this voice, as if on purpose for our talk, announces the time of beholding Jagannatha, I think that Sri Krishna Chaitanya too will soon come.

**Bhattacharya.** Sire, just so.

**King.** Bhattacharya! This Purushottama will become as it were the seed of Purushottama-kshetra.[^07-46] From Him, surely, many shoots of bliss will spring. There will also be a special happiness in the service of the Moon of Nilachala.

**Sarvabhauma.** That is true. Righteous kings are portions of the gods. Therefore what unfolds in your mind is the truth.

**Behind the scenes.** Ah! True, true!

**King** *(joyfully)*. Again the same omen in words! Look, find out who it is.

**Bhattacharya.**[^07-47] It is a pilgrim, together with another pilgrim, pouring out his eagerness to see Jagannatha.

**Doorkeeper** *(entering)*. Sire! Some people are running here at full speed.

**King.** Find out whether they are unarmed or armed.

**Doorkeeper** *(goes out and enters again)*. Sire! All are unarmed.

**Sarvabhauma.** Probably that very God is returning.

*(Again behind the scenes, all together, a noise: "I praise Hari! I praise Hari!")*

**Sarvabhauma.** It is true — the Lord has returned!

**Behind the scenes.**

> **26.**[^07-48] In all those holy places He found no such satisfaction,
> and so He has hurried back to Purushottama.
> Look: by the Abode of Jewels are two moving jewel mountains![^07-49]
> Fate is favourable to us.

**Sarvabhauma.** Maharaja! Since it is Gopinatha Acharya crying out so joyfully, the Lord has really come. I shall go to Him.

**King.** Hurry, hurry! Malla Bhatta, you too rest now. And we shall go on a special matter.

*(Exeunt all.)*

*Thus ends the seventh act of the* Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya, *called "Pilgrimage".*

[^07-1]: *Gajapati* ("lord of elephants") is the title of the kings of Orissa; here Prataparudra.
[^07-2]: Literally "came without a summons" (*anāhvānena*): Sarvabhauma was already on his way to the King when the latter ordered him to be called.
[^07-3]: *Niṣkiñcana*, "one who has nothing", a renunciant; possibly a hint that for a king, a possessor of wealth, it is especially hard to see a sannyasi (cf. the plot of Act 8).
[^07-4]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 1.13.10 (the words of Yudhishthira to Vidura).
[^07-5]: That is, the Lord, who bears everything within Himself, is never "alone".
[^07-6]: *Setubandha* ("the Bridge", Rameshvaram), the southernmost point of the pilgrimage, the place of Rama's bridge to Lanka.
[^07-7]: *Ālālanātha*, a temple of Vishnu (Jagannatha) south of Puri, at Brahmagiri.
[^07-8]: *Kūrma-kṣetra*, the holy place of Kurma (the incarnation of Vishnu in the form of a tortoise), today Srikurmam in Andhra.
[^07-9]: A play: both the deity and the master of the house bear the name Kurma.
[^07-10]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 10.81.16, the words of Sudama (Kuchela) after his meeting with Krishna.
[^07-11]: A play on words: *āmayāvinā vinā duḥkham*, "being diseased — without grief". Further: *samprati sampratipadyamāna-mānatayā*, "now, when honour comes".
[^07-12]: *Nṛsiṁha-kṣetra*, the holy place of Nrisimha; according to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 8.3), after Kurma-kshetra the Lord came to Jiyada-Nrisimha-kshetra, the temple of Nrisimha on the hill of Simhachalam near Vishakhapatnam. M. Guha and Krishnadasa Baba do not specify the place.
[^07-13]: *Gaurayan*, "making white/golden": an allusion to the name Gaura.
[^07-14]: Translated according to the Kāvyamālā: "…*nirupama-kṛpābhirāme prema-saubhagavati bhagavati kanaka-ketakopavana iva*…". The electronic text of GGM has a repetition (*vihita-viśrāme*… twice) and omits the word *bhagavati*.
[^07-15]: A play on words: *kṛta-samūhaḥ samūhaḥ*, "a throng (*samūha*) that made conjectures (*samūha*)". Cf. also *samanantaram ananta-rahasye*, "at once… full of endless mystery".
[^07-16]: GGM: *vevīyamānāśru* ("trembling, in tears"); KM (by the OCR): *sarerīyamāṇāśru*, apparently *sa rerīyamāṇāśru*, "with flowing tears".
[^07-17]: A play: *subhagambhāvuka* ("becoming fortunate") — *bhāvuka* ("sensitive, full of bhava").
[^07-18]: In GGM the stanza is broken up into speeches without a number (the number 8 is missing); in KM it is marked ॥८॥. We keep the number 8.
[^07-19]: *Yamaka* (assonance): *sānurāge na rāge*, *bhakti-yoge na yoge*, *upadehe na dehe*; *muktā na muktāḥ*, "the liberated, not pearls"; *prakṛti-sarasa*, "juicy, full of rasa by nature" (and of a pearl, "moistly lustrous"). GGM *prayāsattiḥ*, KM *pratyāsattiḥ* ("nearness"): translated according to KM.
[^07-20]: *Aghāri*, "the enemy of (the demon) Agha", i.e. Krishna.
[^07-21]: That is, Krishna (*śyāmāmṛta*, "the dark nectar"), "drunk" by the gazes of the gopis. GGM *cuṣantu*, KM (OCR) *cuṣyantu*.
[^07-22]: Chakoras, by poetic convention, feed on moonlight: the radiance of Krishna's nails is so bright that the road seems licked by them. KM (OCR) *nīcān* instead of *līḍhān*, probably an OCR error; translated according to GGM.
[^07-23]: GGM *pāntu vaḥ* ("you"), KM *pāntu naḥ* ("us").
[^07-24]: The Shrivatsa is the curl-mark on Vishnu's chest; the Kaustubha His gem; Rama (Lakshmi) dwells on His chest. The red trace of Radha's feet eclipses all three.
[^07-25]: This and the next stanza are the words of Radha (or of Her friend); cf. their use in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 8) in the description of the conversation with Ramananda.
[^07-26]: GGM *kāntās tvam* is a misprint; read *kāntas tvam* (KM).
[^07-27]: GGM *dhṛta-kṣaṇa* ("seizing the moment"?) and *…kara-padmenāsyāpy adhatta*; translated according to KM: *dhṛta-phaṇa* ("with raised hood") and *kara-padmenāsyam asyāpy adhatta* ("covered his mouth too"). The reading *dhṛta-phaṇa* is accepted by both M. Guha and Krishnadasa Baba. *Vyadhikaraṇatayā*, literally "because of incongruity (of the subject)": both translators understand it as "because what was said seemed unbefitting"; cf. the Bhattacharya's explanation below — the Lord covered his mouth so that the most intimate secret would not be revealed.
[^07-28]: In GGM the speech is wrongly given to "the Lord" (*bhagavān*); according to KM and the sense, it is the Bhattacharya's. KM adds *bhagavatā*: "the Lord acknowledged…".
[^07-29]: So according to GGM: *na tena yati-bhūmikā bhavati no 'tivismāpanī*; M. Guha and Krishnadasa Baba read and understand it the same way (the form of a sannyasi is no marvel to us). KM: *na te nayati bhūmikā…*, an obscure, apparently corrupt reading.
[^07-30]: GGM *ākasmiko na vidhinā* ("not… by fate"), KM *ākasmiko nu*: translated according to KM, as a question.
[^07-31]: That is, Sarvabhauma's humility is a sign of his true greatness. *Payodhara*, "cloud" (literally "water-bearer"); *gambhīra*, both "deep" and "deeply rumbling".
[^07-32]: The Sattvatas: the ancient name of the Vaishnava Pancharatrikas, Vaishnavas.
[^07-33]: The Pashupatas: a Shaiva school (worshippers of Shiva Pashupati).
[^07-34]: KM adds: *sva-sva-matācārya-varyāṇām*, "of the best teachers of each one's own doctrine".
[^07-35]: GGM: *karhy eva syāma* ("when shall we be…?"); KM: *kva smaḥ*, "where were we?" Translated according to KM.
[^07-36]: GGM *tad etan namenam āśayāmaḥ* is a corruption; according to KM: *tad etad annam enam āśayāmaḥ*.
[^07-37]: In GGM wrongly ||20|| (a repeated number); in KM ॥२०॥, because there Malla Bhatta's stanza (here 20) is unnumbered in the OCR. The numbering further on is aligned: 21–26.
[^07-38]: Cf. the same story in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya-lila, ch. 9).
[^07-39]: A well-known stanza cited as from the *Padma Purāṇa* (the *Śata-nāma-stotra* of Rama); cf. the *Rāma-tāpanī Upaniṣad*.
[^07-40]: An etymology cited by the Gaudiya Vaishnavas with reference to the *Mahābhārata* (Udyoga-parva).
[^07-41]: Half of a stanza from the *Padma Purāṇa* (the words of Shiva to Parvati at the end of the *Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma*: "…*sahasra-nāma-tat-tulyaṁ rāma-nāma varānane*"). In KM stanzas 24–25 are combined under one number, ॥२३॥.
[^07-42]: A stanza cited as from the *Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa* (cf. the *Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta* of Rupa Gosvami).
[^07-43]: Panchavati is a place on the bank of the Godavari where Rama, Sita and Lakshmana lived in exile.
[^07-44]: A play: *kṛṣṇa-pakṣa-pātāḥ*, "partisans of Krishna", but also *kṛṣṇa-pakṣa*, "the dark half of the month".
[^07-45]: The voice behind the scenes (the temple servants) announces the time of the darshan of Jagannatha, but the King takes it as an omen (*vāk-śakuna*, "an omen in words") answering his question.
[^07-46]: A play on words: *Puruṣottama*, "the Supreme Person" (a name of Krishna, here of Chaitanya), and *Puruṣottama-kṣetra*, Puri.
[^07-47]: In GGM and in M. Guha this is a separate speech of the Bhattacharya (and so it is translated). In KM (by the OCR) these words seem to continue the King's speech ("…Bhattacharya, a pilgrim…").
[^07-48]: In GGM wrongly ||24|| (a repeated number); in KM ॥२४॥, with a different numbering of the preceding stanzas.
[^07-49]: *Ratnākara*, "the Abode of Jewels", the ocean. The two "moving jewel mountains" are Nilachala ("the Blue Mountain", i.e. Jagannatha) and Chaitanya, golden like Mount Sumeru.

---

# Act 8. Mercy to Prataparudra (Pratāparudrānugraha)[^08-1]

*The Lord settles in Puri with Kashi Mishra; Paramananda Puri, Svarupa Damodara, Govinda and Brahmananda Bharati come to Him; He refuses to see the King; the Gaudiya devotees headed by Advaita arrive; at the chariot festival King Prataparudra in simple dress clasps the Lord's feet.*

*(Enter Sri Krishna Chaitanya, accompanied by Sarvabhauma and others, and also by Damodara, Jagadananda and others.)*[^08-2]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Sarvabhauma! I have gone round so many lands — and have met no one like you. Only Raya Ramananda. But he is truly not of this world.

**Sarvabhauma.** Master! That is why I told You that he must certainly be seen.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** I have seen many Vaishnavas, but they too are merely worshippers of Narayana. Others are Tattvavadis, and they are of the same kind: their doctrine is not flawless.[^08-3] Many others are Shaivas. And the heretics, the pashandas, are a great multitude, and very strong. But, Bhattacharya, only the doctrine of Ramananda has pleased My heart.

**Sarvabhauma.** He has only entered into Your own doctrine; he is not himself the creator of a doctrine. Master! From now on for us too this doctrine is the most honoured, and it is established by all the scriptures.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Bhattacharya! Has thought been given to the place where the Lord will live?

**Bhattacharya.** Acharya! The King himself has thought of that.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(softly, to him alone)*. And what place is that?

**Bhattacharya.** The house of Kashi Mishra.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Splendid, splendid! It is near the Lion Gate; from there it will be easy to go for the darshan of Jagannatha.

*(With these words they enter the settlement of Purushottama from the seashore. Then enter the pashupalas — servants of the god Jagannatha — with garlands of the prasada of the Lord Jagannatha in their hands, and also Kashi Mishra and Pariksha Mahapatra.)*[^08-4]

**Sarvabhauma.** Here He is, here He is — the master, Sri Krishna Chaitanya! *(All approach eagerly.)* Master! These are the pashupalas of the Lord. This is the one called Kashi Mishra. And this is the chief superintendent of everything, the judge at the Lord's court.

*(Kashi Mishra and Pariksha Mahapatra, approaching, fall prostrate like rods. The pashupalas put a garland round His neck and bow.)*

**Bhagavan.** Ah, what is this? You are intimates of the Lord, and it is for Me to honour you. Why do you do what is not fitting? *(Bows to all and embraces them.)*

**Pashupalas.** Sarvabhauma! Now it is the time of the Lord's midday sleep. Should the master go straight there and wait, or rest, bathe and so on, and then come?

**Sarvabhauma.** Let Him bathe, and then visit the god.

**Kashi Mishra.** Then let them come this way. *(Leads them to his house.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Master! This is the Mishra's own dwelling; it has been cleaned and prepared for Your holy feet. So let the Lord enter here.

*(They act entering. The pashupalas and the others bow and go out. Then enter several notable persons from among the people of Utkala.)*

**The people of Utkala.**

> **1.** Then such good fortune did not fall to us, and so the supreme Compassion did not come onto the path of our eyes.
> But now our luck has come together: ah, we catch with our gazes this very Lord of the Blue Mountain — moving![^08-5]

*(With these words they approach.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Master! This is Janardana, a close servant who serves the Lord's body during the *anavasara*.[^08-6] This is Krishnadasa, an intimate who bears the golden staff. This is Shikhi Mahiti, in charge of the records, and these are his two brothers. This is the one called Dasa Mahasoara, in charge of the kitchen.[^08-7] All of them are natural devotees of the god Sri Jagannatha. These are Chandaneshvara, Murari and Hamseshvara,[^08-8] the best of brahmins, royal mahapatras, Vaishnavas by nature. This is Praharajya Mahapatra,[^08-9] a very great devotee of the Lord. This is Pradyumna Mishra, this is Vishnudasa. These are the brothers of Raya Ramananda, and among them, here, is Vaninatha Pattanayaka. And this is their father, Bhavananda Raya. And all these others, people of Gauda and Utkala, whose minds are in You and whose life is in You, fall prostrate like rods. Favour them with Your mercy as Your own.

**Bhagavan.** They are My very self; how could they be merely "Mine"? All the more the servants of Jagannatha.

**Sarvabhauma.** Master! In Jagannatha and in You the being of Krishna-Chaitanya is one and the same, without difference; and yet there is a certain distinction: He is Brahman in wood, and You are Brahman in a man.

**Bhagavan** *(covering His ears)*.

> **2.** This exaggeration of yours, Sarvabhauma, grates harshly on the ear:
> for if the juice of molasses is boiled down too much, it turns bitter and does not set.[^08-10]

**Sarvabhauma.** Master! The juice of the land of Gauda, however long it is boiled, is always sweet — for the Lord appeared there.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Enough, enough. Now it is the time for the darshan of Sri Pundarikaksha.[^08-11]

**All.** So be it. Let the Lord go ahead. *(Letting the Lord go first, they go round the stage.)*

**Behind the scenes.**

> **3.** Here ahead is the elixir of the eyes, the lord of Paramananda-puri:
> holding the staff to chastise those who heap up sins, he comes out at his time.[^08-12]

**All** *(having listened)*. Oh, it is the time of the god's coming out, since it is being announced.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(to Himself)*. Ah, Paramananda Puri the master is a disciple of the lord of sages Madhavendra Puri, into whom, they say, all the divine power of My elder brother Vishvarupa entered.[^08-13] Has he himself not come? Well, after seeing the god, I shall learn the truth of this.

**Sarvabhauma.** Here before us is the Lord's Paramananda-puri, the City of Supreme Bliss. Enter it, then.

*(All go out with the Lord to see Sri Jagannatha. Then enters Paramananda Puri.)*

**Paramananda Puri** *(eagerly)*.

> **4.** "When shall I see the Lord who has taken the body of a devotee?" — ah, by this ripe thirst my mind is stirred.
> After long waiting, today the season of fruit has come for me; but I do not know what fruit the tree of my fate will bear.

*(Going round the stage.)* Lord Sri Jagannatha, forgive me! That I go to Him first without looking at You — You, the all-knowing, know Yourself what this thirst is.

*(Looking around again.)* Ah, that Lord must be somewhere here, for —

> **5.** Even from the gate of Jagannatha a crowd of people flows here with loud noise and does not go back.
> Therefore I think: O blessed Earth, by your merit that very golden-shining God has appeared here — He will be here.

So let me approach. *(Approaches.)*

*(Then enters Sri Krishna Chaitanya with His companions, motionless with the bliss of the darshan of Sri Jagannatha.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(acting as if remembering)*. Ah, it seems Paramananda Puri the master will come now, for —

> **6.** Following the happiness of beholding the Lord, some further happiness must come now:
> a sudden clarity of mind announces a joy near at hand.

*(Stands in eager expectation.)*

**Puri** *(looking ahead)*. Ah, here He is —

> **7.** Glory to Him — motionless, without a single wave, from the rapture of relishing the rasa of beholding the Moon of the Blue Mountain, which He has drawn into Himself;
> down His chest, gleaming like a slab of gold and jewels, tears flow, and the hair on His body ceaselessly stands on end!

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(looking ahead)*. It is he — here is the cause of this sudden joy: he has come here. *(Rises and bows.)* Svamin! Are you the Puri master?

**Puri** *(in confusion)*. Master! I have come from Varanasi, pining only with the desire to see You.

*(Approaches.)*[^08-14]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Svamin, this way, this way. *(Offers him rest.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** O svamin! There is nothing surprising in this.

> **8.** Whatever rivers there are on earth — masculine or feminine — that carry their currents,
> not one of them anywhere finds rest and refuge except in the ocean, the treasury of pearls.[^08-15]

**Behind the scenes.**

> **9.** Ah, as if to become a teacher of rasa for the Lord, the knower of the arts of rasa, a body in the garb of a mendicant monk has taken form —
> for throughout the earth everyone calls him "Damodara-svarupa", that is, "the very form of Damodara", for love inseparable from Him.[^08-16]
