**Sarvabhauma.** Ah, what natural love (*rati*) the people here have for the Lord! Even in His absence all sing of His divinity.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(acting as if listening)*. Ah, I heard the name "Damodara-svarupa". It seems he too will soon come.

**Sarvabhauma.** Svamin! As a rule, when You descend, all Your associates descend — some before, others after. In due time all must surely meet. That is why it was said: "that carry their currents".

*(Enter Damodara-svarupa.)*[^08-17]

**Damodara-svarupa** *(fixing his gaze on the sky)*.

> **10.** O Sri Chaitanya, treasury of mercy! May Your mercy increase beyond measure —
> playfully shaking off grief, clear, revealing fragrance,
> stilling the disputes of the scriptures, bestowing rasa, instilling madness in the heart,
> ever finding joy in bhakti, intoxicating — the limit of sweetness![^08-18]

*(With these words he approaches.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking ahead, to himself)*. Ah, I have heard of a certain Damodara-svarupa, a disciple of Chaitanyananda — a very great renunciant, a devotee of the Lord, very learned — who, though his guru asked him many times, "Study Vedanta and teach it", never did so. On the contrary —

> **11.** He accepted the fourth ashrama only from the longing to give up everything;
> but out of passion for the pollen of the lotus feet of Sri Krishna, ah, he held even that as nothing, though he wore it.[^08-19]

That is he. I shall report to the Lord. *(Approaches.)* Master! Here, here is that Damodara-svarupa of whom You have heard.

**Bhagavan.** Where is he? Where is he? *(Eagerly goes to meet him.)*

*(Damodara-svarupa approaches and bows at His feet. Sri Krishna Chaitanya raises him with His hands and embraces him.)*

**Behind the scenes.**

> **12.** Devoted to the service of Ishvara Puri, and in himself a devotee of Krishna,
> here comes this man of pure heart, detached from all objects of the senses.

**Sarvabhauma** *(having listened)*. Oh, some attendant is coming from the Lord's town. Who can it be?[^08-20]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Ah, it seems someone is coming from the Puri master.[^08-21]

**Sarvabhauma** *(looking)*. We shall soon know. *(Gazes.)*

*(Then enters one named Govinda.)*

**Govinda** *(to himself)*. Ishvara Puri sent me: "Go straight to Mahaprabhu." Here I am close to Him, and yet I do not grasp the greatness of my good fortune. *(Approaches, bows and joins his palms.)* Svamin!

> **13.** "That form of His which I saw first — ah, in that alone is my bliss; in another garb I do not wish to see Him. So go to Him" —
> so the Puri master sent me to the border of Your feet.[^08-22]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** So it is: their love for Me is unchanging.

**Sarvabhauma.** You are his servant?

**Govinda.** Of course.

**Sarvabhauma.** Svamin! How did he accept a non-brahmin as a servant?

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Bhattacharya, do not speak so.

> **14.** Hari is independent, and so is His mercy: it looks neither to caste nor to lineage and the like.
> Rejecting the food of Suyodhana, God joyfully accepted only the food of Vidura.[^08-23]

**Sarvabhauma.** Master, so it is.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Well, although it is not fitting to make the servant of one's venerable elders serve oneself, since such is his command, so be it. *(Accepts him.)*

**Mukunda** *(entering hurriedly)*. Svamin! Brahmananda Bharati has come, wishing to see You. If You command, we shall bring him straight here.[^08-24]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** God forbid! For they are venerable. So it is I who must go.

**All.**

> **15.** Even the worldly conduct of the transcendental surely serves to reveal their transcendental nature:
> for the course of Vishnupadi over the earth leads the embodied to heaven.[^08-25]

*(Letting the Lord go first, they go round the stage. Then enters Brahmananda, dressed in a skin.)*

**Brahmananda** *(looking ahead)*. Here He is, Sri Krishna Chaitanya. And truly —

> **16.** With arms long, long like golden bolts, shining like a petal of a bright golden ketaka,
> whose lustre is caressed by a garland of fresh *damanaka*, with a charming gait — He comes out to meet me.[^08-26]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(approaches and, though He sees him, concealing His feelings, lets it be understood that the garment of skin does not please Him)*. Mukunda, where is he?

**Mukunda.** Why, here he is, here.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** No, no. If it were he, would he, putting on a skin, have become a slave of outward garb?[^08-27]

**Brahmananda** *(hearing, to himself)*. Ah, evidently it does not please Him that I am dressed in a skin. And indeed —

> **17.** A garment of skin and the like serves only to display hypocrisy, not as a means of reaching the Real.
> One who walks the earth by the straight path easily reaches the goal he strives for.

What is this for? *(Wants to throw off the skin.)*

*(Sri Krishna Chaitanya glances at Damodara. Damodara, understanding the hint, quickly offers an upper cloth. Brahmananda puts it on. Sri Krishna Chaitanya approaches and bows to him.)*

**Brahmananda** *(with fear and reverence)*. Svamin! Though for the instruction of people it befits You to act so, to us it gives great fear. Do not do it again. Look, look —

> **18.** The greatness of Nilachala is beyond the power of such as I to describe — so transcendental is it:
> for here now two Brahmans appear — the moving and the unmoving, the golden and the blue.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Since the word "now" denotes the present time, the "golden Brahman" is just you, who have recently come; besides, the word "brahma" forms part of your name.[^08-28]

**Brahmananda.** That is inferred from the relation of the pervaded and the pervader. And that I am the pervaded, let it be seen at least from my having left the skin at Your sign.[^08-29]

**Sarvabhauma.** The holy man speaks rightly.

**Brahmananda.** Sarvabhauma, look, look —

> **19.** "Golden in colour, with a body of gold, with beautiful limbs, wearing sandal armlets" —
> these names have attained their literal sense in Him alone.[^08-30]

And it is the Lord Sri Jagannatha Himself who makes Him "wearing sandal armlets" — with a cord anointed with the sandal of His prasada. Look, look: even a mere Deity of the Lord bestows supreme bliss — what then to speak of Bhagavan Sri Krishna Himself! Ah, a wonder —

> **20.** Ah, the form — dense bliss and consciousness — the sole means of tasting bliss: in those who gaze at it, it stills the agitation of the outer and inner senses.
> And those who, rejecting it, meditate in the heart on the formless for the sake of attaining bliss — I think, ah, some mighty evil tendency leads them astray.

And further —

> **21.** If the essence of the Lord is formlessness, why then, ah, are pride, envy and the rest, also formless, not counted as deity?
> There is no rule that He is necessarily with form or without form; but supreme bliss, and that from which it comes — that is the Lord: such is my view.

**Sarvabhauma.** Svamin, so it is. This is explained also at the sutra "Made of bliss, on account of repetition".[^08-31] He Himself is bliss and bestows bliss on others too, as one very rich gives wealth to others as well: the suffix *-maya* here denotes abundance. And if His mercy comes, then even from meditation on the formless one falls again into the sweetness of the divine form. As the knowing have said:

> **22.** We, who were worshipped by travellers on the path of non-duality, who were consecrated on the throne of the bliss of our own self,
> have been forcibly turned into slave-girls by a certain cheat — the lover of the cowherds' wives.[^08-32]

**Damodara** *(to Brahmananda)*. Holy one, I have invited you. So go now to what is to be done next.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Svamin, that is fitting.

**Brahmananda.** As you please. *(Goes out together with Damodara and several others.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Sarvabhauma! It is time for you too to go.

**Sarvabhauma.** Master, I must report something.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** What is it?

**Sarvabhauma.** Svamin, if You promise not to be angry, then I shall report it.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Speak without fear.

**Sarvabhauma.** The King is pining with the desire to see Your holy feet. If You allow it, we shall bring him.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(covering His ears)*. Sarvabhauma, you too say this!

> **23.** For one who has nothing, who is intent on the service of the Lord and wishes to reach the far shore of the ocean of existence,
> to see people devoted to the senses, and women — alas, alas! — is worse than eating poison.[^08-33]

**Sarvabhauma.** Svamin! That is truly so; but he is a servant of Jagannatha.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.**

> **24.** One should fear even the form of women and of people devoted to the senses:
> as a serpent agitates the mind, so does its image too.

If this is spoken of once more, I shall no longer be seen here.

*(Sarvabhauma stands silent.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Bhattacharya, it is already very late.

**Sarvabhauma.** As Svami pleases. *(Goes out.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Mukunda, where did Shripada Nityananda go when I went to the south?

**Mukunda.** To Gauda. And he said this: "Reckoning the time of the Lord's return, I shall come here again with everyone, headed by Advaita."

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Now there is neither war between two kingdoms nor other troubles;[^08-34] the road is easy, and the Gundicha festival is near — everything is ready for their coming. Only — has the news of Svami's return reached so far? But enough of doubts.

> **25.** Who in the world spreads the news of the sun, risen and dispelling the darkness with its rays, or of the moon?
> Such is the way of a thing that transcends the world: it reveals itself everywhere of itself.[^08-35]

So, svamin, it is the time of the evening incense offering (*dhūpa*) of the god Jagannatha, if You allow… *(Without finishing, he acts shyness.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Acharya, go and see the dhupa. And I shall go to converse with the Puri master and Svarupa. *(Bhagavan goes out.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Ah, the Lord has already gone. Then I too, having seen the dhupa, shall join Him there again. *(Takes a few steps.)*

**Behind the scenes.**

> **26.** The victorious drive of the chariot of the Lord of all is near — and here the ruler of the earth, Prataparudra, has arrived.
> Let me once more make efforts that he may see with his own eyes the bull among ascetics in His golden form.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Oh, the Bhattacharya's voice is heard; so the ruler of the earth, the Gajapati, has arrived. And the great chariot festival is already near. So I shall go and look at Jagannatha and come back. *(Goes out.)*

*(Then enters the Bhattacharya.)*

**Bhattacharya.** Since the Gajapati summoned me the very moment he arrived, he must be pining to see Sri Krishna Chaitanya. *(Goes round the stage and looks.)* Oh, here is the King. Let me approach. *(Approaches.)*

*(Then enter the King, seated on the throne, and the mahapatras.)*

**King** *(longingly, to himself)*.

> **27.** My cares for the kingdom have ceased to be my cares, the enjoyment of happiness has become a disease.
> If He does not look at me hereafter — alas, I shall not keep even my life.[^08-36]

**Sarvabhauma.** It seems the great King is troubled in his heart by something: I have approached, and he does not notice me. I shall announce myself. Victory, victory to the great King!

**King** *(acting as if coming to himself)*. What, is it you, Bhattacharya! Come near, come near! *(Bows.)*

*(Sarvabhauma, greeting him with blessings, sits down.)*

**King.** Bhattacharya, have you reported to the Lord Sri Krishna Chaitanya?

**Sarvabhauma.** Of course.

**King.** And what did He command?

**Sarvabhauma.** What shall I say?

**King** *(sorrowfully)*. I understood that already when you did not come to me yourself, joyfully, to tell me. Alas, woe is me!

> **28.** He looks even on the low-born, who should not even be looked at — but not on me.
> Can it be that this God has descended resolving: "I shall show mercy to all except him alone"?

*(Reflecting a moment.)* Listen, then —

> **29.** Well known is the vow of Him whose word is true; so, ah, now I too take a vow:
> either I shall part with my life, or — or, what am I to do? — I shall make the pair of His lotus feet a traveller on the road of my eyes.[^08-37]

*(Stands in tears.)*

**Sarvabhauma** *(to himself)*. The excess of his love has passed all measure. What is to be done?

> **30.** To go and tell Him again? Ah, no, that will not do: His resolve is dense — firm, heavy, long-drawn.
> And this one's great passion is almost invincible — broad, keen, mature: neither of them is able to conquer the other.[^08-38]

So now a stratagem is needed. *(Aloud.)* Great King! Be comforted, be comforted: there is a certain means by which the tree of your desire may bear fruit.

**King.** If you know it, tell it.

**Sarvabhauma.** Although the one who will arrange the meeting with Him is this messenger — your love itself — still our stratagem too may serve as a road.

**King.** Tell me, then, what stratagem?

**Sarvabhauma** *(softly, to him alone)*. Making love alone your messenger, all by yourself, putting off your royal garb, recognised by no one, on the day of the chariot festival of the god Jagannatha, when the Lord, to relieve the fatigue of the dancing play, goes into a secluded garden and His outer senses fade away from relishing bliss — approach suddenly and behold the Lord. Otherwise it will not come to pass.

**King** *(reassured, aside)*. So we shall do. But arrange it so that no one except you knows of it.

**Sarvabhauma.** So it shall be.

**Doorkeeper** *(entering)*. Sire! A spy has arrived in haste from the capital.

**King.** Let him enter.

**Doorkeeper** *(goes out quickly, brings him in and enters again)*. Sire, here he is.

**King.** Spy, tell me, why such alarm?

**Spy.** Sire!

> **31.** Many thousands of people have suddenly gathered beyond the Chitrotpala.
> Whether they are pilgrims or from an enemy army I do not know: as soon as I heard the noise I hurried here.[^08-39]

**Sarvabhauma.** Certainly pilgrims; otherwise the news would have come earlier. From this we conclude that they are Gaudiyas — the beloved associates of the Lord Sri Krishna Chaitanya. Well, the Lord will have a great gathering of those who surpass all in sweet conversations.

*(Behind the scenes, noise.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Great King! They have indeed already come to the bank of the Narendra-sarovara — for this joyful clamour is heard.

**King.** So it is.

**Sarvabhauma.** Great King, if you please, go up to the upper terrace and look at the spectacle.

**King.** As you please. *(Goes up to the terrace and looks at the road.)* Sarvabhauma! Who is that running hurriedly to meet the pilgrims with a garland of the remnants of the Lord's offerings?

**Sarvabhauma.** That is Damodara, a beloved associate of the Lord Chaitanya. Evidently the Lord, hearing of the arrival of Advaita and other dear friends, has sent him to meet them with honour, offering a garland of prasada, and to bring them.

**King.** So there are some there who have been favoured with the mercy of the Lord Chaitanya?

**Sarvabhauma.** Of course! Otherwise would it be so? Have Gopinatha Acharya called: he knows them all.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(entering, tossing aside the curtain)*. Here I am. Let the King command what is to be done.

**King.** Sarvabhauma, give the orders.

**Sarvabhauma.** You know them all; so name each one for us.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Very well. *(Approaches.)*

*(Behind the scenes, the sankirtana of the names of Hari is heard.)*

**Sarvabhauma** *(having listened)*.

> **32.** This sound of sankirtana at first, while the words and meaning were not yet distinguished, only gladdened the ear;
> then, when the words began to be made out, it became something else, and when the meaning was grasped, it became something else again.[^08-40]

**King** *(listening intently)*. Such art of kirtana I have never seen anywhere.

**Sarvabhauma.** It is the creation of the Lord Krishna Chaitanya.

**King.** Acharya! And who is the one to whom he offered the Lord's garland?

**Gopinatha Acharya.** I shall name each one. This is Advaita, this is Nityananda.

**Sarvabhauma.** Him I recognise.

**King.** Why does he walk apart, with only a few people?

**Sarvabhauma.** He is indifferent to everything and does not seek the company of others.[^08-41]

**Gopinatha Acharya.** This is Shrivasa. Here, this is Vakreshvara. This is Acharya-ratna. Here, this is Vidyanidhi.

**Sarvabhauma.** These two I saw in my childhood.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** This is Haridasa. Here, this is Gadadhara. This is Murari. These are Shrivasa's brothers. This is Gangadasa. This is Nrisimha Acharya.[^08-42] And these others are people of Navadvipa; I have not seen them before either. If you allow me, I shall find out and come back.

**King.** Do so.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** As the King pleases. *(Quickly goes round the stage, goes out behind the scenes and at once enters again.)* Sarvabhauma! This is Acharya Purandara. This is Hari Bhatta. This is Raghava. This is Narayana. This is Kamalananda. This is Kashishvara. This is Vasudeva, Mukunda's elder brother. This is Shivananda. And this is another Narayana. This is Vallabha. This is Shrikanta. Why say more? All of them are associates of Sri Chaitanya; there is not a single ordinary pilgrim among them.

**King.** How is it that they, leaving the temple of Jagannatha behind, go first of all to the abode of Sri Chaitanya-Krishna?

**Sarvabhauma.** In this lies the greatness of natural love.

**King** *(again looking in another direction)*. Oh, why is Vaninatha, Ramananda's younger brother, hurrying to them with an abundance of mahaprasada?

**Sarvabhauma.** He knows the heart of the Lord Sri Chaitanya. Without a word from Him he is going to treat them to mahaprasada.

**King.** Bhattacharya! There is the injunction: "Shaving of the head and fasting — such is the rule at all holy places." Will they, transgressing it, take mahaprasada today?

**Sarvabhauma.** Sire! That is another path. That is the Lord's command given in His absence, while this is face to face. Moreover, the food of Jagannatha's prasada the Lord makes His own prasada with His own hand — what is there to dispute? And it is said thus:

> **33.** When the Lord, on whom one meditates in the heart, shows someone mercy,
> he abandons the mind fixed on the worldly and on the Vedic.[^08-43]

And further: according to the saying "That is action which pleases Hari", their aim is only His pleasure, not the fruit of pilgrimage.[^08-44]

**King.** So it is. But tell me, when is the chariot festival? The counsel you gave me has sunk into my heart, and now even a moment drags on for me like a kalpa.

**Sarvabhauma.** The day after tomorrow.

**King.** Ho, who is there! Call Pariksha Mahapatra and Kashi Mishra.

**Someone** *(entering and bowing)*. Sire, as you command. *(Goes out, brings both and enters again.)* Sire, they have come.

**King.** Mahapatra! In everything concerning the festive drive of the god Jagannatha, carry out whatever this Kashi Mishra, who knows the heart of the Lord Chaitanya, commands — as my own command.

**Mahapatra.** As the King commands.

**King.** Mishra! And you, day after day, fulfil the wishes of the Lord Chaitanya.

**Mishra.** That is my cherished desire.

**King.** And further: however many Gaudiyas come, arrange it so that they too may see the Lord without hindrance.

**Both.** As the King commands. *(Exeunt.)*

**King.** Bhattacharya! Go nearer and watch the joy of their mutual conversations. If I too had the right to it, would I remain deprived of such supreme bliss?[^08-45] And I shall see to what must and must not be done for the coming victorious drive of the chariots. *(Goes out.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** The King has commanded just what I desired. I shall do so at once. *(Walking a few steps with Gopinatha Acharya.)* Ah, ahead —

> **34.** Deeply rumbling with blissful cries of "hum!", with waves of tandava raised by the wind of rapture,
> bearing beauty — the moving ocean of devotion to Hari surpasses the unmoving ocean.[^08-46]

Let us approach. *(They approach.)*

*(Then enter all who have been mentioned, headed by Advaita.)*

**Advaita** *(looking ahead)*. Damodara! Who is this coming with yet another garland?

**Damodara.** That is Govinda, who attends on the Lord.

*(Govinda, entering hurriedly, offers the garland. Advaita receives it respectfully.)*

**Damodara.** Here, here is the abode of Kashi Mishra. Enter.

*(Advaita and the others act entering.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Ah, a wonder!

> **35.** As at the end of the yuga, in the womb of God, light as a leaf, all these hosts of universes find room,
> so here, in the small abode of the Mishra, hundreds and thousands of people, finding a place, fit in, each in his own place.[^08-47]

*(Looking ahead.)* Oh, here He is —

> **36.** Surpassing the limit of the jubilation born of the rising of the moon Advaita, the ocean of nectar, Sri Chaitanya, seems to heave with high waves.
> Full of bliss, unchanging, ever lofty, indivisible — how, ah, does He receive still further increase even from partial joys?[^08-48]

*(Then enter, as indicated, Sri Chaitanya, the Puri master, Svarupa and the others. Sri Krishna Chaitanya, approaching, bows to Nityananda and embraces Advaita. Advaita, bowing, embraces Him in return.)*

**Sarvabhauma** *(gazing)*.

> **37.** Glory to this mutual embrace of Advaita and Chaitanya — two royal elephants in the forest of love,
> intoxicated with love for each other, nimbly clashing their trunk-arms, loudly trumpeting,
> abundantly sprinkling each other with streams of tears as with musk-fluid![^08-49]

*(All here and there fall prostrate on the ground like rods. The Lord favours all — embraces, converses, bestows His glance. Advaita introduces those whom the Lord has not seen before; but Sri Krishna Chaitanya Himself calls by name even those whom He has never seen.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Ah, here is a marvel!

> **38.** "Are you well, Raghava? Is all well, Vasudeva? Is all dear to you?
> Ho, Narayana! Is all in order, Shivananda? Are you prosperous, Shankara?
> Kamalananda and Kashishvara, is all good with you? Shrikanta, is all in order? Peace to Narayana!"[^08-50]

And further —

> **39.** With such affectionate words, sweet and tender, the Lord softly addresses even those He has not seen before, as old acquaintances.
> What is this — His divine power, or His former, all-knowing love?

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.**

> **40.** Today I have a great festival; and tomorrow or the day after, another festival of the Moon of Nilachala, called Gundicha.
> Although both, ah, equally cause bliss to flow in the heart, this festival, where Advaita is revealed, is especially delightful to Me.[^08-51]

*(With these words He adorns each one — Advaita and the others — with garlands and sandal of the prasada of the Lord Jagannatha, and with His own hand gives each a little of the prasada food.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Now I should not approach: seeing me, they may pass into another mood. These two days have almost passed, and the King has charged me with gathering everything needed for the festival. So I shall go there now. Acharya, you stay here. *(Goes out.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(approaching)*. Victory, victory to Mahaprabhu!

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Ah, it is you, Acharya! Come near, bow to Advaita.

*(Gopinatha Acharya does so.)*

**Advaita** *(embracing him)*. I know you — you are Visharada's son-in-law.[^08-52]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** That is why he is so very distinguished.[^08-53] Acharya, together with Vaninatha arrange lodging for everyone wisely.

**Gopinatha Acharya.** As the Lord commands. *(Goes out.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Vasudeva! Although Mukunda is My old companion, you, though I see you today for the first time, have long been dear to Me.

**Vasudeva** *(humbly)*. Master! What am I, insignificant! But Mukunda has long been favoured by You. The time of the Lord's mercy is the time of birth; therefore, though he is younger, he is my elder brother.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Shivananda! I know that you are very attached to Me.

**Shivananda.**

> **41.** For me, long drowning in the ocean of existence, You, O Infinite One, have been found like a shore;
> and You, Lord, have now found this unsurpassed vessel for Your mercy.[^08-54]

*(With these words he falls to the ground.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Raghava! You are the object of My great love.

*(Raghava, answering without words, falls prostrate.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Svarupa! Although this Shankara is Damodara's younger brother, yet to Me… *(Without finishing, He looks at Damodara.)*[^08-55]

**Damodara.** Master! For me this is great happiness. Finish the rest of Your words.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Svarupa! For Damodara I have an attachment mixed with reverence, but for this one a love without any admixture. So let him live here, with you. Govinda, you too be kind to him.

**Both.** As the master commands.

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(entering hurriedly)*. Svamin! Everything has been arranged as You commanded. Especially for Gadadhara a fine place has been found near Yameshvara — for all time.[^08-56]

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Advaita! This Puri master is like a second Munindra;[^08-57] for he is the beloved disciple of your guru. Bow to him, then.

*(Advaita does so. All bow too.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Acharya Advaita! And this here is Svarupa. By the interpretation "he who has his own form" he is always unchanging; know that he is My very heart.[^08-58]

**Advaita.** So it is. *(Bows. All bow.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Master, allow them to go and rest.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Acharya, tell them yourself. How can I say "go"?

*(Advaita and the others, understanding the hint, go out.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** Svamin Puri master! Loving Svarupa! Today I am full.

**Svarupa.** Svamin!

> **42.** The Lord, though full in Himself, is filled by His associates:
> the full moon too, the lord of the night, is empty without the stars.[^08-59]

Come, then: evening has come. Without You the Puri master has not yet accepted alms either.

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** So it is. *(Exeunt.)*

**Behind the scenes.**

> **43.** Shatadhriti too has lost his firmness, and the thousand-eyed has become quite blind —
> from the thirst to behold the drive of the chariot of the Moon of the Blue Mountain.[^08-60]

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(having listened)*. Ah, two days have flown by like a moment: here is the Bhattacharya starting to speak of the chariot festival. I shall go and look. *(Walking a little.)* Ah, a wonder!

> **44.** Like the three Vedas embodied, like the three eyes of Shambhu,
> like the three streams of the heavenly river — ahead gleam three chariots.[^08-61]

**Again behind the scenes.**

> **45.** Today the day of the chariot festival of the god, the lord of Nilachala, has come; today before Him the golden Hari will dance in His own bliss;
> today, when He has finished dancing, He will rest in the jasmine grove — ah, today this desire of one such as I will be fulfilled!

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Oh, it is like the voice of the great King Gajapati; I must listen carefully. *(Gazing.)* And indeed: the great King, conversing with the Bhattacharya, is pining with impatience. Let me see why they delay in raising Jagannatha onto the chariot. *(Looks.)*

*(Behind the scenes, the thunder of kahala trumpets and the rest.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(gazing)*. Ah!

> **46.** Like the light in the heart of those absorbed in samadhi, like the hot sun on the peak of the mountain of sunrise,
> the Lord of the Blue Mountain mounts the chariot — a beauty that is an elixir for all eyes.

*(Gazing again.)*

> **47.** Surrounded by hosts of all His friends headed by Advaita, and by other singers headed by Sri Svarupa,
> with many intimates immersed in nectar, headed by Sri Vakreshvara —
> this ocean of love, the golden king of ascetics, dances here and dances without end.[^08-62]

*(Behind the scenes, noise.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(joyfully)*.

> **48.** The throngs of chariot-pullers, called Gaudas, hold it with their left hands, as if playfully dragging a bundle of thick ropes —
> and the chariot, ah, obedient only to its own will, now stands and stands, and now suddenly rushes at full speed,
> now runs and runs, and now, ah, stands more motionless than before.[^08-63]

*(Again behind the scenes, the roar of sankirtana.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(gazing with curiosity)*.

> **49.** When Jagannatha moves off, Gaura draws back before Him; when Jagannatha stops, Gaura steps forward before Him.
> So the two Gods, full of playfulness and longing for each other, seem to start a game — the Moon of Nilachala and the jewel Lord.[^08-64]

*(Gazing again.)* Ah —

> **50.** When the Lord, the Lord of the world, has stopped at the pavilion of Balagandi, the king of ascetics, having finished the dance,
> goes into the garden with His loving associates to rest from His extreme fatigue. Glory to Him![^08-65]

From the Bhattacharya's sign I have understood that now the King too must come there secretly, in disguise. So I too shall hurry. *(Approaches.)*

*(Then enters Sri Krishna Chaitanya — motionless, experiencing the bliss of the dance, with closed eyes, a delight to the eyes; He sits in a garden bower, stretching out the trembling pair of His lotus feet with shanks like lotus stalks; His chest is bathed in tears flowing from His eyes; He is like the very embodiment of the bliss of love. His associates sit silently, each at the foot of a tree.)*[^08-66]

**Bhagavan.** "And therefore the swans take refuge at Your lotus feet, pouring forth bliss, O Lotus-eyed One…"[^08-67] *(With closed eyes He repeats only this half-verse again and again.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking)*. Ah, how great is the relishing of the bliss of love! Savouring, the Lord relishes the fact that the bliss He experienced during the dance — the bliss of directly beholding the Lord Krishna — is more wondrous even than the bliss of Brahman. "And" (*atha*) — that is, upon completing the understanding of all that the scriptures, high and low, teach. "Therefore" (*ataḥ*) — because it is more wondrous even than the bliss of Brahman. "The swans" — skilled in discriminating essence from non-essence — "take refuge at the lotus feet". Why? "Pouring forth bliss" — by this the greatness of the bliss He Himself has tasted is indicated. *(Looking around.)* Ah, here too are all the greatest devotees. And truly —

> **51.** Motionless, with bright radiance, with lovely flames, full of the oil of love, dispelling darkness,
> at the foot of every tree these great souls shine in a beautiful state — like lamps at an auspicious great festival in a windless place.[^08-68]

Well, I shall sit quietly somewhere here and wait for the King to enter. *(Does so.)*

*(Then enters the King, having taken off his royal garb, dressed in a pair of washed garments.)*

**King** *(agitated)*.

> **52.** Longing, overpowering the two strong ones — fear and deliberation — shakes me greatly; and my feet — alas, woe! — how they stiffen!
> Ho, fate! Today your test will surely be a test of my very life; but to my life I am not attached at all.[^08-69]

*(Very, very slowly goes round the stage.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(looking at the King)*. Ah, a wonder!

> **53.** All that is royal in him is his majesty alone; he is ahead, like the heroic rasa asleep;
> seized by bliss, by doubt, by fear and by deliberation, he sets down his lotus foot with difficulty.[^08-70]

*(All around — to themselves.)* Ah, it is King Prataparudra, his hand sealed with the auspicious cord![^08-71] How is it that he, having taken the guise of an ascetic, suddenly approaches here? Svami will be disturbed. Let us see what he will do.

*(The King, slowly looking about, suddenly approaches and with his arms, long like iron bars, tightly clasps the trembling lotus feet of the Lord.)*

**Sarvabhauma** *(looking)*.[^08-72] Ah, here is great trouble! The Lord, with His lotus eyes closed, helpless in the rapture of His own bliss, did not notice him — and he has seized the Lord's lotus feet. We do not know what will happen now.

**Bhagavan** *(still in His bliss, with closed eyes, without looking, tightly embracing him)*.

> **54.** Who, O King, among those possessing senses, surrounded on every side by death, would not serve the lotus feet of Mukunda,
> worshipped by the best of the immortals?[^08-73]

*(Repeats this again and again.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya.** Ah, a marvel!

> **55.** Boldness sometimes serves for good and sometimes turns into a vice;
> but what the King has accomplished by boldness is not attained even by all austerities.

*(Gazing again.)*

> **56.** He by whom great wrestlers, squeezed by his broad arms and chest, were left powerless, as if with broken bones —
> he himself, squeezed by the Lord's arms, has become like a plantain trunk encircled by the trunk of an intoxicated royal elephant.[^08-74]

*(Behind the scenes, noise. The Lord, leaving the King and hurrying, since from the noise He understands that the chariot is moving off, again goes out, wishing to see Jagannatha, still in the same bliss. All, each as is fitting, go out after Him.)*

**Gopinatha Acharya** *(approaching)*. Great King! God has gone to see Jagannatha. Now it is time for you too to go.

*(With these words he goes out, leading away the King, motionless with bliss. Exeunt all.)*

*Thus ends the eighth act of the* Sri Chaitanya-chandrodaya, *called "Mercy to Prataparudra".*

[^08-1]: The title follows the colophon of the Kāvyamālā edition: *pratāparudrānugraho nāma aṣṭamo 'ṅkaḥ*; the act is named likewise in M. Guha ("Pratāparudra-anugraha") and in Krishnadasa Baba. The electronic text has *pratāparudrāgrahaḥ*, a corruption.
[^08-2]: Svarupa Damodara appears only below; here Damodara Pandita is probably meant.
[^08-3]: The Tattvavadis are the followers of Madhva (cf. the meeting with them during the southern pilgrimage, Act 7).
[^08-4]: *Paśupāla* (literally "herdsman") is here apparently a class of temple servants of Jagannatha. Pariksha Mahapatra (Oriya "Paricha") is the chief superintendent of the temple. In the Kāvyamālā edition the pashupalas and Kashi Mishra with Pariksha are separated by the stage direction differently, with no difference in sense.
[^08-5]: "Then", i.e. during the Lord's first stay in Puri, before the southern pilgrimage. *Jaṅgama*, "moving", in contrast to the unmoving wooden form of Jagannatha (the "Lord of the Blue Mountain", Nilachala).
[^08-6]: *Anavasara*, the period after the bathing festival (Snana-yatra), when Jagannatha is hidden from view "by illness" and is served only by special servants.
[^08-7]: *Dāsa-mahāsoāra*: Oriya *mahāsuāra*, the "head cook" of the temple kitchen; "Dasa" is a name or by-name.
[^08-8]: In the Kāvyamālā edition, Simheshvara (*siṁheśvara*).
[^08-9]: *Prahara-rājya-mahāpātra* (Kāvyamālā *prahara-rāja-*) is apparently the title of a royal official; the interpretation is unclear.
[^08-10]: A play on words: *guḍa* is "molasses, cane sugar", while *gauḍa* is both "of molasses" and "Gaudiya, Bengali". The Kāvyamālā edition has plainly *gauḍasya rasasya*, "the juice of molasses / the 'rasa' of Gauda"; the end of the line there is *na caiti baddham* ("does not set"), the electronic text has *randham*. Sarvabhauma replies, playing on the sense "Bengali".
[^08-11]: Pundarikaksha, "the Lotus-eyed", i.e. Jagannatha.
[^08-12]: A double meaning: "the lord of the City of Supreme Bliss" (*paramānanda-purīśvara*) is Jagannatha, who comes out to the people at the hour of the temple's opening and chastises sinners; but the sannyasi Paramananda Puri with his monastic staff (*daṇḍa*) is also so called. Chaitanya understands the cry in the second sense, and Sarvabhauma then calls the temple "the Lord's Paramananda-puri".
[^08-13]: The electronic text has *agajasya*, an obvious misprint; the Kāvyamālā edition *agrajasya*, "of the elder brother". Vishvarupa is Chaitanya's elder brother, who accepted sannyasa.
[^08-14]: In the Kāvyamālā edition here: "**Sri Krishna Chaitanya.** I am favoured. *(Approaches.)* **Jagadananda.** Svamin, this way, this way. *(Offers him rest.)*", i.e. the last words belong to Jagadananda.
[^08-15]: *Nada* and *nadī* are rivers of masculine and feminine gender; an image of the associates flowing together to Chaitanya, the ocean. Below, Sarvabhauma interprets the word *sa-pravāhāḥ*, "with their currents", as a hint at the associates descending, some before, others after.
[^08-16]: A play on the name: *dāmodara-svarūpa*, "the very form of Damodara (Krishna)". Sarvabhauma understands the verse as a glorification of Chaitanya Himself, while Chaitanya hears in it the name of an expected associate. The electronic text has *bādhita*, the Kāvyamālā edition *vyadhita* ("created"); the latter is adopted.
[^08-17]: The stage direction is in the Kāvyamālā edition; it is missing in the electronic text.
[^08-18]: The verse is built on a continuous rhyme in *-dayā* ("mercy" and an instrumental ending), including *rasa-dayā*, "bestowing rasa", and *sa-madayā*, "intoxicating".
[^08-19]: Svarupa, according to tradition, took sannyasa without formal initiation, keeping the brahmachari name "Svarupa". The Kāvyamālā edition has *vairāgya-vaśena*, "under the sway of renunciation", instead of *vaiyāgrya-veśena*, and *vadann api*, "though he spoke of it", instead of *vahann api*.
[^08-20]: The Kāvyamālā edition adds: "Is it not Pariksha Mahapatra's deputy? No, that one is not so detached."
[^08-21]: Here "the Puri master" is Ishvara Puri, Chaitanya's guru (not Paramananda Puri).
[^08-22]: Ishvara Puri, who had given Chaitanya initiation, saw Him still as a householder; he does not wish to see Him in the garb of a sannyasi and therefore sends his servant. In the Kāvyamālā edition Govinda's first words ("Ishvara Puri sent me…") are set out as a separate quoted verse.
[^08-23]: Suyodhana is Duryodhana. Krishna, coming as envoy to Hastinapura, refused Duryodhana's hospitality and ate at Vidura's (*Mahābhārata*, Udyoga-parva).
[^08-24]: In the electronic text Mukunda's speech breaks off at the address; it is restored from the Kāvyamālā edition; the same text is in M. Guha.
[^08-25]: Vishnupadi is the Ganga, flowing from the foot of Vishnu; descending to earth, she raises people to heaven.
[^08-26]: *Damanaka*, a fragrant plant (artemisia) from which garlands were woven.
[^08-27]: In the Kāvyamālā edition: *bāhya-veśāveśa-vaśatvam*, "subjection to infatuation with outward garb".
[^08-28]: Chaitanya deflects the praise: "the newly arrived golden Brahman" is Brahmananda himself, whose name contains the word "brahma".
[^08-29]: *Vyāpya-vyāpaka*, the logical relation of "pervaded and pervader" (e.g. smoke and fire): Brahmananda acknowledges himself as subordinate and Chaitanya as the all-pervading Brahman, and as proof refers to his having obeyed His sign.
[^08-30]: Names from the *Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma* (*Mahābhārata*, Anushasana-parva): *suvarṇa-varṇo hemāṅgo varāṅgaś candanāṅgadī*. *Sānvayatva*, "agreement with the etymology".
[^08-31]: *Brahma-sūtra* 1.1.12 (*ānandamayo 'bhyāsāt*). There follows a grammatical interpretation of the suffix *mayaṭ* in the sense of "abundance" (and not of "modification").
[^08-32]: The verse is cited as a well-known quotation; it is found also in Rupa Gosvami's *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (3.1.44, in the common numbering). The speakers are former Advaitins; "slave-girls" (*dāsī*) is feminine in the original.
[^08-33]: Verses 23 and 24 are quoted in connection with this same episode in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* of Krishnadasa Kaviraja (Madhya 11.8 and 11.11); there too (11.47) verse 28 is quoted.
[^08-34]: *Dvairājya*, "the rule of two kings", i.e. war between two kingdoms (Orissa and Gauda), which made the road dangerous.
[^08-35]: The electronic text has *vidhura*, the Kāvyamālā edition *vidhūya* ("dispelling"); the latter is adopted.
[^08-36]: In the electronic text the negative is missing (*dhārayiṣye*); according to the Kāvyamālā edition, *na dhārayiṣye*, which the metre also requires.
[^08-37]: *Pakṣa* is both "vow, side" and a logical "thesis": the King, as it were, puts forward his own condition in a dispute with Chaitanya.
[^08-38]: A piling up of abstract nouns (*draḍhiman, gariman, drāghiman; prathiman, paṭiman, prauḍhiman*) — a deliberate play of sounds. The Kāvyamālā edition has *prathita-*, "famed", instead of *prathima-*. The last words (*kaścit kam api na vijetuṁ prabhavati*) we understand as "neither can overcome the other".
[^08-39]: The Chitrotpala is the river Mahanadi, the boundary of Prataparudra's dominions on the side of Bengal (on the way from Gauda to Puri). *Paracakra*, "a foreign army".

[^08-40]: M. Guha's edition reads *'vibhakta-sarvārtha* (so too the electronic text: *avibhakta-*) and *śravaṇa-pramodī*, "gladdening the ear"; in Guha's commentary: at first neither words nor tune nor rhythm are distinguishable, and his Bengali translation understands the verse in the same way. The Kāvyamālā edition: *'vibhakta-śabdārtha*, the same in sense. *Śaraṇa-pramodī* of the electronic text is a misprint for *śravaṇa-*.
[^08-41]: *Sarvādṛtatvāt* can be understood both as "being honoured by all" (*sarva-ādṛta*) and as "caring for nothing" (*sarva-adṛta*); the second agrees better with the image of Nityananda the avadhuta and with his "not seeking the company of others".
[^08-42]: Probably Nrisimhananda (Pradyumna Brahmachari) or another Nrisimha; the identification is unclear.
[^08-43]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 4.29.46 (the electronic text gives the reference 4.29.47; the numbering of verses differs between editions).
[^08-44]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 4.29.49: *tat karma hari-toṣaṁ yat*.
[^08-45]: *Sati tādṛśe 'dhikāre mayaiva tādṛśa-paramānanda-bhogād avañcitena kathaṁ bhavitavyam*: an unreal condition — the King does not yet have such a right (of access to the Lord). So M. Guha's Bengali translation and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understand it; that is why the King sends Sarvabhauma in his place.
[^08-46]: *Lāvaṇya*, "beauty" and "saltiness" (from *lavaṇa*, "salt"): the ocean of bhakti "bears salt" just as the real ocean does. The unmoving ocean is the ordinary sea (at Puri; in M. Guha's commentary *sindhum* = *samudram*), the "moving" one is the kirtana procession: Guha explains *calaḥ* as "flowing with a stream of beauty".
[^08-47]: An allusion to the vision of Markandeya (*Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 12.9): at the end of the yuga he sees an infant on a banyan leaf and enters its womb, in which all the worlds abide. The Kāvyamālā edition has *brahmāṇḍaka-samudayād eva vapuṣaḥ … mānti*, "find room"; the electronic text has *yānti sam*; *mānti* is adopted.
[^08-48]: A play on words: *advaita* is the name of Advaita and "non-duality"; *akhaṇḍa*, "indivisible", is contrasted with *khaṇḍa-ānanda*, "partial joys" (possibly also a hint at *khaṇḍa*, "sugar", and at the devotees from Shrikhanda). The ocean heaves at the rising of the moon.
[^08-49]: *Dāna* is both "gift" and the musk-fluid (*mada*) flowing from an elephant's temples in rut.
[^08-50]: All the greetings are synonyms of "well-being" (*kṣema, śiva, priya, kalyāṇa, bhavya, bhadra, kuśala, svasti*); *śivam* plays on the name of Shivananda.
[^08-51]: A play on words: *advaita-prakaṭana*, "the manifestation of Advaita" and "the manifestation of non-duality". The second half of the verse is corrupt in the electronic text (*hetu-garbha-viśeṣāṇaṁ*); translated according to the Kāvyamālā edition (M. Guha reads and understands it likewise): *tulyau yadyapy ahaha hṛdayānanda-niḥsyanda-hetū yatrādvaita-prakaṭanam asāv utsavo me pramodī*.
[^08-52]: Maheshvara Visharada is the father of Sarvabhauma; Gopinatha Acharya was married to his daughter.
[^08-53]: In the Kāvyamālā edition: *svata eva mahattaro 'yam*, "he is very great in himself as well".
[^08-54]: A verse from the *Stotra-ratna* of Yamunacharya (21 in the electronic text; in other editions the numbering may differ).
[^08-55]: Damodara here is Damodara Pandita, the elder brother of Shankara Pandita, not Svarupa Damodara (to whom the speech is addressed).
[^08-56]: Yameshvara is a temple of Shiva in Puri; near it, according to tradition, Gadadhara settled (the site of Tota-Gopinatha). In the Kāvyamālā edition (OCR) *mameśvarasya*, apparently an error.
[^08-57]: "The lord of sages" is Madhavendra Puri, the guru of Advaita and of Paramananda Puri.
[^08-58]: A play on the name "Svarupa": *svaṁ rūpam asyāsti*, "he has his own form", i.e. he does not change.
[^08-59]: In the Kāvyamālā edition *aṁśubhir vinā*, "without rays", instead of *uḍubhir vinā*, "without stars".
[^08-60]: Shatadhriti ("Hundredfold firm") is Brahma; "the thousand-eyed" is Indra. A play: the firm one loses his firmness, the many-eyed one goes blind.
[^08-61]: The three chariots are those of Jagannatha, Balarama and Subhadra; "the heavenly river" is the Ganga, flowing in the three worlds in three streams.
[^08-62]: In the Kāvyamālā edition *sukhāviṣṭa*, "immersed in happiness", instead of *sudhāviṣṭa*, "immersed in nectar".
[^08-63]: *Gauḍākhyair ratha-karṣibhiḥ*, "by the chariot-pullers called Gaudas". The commentary in M. Guha's edition explains *gauḍākhyāḥ* by the local word "kalapithiya" (cf. Oriya *kālabeṭhiā*, the hereditary servants who pull the chariots in Puri); but Guha's own Bengali translation and Krishnadasa Baba's Hindi translation understand "Gaudiyas" (Bengali devotees). The translation keeps both possibilities. The chariot moves only by its own will, and the people merely pretend to pull it.
[^08-64]: The electronic text has *nīlācalendra-maṇīśvarau*, "the Moon of Nilachala and the jewel Lord"; the Kāvyamālā edition *-munīśvarau*, "… and the lord of ascetics" (i.e. Chaitanya), which is clearer.
[^08-65]: Balagandi is the stopping place of the chariots on the way to the Gundicha temple, where food is offered to Jagannatha.
[^08-66]: In the Kāvyamālā edition *nṛtyānanda* → *hṛdayānanda* and *premānandaḥ* → *paramānandaḥ*.
[^08-67]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.29.3 (the words of Uddhava), the first half. Gopinatha then comments on it word by word.
[^08-68]: Every epithet has two meanings — of the devotees and of lamps: *suśikhāḥ*, "with lovely flames" and "with fine brahminical tufts (*śikhā*)"; *sneha*, "oil" and "love"; *tamas*, "darkness" and "ignorance"; *daśā*, "wick" and "state"; "motionless" — in the windless place and in ecstasy.
[^08-69]: The Kāvyamālā readings are adopted: *utkaṇṭhā bhaya-tarkayor* (the electronic text has *utkaṇṭhā-bhara-tarkayor*) and *prāyaḥ parīkṣā* (the electronic text has *prāya-parīkṣā*, which might also be understood as "the test of death by fasting").
[^08-70]: The heroic rasa (*vīra-rasa*) is the feeling befitting a king; here it is "asleep", having given way to humility.
[^08-71]: *Maṅgala-sūtra*, the protective cord (*kaṅkaṇa*) tied on the hand at a vow before a festival; at the chariot festival the King performs the duties of a servant (sweeping before the chariot).
[^08-72]: So in the electronic text; Sarvabhauma, however, had left earlier. In the Kāvyamālā edition the speech belongs to "all" (*sarve*), which is more logical.
[^08-73]: *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* 11.2.2 (the words of Narada to King Vasudeva; the address "O King" in the mouth of Chaitanya unwittingly applies to Prataparudra). In the electronic text the verse is unnumbered; the number 54 follows the Kāvyamālā edition.
[^08-74]: The electronic text has *ajani*, "became", the Kāvyamālā edition *bhavati*; the beginning of the verse is damaged in the Kāvyamālā OCR.

---

# Act 9. The Journey to Mathura (Śrī-mathurā-gamana)

*A pair of Kinnaras tell of the three kinds of the Lord's mercy; Prataparudra learns from messengers of Chaitanya's journey through Gauda to Vrindavana, of the meetings with Rupa and Sanatana, and receives Him on His return to Nilachala.*

*(Enter a pair of Kinnaras.)*

**Man.** My dear! This year the Gundicha festival of Jagannatha appeared to us supremely beautiful — more beautiful than in all the years gone by.

**Woman.** How so?

**Man.** This year the Lord Sri Krishna Chaitanya Himself — bliss embodied, golden like a golden mountain, wearing the garb of a king of ascetics, descended in the form of a devotee — made this great festival supremely beautiful in all its fullness.[^09-1]

**Woman.** Alas, alas! Why was I not taken along? Why was it not my lot to see it?

**Man.** My dear! Next year you will be able to see Him.

**Woman.** Next year — if only it will be so.

**Man.** My dear! From now on He will abide right here.

**Woman.** And what is certain about that?

**Man.** I know how matters stand.

**Woman.** How do you know it?

**Man.** From the account that His people pass on to one another — those who know His wondrous deeds.

**Woman.** What account is that?

**Man.** Listen, my dear: the ways in which He shows mercy to people are three.

**Woman.** What are these three kinds?

**Man.** The first — by appearing in person; the second — by entering the heart of another; the third — by appearing at the mere thought of Him.

**Woman.** Tell it in detail.

**Man.** To those who are able to come to the land of Purushottama He appears in person. For every year from various countries thousands upon thousands of beings come swiftly flocking, who have never before seen Him or heard of Him — and all of them long to see Him even more than to see Jagannatha.

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then: among them the Gaudiyas are dear to Him, and among the Gaudiyas there are some especially dear — those who have seen Him hundreds of times, and even those who have not seen Him but possess good fortune.[^09-2] Such as these:

> **1.** Certain natives of Khanda, headed by Narahari and Raghunandana — though from "Khanda", yet of undivided (*akhaṇḍa*) good fortune:[^09-3] never having seen Him before, every autumn they attain Purushottama.[^09-4]

> **2.** And also the people of Kulina-grama, descendants of Gunaraja — Ramananda and the others, friends of the Lord;[^09-5] and these great scholars, Nyaya Acharya and the rest, full of growing love — every autumn they come here.

And the Nyaya Acharya named Bhagavan[^09-6] has stayed in Purushottama itself for life, wishing to see the Lord Chaitanya. Such is His mercy shown in person. To those who are unable to come He shows mercy by rising in the heart of another. And worthy to have Him rise in their hearts are Advaita, Nakula Brahmachari and those like them.

**Woman.** Tell me at least something.

**Man.** The story of how He entered into Advaita is too long — it would take much time. Listen, then, how He entered the heart of Nakula Brahmachari.

**Woman.** Tell it, I am all attention.

**Man.** In Ambu-grama[^09-7] lives a supreme Vaishnava, a brahmachari from birth, named Nakula. Once a certain state came upon him, as if he were possessed by a spirit; he was overflowing with tears of bliss and trembling, and by his mere appearance he produced in the depths of everyone's heart the conviction: "Sri Chaitanya has entered into him." So several days and nights passed.

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** After that —

> **3.** colouring all the quarters around with a golden radiance, having lost the outward signs of consciousness in the enjoyment of bliss, he became an object of worship for hundreds of thousands of loving people — from children to old men and youths.

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then by the will of fate there came there at that time Shivananda, an associate of the Lord Chaitanya. Hearing this news, he doubted it strongly and, though he wished to see him,[^09-8] thought: "Ah, what is it to me to see him? For I have seen the Lord Himself face to face. What happiness at the sight of this man could equal the happiness of beholding Him? None." Having turned back, he thought again: "Ah, if he himself calls me, standing outside the whole crowd, brings me near to him and declares my chosen mantra — then truly the Lord abides in him." With this thought Shivananda stood outside the spreading crowd. And the other, though he had kept silent all the time the descent lasted, said only: "Ho, who is here? Call Shivananda, who is standing far off!" — and at this single command several people rushed off running, searched for him here and there, calling his name, and, finding him standing very far away, brought him to him. Then he declared: "Shivananda! Listen to what you have conceived in your mind: your chosen mantra has four syllables, its deity is Gaura-Gopala." Understanding this, Shivananda concluded: "This report is true."[^09-9]

**Woman.** Noble husband! And what is the third?

**Man.** Hear about the third too, the appearance at a mere thought. Once that same Shivananda's nephew, Shrikanta, came alone to Sri Purushottama for the first time and beheld the feet of the Lord Chaitanya. And at that very time the Lord, the only friend of the world, jestingly said something to him in the presence of the Puri master, Svarupa and the others: "Shrikanta! This year tell Advaita and the others, burning with compassion, not to come: I Myself shall go there." And further: "Tell Shivananda too, your maternal uncle: in the month of Pausha I shall be there with him. Jagadananda is there — I shall accept alms from him." When the returning Shrikanta delivered the Lord's message, Advaita and all the others gave up their preparations for the journey. But Shivananda, longing for the Lord's coming and reasoning, "This will be useful for the Lord's meal", began to store up what the Lord loves: vastuka leaves, the heart of the plantain stem, ears of grain and the rest.[^09-10]

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then by the will of fate Raya Ramananda came from the Godavari, and, held back by his entreaties, the Lord could not set out.

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then Shivananda in due time said to the renowned Sri Nrisimhananda Brahmachari — the highest of the lords of yoga, like Nrisimha Himself — who was formerly known as Pradyumna Brahmachari but had received the name Nrisimhananda from the Lord Himself, since he had attained perfection in the worship of Nrisimha: "Teacher! The Lord said, 'I shall come', and He has not come. I look at the vastuka greens — and there is only grief in my heart."

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then he said: "I myself shall bring Him — wait two days." Knowing his power, Shivananda believed him. And Nrisimhananda — the joy of Nrisimha and one who rejoices in Nrisimha[^09-11] — from that very time sank into samadhi, and within two days called Shivananda: "Ho! The Lord Chaitanya has been brought to the house of Raghava. In the morning He will come here. I myself shall cook, and I shall give the alms." Hearing this, Shivananda too set to work, while he himself bathed at dawn, became especially pure and began cooking. He cooked as he himself wished, whatever he pleased. Then at that very time, full of rasa, he divided and prepared three separate offerings: for Sri Chaitanya, for Jagannatha and for Nrisimha. Then, having offered them — each under its own name — he went outside and, closing his eyes, saw with his inner eye that all three offerings were being eaten by one and the same Lord. Then, filled with great bliss, with streams of tears flowing, he cried out loudly, weeping, in loving anger and with gracious reproach: "Good, good! You are one with Jagannatha — so let You eat Jagannatha's offering. But how can You eat the offering of my Nrisimha? Today through my fault Nrisimha has been left hungry!" When he wept so loudly, Shivananda asked: "Teacher! Why are you crying out so?"

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then he said: "Your Gosvami Chaitanya has eaten all three offerings. Nrisimha has had to fast."

**Woman.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then Shivananda said: "Teacher! Another offering must be prepared for Nrisimha." When this was done, he calmed down. There the matter rested, but in Shivananda a doubt arose: "Did he say this only in a state of possession, or is it true?" With this thought he came to Purushottama the next year and went to the Lord Chaitanya. And when, in the course of conversation, the Lord began to speak of the greatness of Nrisimhananda and incidentally said that he cooks very well — and all were surprised — "Last year, in the month of Pausha, I accepted alms from him; there I learned his skill in cooking" — all remained in doubt, but Shivananda was completely convinced. So I have explained the three kinds of mercy to you.

**Woman.** A wonder, a wonder! Then tell me about this too: why did Ramananda prevent Him from going there?

**Man.** My dear! He loves the Lord so much that he cannot bear separation from Him. Therefore, yielding to his entreaties, the Lord, though He wished to go to Mathura, delayed for two years, saying "today", "tomorrow".

**Woman.** So from now on He will abide here. Or else He will go to Mathura.

**Man.** My dear! Now, after long persuading that same Ramananda and obtaining his consent, He has set out to go — by way of Gauda.

**Woman.** Noble husband! And will He come back here again?

**Man.** Certainly.

**Woman.** That is just the doubt: for Mathura is His beloved place.

**Man.** Be that as it may, still —

> **4.** to lighten the heavy burden taken up by the Moon of Nilachala, who wishes to deliver all beings, down to the last simpleton, this Purushottama will again abide in Purushottama.[^09-12]

**Woman.** Yes, so it will be, so it will be.

**Behind the scenes.** Bhattacharya! How could Ramananda give his consent to this matter?

**Man.** My dear! Do you hear — just what I was saying: here is the Gajapati too, grieved by separation from Him, conversing with Sarvabhauma. Let us too go and serve the Lord, the Moon of Nilachala, with song. *(Exeunt both.)*

*— End of the introductory scene (praveśaka) —*

*(Enter the King, seated on the throne, and Sarvabhauma.)*

**King.** Bhattacharya, the Lord has set out only because the knot of the noose of Ramananda's insistent entreaties has loosened.

**Sarvabhauma.** What persistence can one show against the Lord? And still He delayed a whole two years.

**King.** Bhattacharya, Ramananda has done me a great kindness. For —

> **5.** he brought the All-merciful One onto the road from the capital and arranged that I should see Him;
> he granted me the touch of the lotuses of His feet — inaccessible, yet bringing happiness in full;
> he gave me to drink the nectar of His speech — merciful, sweetest, drunk by the ear.
> What could not be achieved by great efforts came about suddenly — and still there is emptiness in my heart.

**Sarvabhauma.** Maharaja! Ramananda is truly the best of Bhagavatas. For it is said: "He who holds the lotuses of His feet with the rope of love is called the foremost of Bhagavatas" [*Bhāg.* 11.2.55]. Therefore the Lord is in his power. That is why, yielding to his request, the Lord showed you such mercy.

**King.** How far is Ramananda to accompany Him?

**Sarvabhauma.** I have heard — as far as Bhadraka.

**King.** How many people have gone with the Master?

**Sarvabhauma.** Only five or six: the Puri master, Damodara, Jagadananda, Gopinatha, Govinda and others.

**King.** Alas —

> **6.** although the Lord of the world, the Lord of the Blue Mountain, shines, seated on the throne, in all His manifest supreme majesty,
> yet when the Lord Sri Krishna Chaitanya-deva goes off again to the north — alas, the three worlds are empty!

**Sarvabhauma.** King! Such is the way[^09-13] of love free of self-interest.

**King.** Did none of our people follow the Master?

**Sarvabhauma.** King, that is said only out of love. What need has He of your people! And still, as far as the borders of your dominions, some have already gone ahead with your letter — they will arrange everything properly.

> **7.** At every halting place they set up a new lodging beforehand, fill every house with abundant food,
> and the people appointed for it everywhere arrange everything carefully[^09-14] — and, entering there, the travellers shake off the fatigue of the journey on foot.

And the Lord thinks only that this is Ramananda's doing.

*(Enter the doorkeeper.)*

**Doorkeeper.** Sire! Ramananda and others are standing at the gate.

**King.** Bring them here quickly.

**Doorkeeper.** As you command. *(Goes out and returns with them.)*

**King** *(respectfully seating him)*. Tell me, how far did you accompany the Lord?

**Ramananda.** Although at every step I was told, "From here, from here go back!", I accompanied Him as far as Bhadraka. Maharaja, it is hard to leave the path of worldly duties, for —

> **8.** even Him, the ocean of compassion, the saviour of the most wretched — O Shiva, Shiva! — I left and came back only for fear of you.
> Ah, why did my body not fall there? Alas, I, whose frame is hard as a diamond thunderbolt — I have come back!

*(Sheds tears.)*[^09-15]

**Sarvabhauma.** Ramananda! You are so firm of spirit — why do you torment yourself so? Such are the Lord's pastimes: He left the people of Vraja and went to Mathura, from there to Dvaraka, and from there somewhere else. How did the people there bear separation from Him? Although separation from Him is unbearable, He Himself gives the strength to bear it. So enough of lamenting. You should now console the King, not sadden him by showing your sadness.

**King.** Tell it.

**Ramananda.** As far as the borders of your dominions your people are going. Beyond that, along the road, my knowledgeable people: some will go all the way to the kingdom of Gauda, some of them will return from no great distance, and some will go still further.

**Doorkeeper** *(entering)*. Sire! Of the Raya's people who went to escort Mahaprabhu, some have returned.

**King.** Let them in without delay.

*(The doorkeeper does so.)*

**Man** *(entering)*. Glory to the King!

**Ramananda.** Tell it, ho! How far has the Lord gone?

**Man.** As far as Kuliya-grama.

**King** *(looks into Sarvabhauma's face)*.

**Sarvabhauma.** Sire! Opposite Navadvipa, on the other bank of the Ganga, there is a village of that name.

**King.** Tell it from the beginning.

**Man.** Sire, from here to the borders of the King's dominions everyone went on foot, and the comfort of the journey was ensured by your power alone. To enter the territory of Gauda there are three ways: two are blocked, and the third is a water fortress. When they moved towards it, the governor of the frontier there, a Turk — like an ulcer-abscess in the heart,[^09-16] tormenting everyone to the very quick, a great drunkard, the crown of villains — was heard to say: "It will go badly for those who come from that country." Fear arose in everyone. No one told Mahaprabhu of it. Our frontier chief said: "Stay here a little, until I conclude an agreement with him." And at the very time this was said, a certain man came from that same governor to our frontier chief.[^09-17]

**King.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then ours said: "If he comes with three or four people, let him come." Hearing this, he came so, fell to the ground near the Lord's feet and bowed for a long time. Then all said: "Lord! Only with his help can one go safely. Cast a merciful glance on him." Yielding to their request, the Lord looked at him — and this Yavana, with his hair standing on end, in tears, his voice breaking, became as if possessed. Then Gopinatha Acharya said: "Ho! How could Mahaprabhu go safely?"

**King.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then he asked: "How far must you go?" Gopinatha answered: "For now — as far as Panihati."

**King.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then —

> **9.** with hair standing on end, with streams of tears flowing, he said in a choked voice:
> "Oh, how great is my good fortune — that I can serve the Lord with help!"

Then a new boat with a cabin in the middle, manned by boatmen who were all good people,[^09-18] was washed again and in the twinkling of an eye brought to the river. Then he himself got into another boat and said: "Let the Lord board this one" — and all boarded it together with the Lord. Then he himself, to ward off danger from river pirates, went ahead and, passing Mantreshvara, reached the village of Picchhanada. When he was turning back, the Lord bestowed on him mahaprasada of Jagannatha — sweet balls called "manohara". Receiving them, he cried "Say 'Hari'!", went into ecstasy and attained the state of a great Bhagavata.

*(The King acts amazement.)*

**Sarvabhauma.** Such is the Lord's play. For —

> **10.** this independent Lord shows mercy even where it is out of place, and where it is in place, He produces, indeed, only indifference.
> The divine Rama made Guha[^09-19] His bosom friend, while Krishna, when the Creator praised Him with hymns and bowed to Him — alas, remained silent.[^09-20]

**King.** And then?

**Man.** By his command the boatmen, singing of the Lord, drove the boat so fast that in a single day we landed at the village of Panihati.

**King.** Who lives there?

**Sarvabhauma.** Raghava Pandita.

**Man.** What happened there is a wonder.

**King.** How so?

**Man.** Sire!

> **11.** The Lord had not yet reached the edge of the bank of the River of the gods when it all — alas, what is there to say! — became a crowd of people.
> Had the dust of the earth, ah, become people there? Or had the stars in human bodies fallen from the sky to the earth?

So the Lord with difficulty made His way from the bank of the Ganga to his house.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Spending that night there, the next day He moved on by the same waterway.

> **12.** And so the line of people became long like the Ganga: unbroken is its auspicious stream, there are no barriers to it, its waves are moving arms,
> ceaselessly it descends to the foot of Vishnu.[^09-21]

Then He came to Kumarahatta, to the house of Shrivasa Pandita. And there, when He was walking from the bank of the Ganga to the house —

> **13.** wherever the Lord stepped, there, to gather the dust of His feet, people's hands went down —
> and the road, alas, was all covered with pits.[^09-22]

As soon as the Lord landed there, Jagadananda, unnoticed by the Lord, went off to Shivananda's house. Since he had lived there a long time, out of attachment to that house he reasoned, "The Lord must be brought here", and arranged everything with special care.

And so —

> **14.** when people had filled the tops of the walls, all the branches of the trees, the ground, every street and every road,
> loudly crying, "Louder, louder say 'Hari'!" — the Lord at the end of the night, led by Shivananda, set out by boat.

Then Jagadananda, on both sides of the road all the way to Shivananda's house, adorned the way with special skill with plantain trunks, full pitchers, buds and rows of lamps. Setting foot on it, the Lord, with a smile on His sweetest lips, thought: "Jagadananda has arranged this." But seeing that on the left the road to Vasudeva's house was decorated in the same way, He hesitated: "Should I go on from here, or there?" And Vasudeva said: "Lord! First grace the house of Shivananda with Your presence." So He did: Jagadananda washed the lotuses of His feet, and He entered his house-temple. Then Jagadananda sprinkled a little of the water from His feet on the roof of the house, and part he himself distributed to the household in the inner apartments. Staying there a *muhūrta*, the Lord came to the house of Vasudeva, stayed there a moment and again boarded the boat. When the Lord set out, the people, to obtain the water of His feet, went into the water up to their necks, and, seeing their agitation, the Lord was filled with such compassion that all obtained the water of His feet. Then all the people moved along the bank, and no one turned back.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Man.** Then He arrived at Advaita's house, where Haridasa bowed to Him, and by the same way, by boat, He landed on the bank opposite Navadvipa, in the village of Kuliya, at the house of Madhava Dasa. Out of mercy to the people of Navadvipa He stayed there seven days. And in crossing from Navadvipa to the other bank —

> **15.** the ferry fee, which before had been only one *kākiṇī* from each person, gradually,
> even on the largest boats, became more and more — already in *kārṣāpaṇas*.[^09-23]

And further —

> **16.** the fence of the strongest thick bamboos that Madhava erected at night, by morning the crush of the throngs of people gradually turned to dust.
> So all seven days passed: such was the press of those longing to see Him that the Lord, though He is the Lord, could not Himself go to bathe in the River of the immortals.

Staying there seven days so, He again moved on by the road along the bank.

> **17.** Wherever the Lord was heading, there people, hearing of it beforehand, flocked together;
> and so the very earth here and there sagged greatly — astonishing especially the King of serpents.[^09-24]

And it is also heard: when the Lord was walking along the bank of the Ganga opposite the capital of the lord of Gauda, with throngs of people moving after Him on both sides, the lord of Gauda, seated in a pavilion pitched on the bank of the Ganga, saw it and asked in amazement: "What is this?" Then his minister named Keshava Vasu said: "Shuratrana,[^09-25] it is a certain great man named Sri Krishna Chaitanya going from Purushottama to Mathura. These people are moving, wishing to see Him." Then he said: "He who draws people so is God." And this too is heard: going a little further from there, He turned back and will go to Mathura not by that way but, coming to Purushottama, by the forest road. We do not know whether the report speaks the truth or not.

**Behind the scenes.** True, sirs, true!

> **18.** He suddenly came alone, saw the ornament of the Blue Mountain,
> and, for fear of the throngs of people, secretly went to Mathura by the forest road.

**King** *(with amazement)*. It sounds like the voice of Kashi Mishra. Call him.

**Kashi Mishra** *(entering hurriedly)*. Sire, here I am — come even uncalled.

**King.** Tell me how matters stand.

**Kashi Mishra.** It is true: for fear of the people He turned back from there. And from here He left unnoticed by anyone.

**King.** Sarvabhauma! For us it is one and the same road — for its fruit is one: the grief of separation. But how will He manage alone?

**Kashi Mishra.** Several brahmins worthy of giving Him alms have been sent. The Lord, however, does not know it.

**King.** Good, Mishra, good! Did He say anything as He was leaving?

**Kashi Mishra.** "Consider that I have already returned."

**King.** When will that day come? Bhattacharya! Let some runners[^09-26] be dispatched to bring news of the Lord.

**Sarvabhauma.** That is truly fitting.

**King.** Kashi Mishra! Convey my order to the mahapatra Chandaneshvara, that he do so.

**Kashi Mishra.** Sire! I have already informed Pariksha Mahapatra and have given orders for it myself.[^09-27] Some have almost returned already.

**Doorkeeper** *(entering)*. Sire! At the gate are messengers with news of Mahaprabhu.

**King.** Let them in.

*(The doorkeeper does so.)*

**Messengers** *(entering)*. Glory, glory to the King!

**King.** Tell what you know.

**Messengers.** We know everything.

**King.** Tell it.

**Messengers.**

> **19.** The Lord has returned from Madhupura, having beheld the beauty of Vrindavana, sporting in every grove along the banks of the Daughter of the Sun;
> having visited the best of mountains, Govardhana, and wandering, wandering from forest to forest for several days — He has been seen on the road.

**King.** Ho! And do you know what the Lord did in Vrindavana?

**Messengers.** Of course. We heard it from the lips of certain fortunate ones who came back together with the Lord.[^09-28]

**King.** Tell it.

**Messengers.**

> **20.** He, the single root of the whole multitude of His own blisses, from His very appearance, it is true, revealed all their manifestations ever more strongly;
> but when He came to Vrindavana — ah, these waves grew so high that neither word nor thought can reach beyond them.

For —

> **21.** now, gazing at the beauty of the forest borders by the Yamuna, the Lord of all, stretching out His longing arms,[^09-29]
> embraces every creeper, every tree, and weeps aloud with love, not restraining His voice.

And further —

> **22.** mad with joy at the sight of herds of beautiful cows, He, with streaming tears, fell —
> like a slope of Meru broken by the wind, down which the River of the gods flows.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** Then they told:

> **23.** Suddenly catching sight somewhere of a lustre like the neck of a peacock intoxicated with joy,[^09-30]
> the Lord stumbles, rolls on the ground, trembles, cries out, melts, falls into despondency — ah! — and loses consciousness.

And further —

> **24.** Somewhere in the forest, seeing a running herd of calves with tails raised,
> the Lord at once rushes after them along a path strewn with thorns — and falls, His whole body wounded.[^09-31]

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** Then —

> **25.** when at times, losing consciousness by chance, He fell to the ground within the bounds of a grove,
> the deer licked the foam from His lips, and the birds drank the water of His eyes.

At times —

> **26.** the Lord fell even at the foot of Mount Govardhana, but, immersed in the very depths of the nectar ocean of love,
> though He was bruised, He knew no outward pain.[^09-32]

> **27.** When the Lord, gazing at every forest and every grove, wept aloud with love, not restraining His voice,
> the creepers and trees too seemed to weep, and the flocks of birds and beasts lost consciousness.

And further —

> **28.** When the Lord sobbed piteously — though His voice was firm and deep like the thunder of a cloud —
> somewhere the blue-throated peacocks, with tears in their throats, abandoning their dance, sobbed long after Him.[^09-33]

Seeing that from the excess of love He was so beside Himself and His body held together only by the power of His divinity, the pious Balabhadra Bhattacharya and others thought, "Ah, a calamity has happened!" — and as if by force led the Lord away from Vrindavana. That is why He stayed there only a short time. And then —

> **29.** him who even before, though tightly bound by the multitude of virtues dear to him, was free of attachment to home — like the supreme rasa, embodied and at the same time bodiless —
> that Sri Rupa together with Anupama the Lord favoured in Prayaga with conversations full of love and the rapture of the firmest embraces.[^09-34]

**King.** Sarvabhauma! Why did He, who so loves Vrindavana, stay there so short a time?

**Sarvabhauma.** It is as if Jagannatha, unable to bear separation from Him, drew Him back. And further —

> **30.** into Rupa — dear by his own nature, beloved by his own nature, love by his very nature, beautiful from birth,
> like unto Himself — the Lord, one in His form, placed the form of His own pastimes.[^09-35]

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** And then in Varanasi —

> **31.** in the house of a god of the earth[^09-36] known by the name of Chandrashekhara, the Lord of the world, the king of ascetics,
> then stayed — by the former accumulations of good deeds of the master of the house.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** And then —

> **32.** Vishveshvara as it were commanded the whole world: "Go and see Him!" — commanded with love.
> Otherwise how would all people at one and the same time have done one and the same thing?

And further —

> **33.** to Him came brahmacharis, householders, mendicants and hermits, priests and keepers of vows;
> only a few envious heads of sannyasis did not go there — and did not see Him.

**King.** Sarvabhauma! Why is there such envy in such people?

**Sarvabhauma.** Maharaja! As long as the mind is not conquered, so long the venerable Lady Envy[^09-37] leaves no one.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** And then —

> **34.** he who was the jewelled ornament of the assembly of the lord of Gauda — Rupa's elder brother — leaving his flourishing wealth, took as his wife the young Lakshmi of renunciation.
> His heart is full within of the rasa of bhakti, while outwardly he has the form of an avadhuta; like a great lake covered with duckweed, he gives joy to those who know his worth.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.**

> **35.** As soon as He saw with His own eyes Sanatana, who had come, He, golden like a champaka, boundlessly softened by compassion,
> compassionately embraced him with His arms, long as bolts.

**King.** How did their meeting come about?

**Messengers.** We heard it from his own lips.

**King.** Tell it.

**Messengers** *(conveying Sanatana's words)*.

> **36.** "Those who, led by eagerness alone, go first to the Lord come out from Him absorbed in the name of the Lord, in tears, with hair standing on end.
> But I stood outside, counting the series of those coming and going and taking up the dust of their feet — and the all-knowing Lord commanded someone to bring me."

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** And then, saying, "Not dear to Me is one who knows the four Vedas…" [*Itihāsa-samuccaya*][^09-38] and so on, He hurriedly came down and clasped him in His arms.

**King.** And then, and then?

**Messengers.** And then, there too, we heard a certain report.

**King.** What was it?

**Messengers.**

> **37.** Coming to Purushottama and spending some days there, gladdened by the Lord Himself,
> they will then, at His word, go to Vrindavana.

**King.** Together with the Lord, or afterwards?

**Messengers.** Afterwards, for it was seen that the Lord left Varanasi alone. And we heard something else there.

**Sarvabhauma.** What was it?

**Messengers.**

> **38.** Since in the course of time the accounts of the pastimes in Vrindavana had been lost, in order to proclaim them especially,
> the Lord there sprinkled with the nectar of mercy both Rupa and Sanatana.

**Ramananda.** That is quite fitting.

*(Behind the scenes, from afar, a loud noise: "Glory, glory!")*

**All** *(having listened)*. Oh! It seems the Lord Sri Krishna Chaitanya has come — for an unprecedented noise is heard.

**Again behind the scenes.**

> **39.** Today our birth has borne fruit, our eyes have reached their goal; every fever has at once subsided, the heart has found peace.
> What more is there to say? Look — for us this is a new birth: the king of ascetics has returned[^09-39] from Vrindavana to the Blue Mountain!

**King.** Why do you still delay? Let us go there. *(Exeunt all.)*

*(Enter Sri Krishna Chaitanya, and Paramananda Puri and Svarupa, who have met Him at this time; around them Kashi Mishra and others who have gathered to see Him.)*

**Sri Krishna Chaitanya** *(to the Puri master)*. Teacher!

> **40.** Although these two tirthas — the great souls and Kashi and the other holy places — are equal and equally wash away sins,
> yet bliss is bestowed, indeed, only by the great-souled — for to us happiness is the joy of seeing you.[^09-40]

Therefore association with such as you is more beautiful even than serving other tirthas — that is why I have come back so soon.

**Puri master.** Such is our good fortune, that in so long a time we were not burnt[^09-41] in the forest fire of separation from the Lord.

*(Hurriedly approaching, Sarvabhauma and Ramananda fall prostrate like rods; Sri Krishna Chaitanya embraces them.)*
